# Design

## Current-count integration disposition (2026-11-03 HH)

HH admits the integration described below through authored full/partial-cap andFP controls. Count identity uses the same active stopped event/lifetime/generation andnested sidecar, with both wrapper words joined to each independent current model-wrapper read. The remaining bounded action is a fresh-root native observation after ownership clears, preserving current-count versus earlier-getter history andstatic type-candidate versus entity identity. HF's earlier cutoff stays frozen; HH has a separate seal.

## Next bounded research integration (2026-11-03 HE)

HE admits only the standalone symmetric current-count reader. Integrate its separate journal within the existing nested interval; derive all identity fields from the same active exception/lifetime/generation, compare its wrapper8 with the independently read current model-wrapper prefix, andexercise full/partial caps plus unchanged caller FP. Preserve raw signed count andnominal-domain diagnostics; a coherent out-of-range count is not retroactively a corrupted capture. A later native root needs fresh ownership admission. Match any subsequently observed vptr to HDstatic candidates only after module/generation normalization, preserving type-candidate versus entity-handle/serial/actor identity. No virtual-function invocation orclaim of earlier getter consumption is authorized by those reads alone.

## Research refinement: raw pose span and current count (2026-11-03 HB/HC)

Treat the planned96-byte pose span as raw stack words. Exact retail copy100a46d0 writes only a dynamically counted prefix, uses x87 FLD/FSTP, supplies no24 clamp anddoes not clear the tail. A nonnull virtual-model pointer selects the count at virtual+50h rather than the local-header count. Separately fixture-admit symmetric wrapper8/current-count4 reads with full pointer/branch/BOOL/error/identity controls before interpreting a current prefix. Preserve unused/raw words andunusual IEEE domains. Even a current count at the later initializer RET does not establish the count orvalues consumed by an earlier getter; that history needs its own witness. Keep isolated safe oversized-count/precision experiments separate from native state.

## Research plan: nested caller frame and current context (2026-11-03 GX)

Within the admitted stack36/wrapper28 interval, separately fixture-admit symmetric outer-frame28, dereferenced context16 and poseparameter96 reads. Use the saved outer EBP value B, preserve its distinction from the slot address, and check the exact caller relation B=innerESP+1104h. Frame position/quaternion/mask must match existing arguments; context words must bind model-wrapper/mask/poseparameters at B-74h/current zero callback. Add bounded raw EDI prefix12, sequence/unknown/cycle span12 and IK-pointer4 witnesses, plus a separate33-byte loaded caller setup/CALL window. Retain raw time/cycle/poseparameter words without normalization, domain filtering or assumed equality to an earlier local cycle. Require independent address/dereference/API/identity/mutation controls and multithreaded cap/FP/lifecycle admission before native use. Object/type candidates do not establish entity handle/serial generation, actor/scene name or cache/rendered-pose ownership. Preserve all prior roots and failures.

## Research plan: symmetric outer-frame and wrapper-code witnesses (2026-11-03 GR)

Keep the frozen initializer capture formats and stopped FX/GB approaches unchanged. In a fresh private fixture, add a separate bounded journal for two 36-byte caller-stack reads enclosing two 28-byte loaded-wrapper reads at the fixed direct RET. Retain API success independently of requested/actual counts and error, plus capture/stop/PID/thread lifetime/module generation/base/EIP/ESP identity. Require both stack images to agree with the legacy prefix, EBP to equal ESP+20, the outer output arguments to match the inner arguments, and both wrapper images to match the exact admitted bytes. Preserve the saved EBP value and unknown outer returns without inventing caller or actor identity. First admit range, API-failure, mutation, identity and transcript controls in an authored reader; then validate actual multithreaded full/partial-cap wrapper calls and unchanged FP/lifecycle behavior. A native attempt requires those admissions and a fresh ownership guard. This research changes no Rust behavior and proves no final pose or campaign parity.

## Next bounded research step: outer caller sidecar (2026-11-03 GN)

Preserve the admitted 20-byte stack format. Independently validate a symmetric, bounded 36-byte sidecar at the same fixed RET, retaining read errors and PID/thread lifetime/generation/ESP identity and requiring its first 20 bytes to match the legacy sample. The exact wrapper puts its saved outer return at ESP+24. ESP+20 is the address of its saved EBP slot, and the observed wrapper EBP equals that address in all 32 contexts; the slot's saved value was not read. Bind an observed outer return to exact module/hash/call bytes before retrieving the caller on demand. Existing actor/tool labels are selected static candidates, not exhaustive coverage or live actor identity. This sidecar is planned, not implemented or tested.

## Research disposition: default initialization versus final pose (2026-11-03 GJ–GL)

The fixed-RET capture now observes selected native output/default correspondence with fixture-admitted per-thread cap handling. Preserve the boundary: unselected slots have unknown preimages; initialized defaults do not identify actor/frame ownership, cache publication, virtual-model merge or later gesture/layer/root/flex composition. Use the raw selected words as a narrow oracle, including slightly nonunit defaults, without inferring a later normalization policy. The next bounded design should bind one actor/model generation and a later cache/blend boundary with independently admitted observation; it must not reuse stopped FX dynamic pairing or GB global cap disarm. The original16-record first-map ledger,including its four missing exact procedures,remains unchanged.

## Research plan: per-thread cap drain (2026-11-03 GC)

GB's raw failed stops show actual debug registers still active despite disarmed bookkeeping after its global cap write; do not infer undocumented OS delivery details. Keep GB and FX stopped. In a fresh fixed-site producer, freeze cap membership by thread lifetime/generation, self-disarm the currently stopped thread, and leave other members unchanged until their own exact-site stop or explicit exit/retirement. Drain stops acquire no output samples and each member can drain only once. Record every stopped/requested/actual context; never arm new threads after cap. Separately journal module-unload retirement and verify zero debug registers instead of claiming it is a self-disarm. Require terminal membership accounting, bounded stops/deadline, multi-thread cap1/cap12/full36 checks, read/identity/FP/output and corruption controls before native use.

## Research plan: one fixed completed-state boundary (2026-11-03 GA/GB)

FX's dynamic paired observer stays stopped. Statically admit the direct RET at clientRVA0x97d60: its epilogue restores entry ESP before RET, so the original return address/four arguments remain readable there. Validate a different one-site recorder on the authored exact initializer, preserving full stopped contexts even for rejected or post-cap queued events, code400 bytes behind the site and symmetric source/output reads. There is no dynamic return breakpoint or pending-call ledger. Independently compare only selected output poststate with current source words; native unselected preimages, exact stores and continuity with previous entry captures remain unknown. Fixture-test multi-thread/full and partial caps, bounds/read failures and transcript corruptions before a fresh guarded native run. Keep all prior failures frozen; no reuse of the stopped FX producer.

## Research plan: conditional loaded-source/observed-FP replay (2026-11-03 FZ)

After stopping FX's hardware-pair mechanism at two rejected controls, use a different evidence path: supply FV's retained loaded direct records, wrapper flags, masks and actual32 entry FP images to the isolated401-byte initializer buffer. Rebase only authored model pointers and preserve all original source/context provenance. Independently compare selected finite position/quaternion words, untouched slots, canaries, stack/RO/code integrity and raw pre/post FP state; seed and immediately verify the observed sticky state rather than inferring it from matching control words. Include explicit zero-mask controls and an independently captured final original-state restore image. This is a conditional replay of observed inputs in an authored process; it does not observe the original initializer returning or establish its actual output, actor/frame identity or rendered pose. No additional native paired-observer retry.

## Research plan: closed initializer entry/return observation (2026-11-03 FX)

Place only the exact call-free401-byte initializer in an authored DLL and call it on finite70-bone sources with explicit masks and guarded output arrays. Extend the owned hardware debugger to bind one pending call per thread to PID/module generation/entry ESP/return address/four arguments, arm a per-thread return breakpoint, and preserve requested/actual contexts at both stops. Require exit ESP=entry ESP+4, unchanged sampled source/argument identities and independently checked selected-source versus untouched-preimage output words. Stop arming new entries at the global cap while draining already pending returns; reject orphan returns, thread/module teardown with pending pairs, overlap, incomplete reads and inconsistent footers. Exercise actual multiple threads and cleanup before any guarded native output run. Endpoint equality does not exclude all intervening mutation or establish rendered pose, actor/frame identity or timing fidelity. Keep the rejected FT deadline run frozen.

## Research plan: loaded direct defaults and paired initialization witnesses (2026-11-03 FR)

Extend the separately admitted FL external reader in a fresh private root to retain bounded direct bone records, source words and the initializer's output-array arguments. Check signed offsets/counts and widened address arithmetic before every read; preserve requested/actual byte counts, errors, generation/thread identity and symmetric wrapper/header/flags/record rechecks. First exercise authored 70-bone objects, invalid and partial reads, invalid extents, alternate-header rejection, mutation controls, unload/reload and capture-cap cleanup. An entry snapshot establishes source/argument data only. Any later output claim requires a separately admitted same-thread, same-generation entry/return pair and stable source/output identities, plus independent selected-store/untouched-slot comparisons. Only after fixture admission may a fresh ownership guard permit an owned native capture. Keep loaded data, owned-candidate correspondence, actor/frame association, hierarchy and rendered pose as separate evidence boundaries; no Rust implementation or parity claim.

## Research plan: candidate parent topology and mask closure (2026-11-02 FQ)

Audit only FN's hash-bound owned 70-bone record range: signed parent indices at record+4, single-root/acyclic reachability and whether the two observed flag masks select a subset closed under candidate parent ancestry. Preserve record indices, every ancestor chain and any omitted parent without inventing loaded bone names or hierarchy. Test invalid parent/root/cycle/flag cases independently. This prepares later hierarchy witnesses; it does not observe a loaded skeleton or compose poses. No native launch or Rust action.

## Research plan: loaded-header and owned-member correspondence (2026-11-02 FN)

Re-read only the owned models/gman_high.mdl candidate through the pinned stock reader, preserving member/archive/CRC/hash provenance and selected raw ranges. Independently compare its408-byte header and ordered70-bone flag words with FM's rechecked loaded bytes, retaining every difference and separating same-name/checksum/partial-byte correspondence from proof of loading or conversion. Do not infer loaded bone names/order, source default words, actor/frame identity or pose outputs from matching header fields. No native launch or Rust action.

## Research plan: bounded loaded model-header observation (2026-11-02 FL/FM)

Extend only the owned debugger with a separate ordered read/error journal for the initializer's first argument: 48 wrapper bytes, 408 header bytes, bounded one-to-256 bone flags and symmetric rechecks. Preserve every requested/actual read size/error, snapshot generation, pointers and rejection reason; never dereference target pointers in the observer itself. First test valid authored StudioHdr-like objects and deliberately invalid header pointers on a disposable unload/reload fixture, retaining full caller/FP/entry/cleanup checks. Only after admission may a fresh guarded native run bind raw loaded header/name/checksum/bone-count/flags to entry masks/FP state. Header strings/checksums remain loaded-object evidence, not a proven asset conversion, actor association, frame identity or completed pose. No Rust action.

## Research plan: owned child-record delta composition (2026-11-02 FH)

Join DL's admitted selected DELTA decoder outputs to CR/EJ's exact sequence/animation/weight identities and FE's corresponding owned default bone words by an explicitly authored name correspondence. Feed each selected record state into a one-bone conditional full-blend fixture using actual descriptor flags, explicit masks/weights and both base models; preserve absent full-model/virtual/cache/frame association. Admit derived scale/index ranges before execution, extend only authored loader count/flag gates, and compare independently recomputed/prepared/native server/client words plus code/RO/context/FP/stack/guard/cleanup. Deduplicate the DELTA base-independent source pairs rather than count them twice. This is per-record local composition, not an actor gesture, assembled full skeleton or campaign/Rust parity.

## Research plan: conditional blend with owned defaults (2026-11-02 FG)

Reuse only the sealed EI authored loader and admitted server/client fast bundles in fresh output roots. Build finite nondelta cases from FE's exact owned effective defaults and EJ's four selected weight tables, with explicit masks, incoming weights and both authored source directions. Reorder by diagnostic bone-name correspondence only as an authored input choice; do not call it a loaded virtual map or actual gesture pose. Independently admit all derived finite dot/index domains, prepare outputs through the canonical source-pinned reference and compare native modules/raw FP/code/RO/context/stack/guards/cleanup. Preserve any disagreement rather than assume prior synthetic cases cover these slightly nonunit inputs. No game/DLL initialization or Rust action.

## Research plan: effective owned default-word domain (2026-11-02 FE)

Revalidate EJ's two exact owned members with its read-only verifier, then independently reconstruct only the initializer-consumed positions/quaternions from retained source ranges and direct/alternate offsets. Classify their raw float32 domains, retain exact rational quaternion norms, and compare embedded-record versus effective-array diagnostic name joins separately. Require range/word/route/name corruption controls. A diagnostic name join, finite default domain or matching consumed words does not establish loaded conversion, actual virtual bone mapping, runtime masks, actor pose or rendering parity. No native launch or Rust action.

## Research plan: initializer capacity and unmasked exceptions (2026-11-02 FD)

FC verifies zero/seven live x87 slots with masked exceptions. In a separate authored fixture, supply a full eight-slot stack and selectively unmask invalid/denormal exceptions for direct/alternate and selected/unselected paths. Retain OS exception records/contexts, partial output words, stack/control/status and independent final original-state restore snapshots; preserve faults as outcomes. Use integer-only capture of the supplied exception context and restore safe state before reporting. Compare explicit scenarios rather than infer arbitrary ABI/unwind safety. No game/user process or Rust action; actual caller stack/CW remains unobserved.

## Research plan: initializer exceptional-word copies (2026-11-02 FC)

While native launch is deferred by current ownership, execute only DU's exact call-free 401-byte initializer in a new authored x86 fixture. Supply minimal direct/alternate model scaffolds, explicit selected/unselected masks, finite/zero/subnormal/infinity/quiet/signaling-NaN float32 words, nearest/directed rounding and 24/53/64-bit masked x87 precision, plus empty/seven-live-slot x87 stacks. Compare raw output words, live stack/control/status/MXCSR, guards, stack balance and code/source pins against an independent bit-level copy/quieting model. Retain any disagreement; do not assume x87 FLD/FSTP has MOVSS bit-copy semantics. Unmasked exceptions, full-stack faults, arbitrary NaN payload coverage, actual owned defaults/actor outputs, native timing and Rust parity remain outside this finite contract.

## Research plan: module generations before native retry (2026-11-02 FA)

EZ rejects a repeated native target-module load before any entry. Do not infer which unload belongs to that target from incomplete logs. Extend the authored debugger to retain each target load path/file/base and every unload base, disarm on target unload, and bind entry/read records to explicit load generations. Test an authored load/unload/reload host before any second native run; preserve the first rejected attempt and require fresh ownership again. Active duplicate loads without an admitted unload remain rejected. The global capture cap must survive generations, and successful state observation still does not establish normal timing or completed native output.

## Research plan: bounded native initializer entry (2026-11-02 EY/EZ)

Generalize the admitted module observer only for the authored entry and exact client initializer: explicit RVA/code extent/command line, byte-identical expected module file before arming, bounded capture/disarm and retained OS state/read provenance. Run that adapter against the authored DLL first, including a mismatched expected-file rejection. Only after admission, repeat process/cursor/foreground guards immediately before an owned native launch using a fresh private write mount. Capture initializer entry CW/MXCSR, stack argument pointers/mask and exact loaded code; retain settings/source/cleanup and a visual context witness. Entry observations do not establish caller ABI in general, actor association, completed initialization/output pose, natural timing, campaign completion or Rust parity. Stop for unexpected ownership/state failures and continue independent static research if launch cannot be admitted.

## Research plan: module-load and capture-cap fixture (2026-11-02 EW)

Before a retail launch, move the admitted authored observer into an owned x86 DLL and load it through an authored host. Extend the debugger to bind the exact module path/file bytes/load base/code at LOAD_DLL, arm only after that admission, and disarm every live thread at an explicit global capture cap. Preserve entry/raw reads/actual resume contexts and verify the full host workload still completes after capture stops. Test finite invalid module/cap/source controls independently. This adds neither native actor association nor timing parity; any later native run needs a fresh process/cursor/foreground guard and private game-write mount.

## Research plan: paired authored observer perturbation (2026-11-02 EV)

Use the identical sealed EP workload/executable in fresh private run directories, alternating eight ordinary and eight ET-debugged executions in an ABBA schedule. Pin executable/debugger/verifier sources before execution, record whole-process wall intervals with exit/cleanup, independently recheck the raw caller/observer/FP/guard/footer results for every run, and retain all samples rather than selecting the fastest. Compare paired differences and distribution summaries only for this disposable workload. Startup, file output and debugger read/stop overhead are included; this is not a target-call latency measurement or native HL2/FPS evidence. Native entry capture still requires fresh ownership and exact module/source admission.

## Research plan: independent Windows entry-context capture (2026-11-02 ER)

After the bounded EP observer and separate EQ report audit, test a hardware execution breakpoint on the same authored x86 executable in a new private output root. Launch only that owned child under the Windows debug API; retain raw WOW64 contexts and source memory reads while debug events suspend execution, admit each thread's debug-register setup, and compare stopped entry GPR/ESP/EFLAGS and x87/SSE images against the child's independent caller/observer records. Retain resume-state writes, module/PE identity, failures, bounded completion and process/handle cleanup. A debugger stop changes scheduling; measure that perturbation separately and do not infer native timing. Do not initialize a Valve DLL or capture a game until this mechanism is independently admitted.

## Research plan: bounded entry-state observer fixture (2026-11-02 EP)

Resume EO with an authored x86 process before any native detour. Capture entry EFLAGS/GPR/ESP and aligned FXSAVE state before a helper runs; deliberately alter helper FP/SSE state, restore the saved image and registers, and compare independent caller-side snapshots. Exercise finite control words, x87 stack contents, eight XMM registers, direction/arithmetic flags, nested calls, disjoint thread contexts and bounded recorder overflow. Pin source/build/executable, retain raw images/records/footer/cleanup and deliberate corruption controls. Reserved image bytes are not architectural state; report the compared field ranges explicitly. CPU feature admission and successful user-mode execution are separate from unobservable privileged OS controls. This fixture does not establish arbitrary exception safety, concurrent patching, native initializer ABI, extended AVX state, game actor inputs or Rust parity. No Rust/game action is part of EP.

## Research plan: exact acos dispatch predicate (2026-10-29 EN)

EO now admits only the exact six-byte initializer entry boundary/continuation and rejects four invalid cuts/corruptions. Before a future native hook, build a disposable fixture that verifies entry GPR/ESP/EFLAGS and x87/SSE preservation before any callback, alignment, source identity, disjoint bounded recorder memory, reentrancy/thread isolation, overflow/drop/footer behavior and perturbation. Entry admission alone does not establish safe patching, observer transparency or actual actor data. Repeat current ownership before any owned launch.

EM observes fast output invariance across three supplied x87 precisions and scalar sensitivity under those same controls. Next independently bind the exact client/server dispatcher masks, comparisons and branch ordering to frozen PE instructions. Enumerate the declared16-bit control-word domains without executing reserved settings, distinguish tested numerical contexts from Boolean gate admission, and record which precision/rounding/DAZ/FTZ/sticky fields the gate actually inspects. Require paired-code/source checks and coherent instruction/control corruptions. This static gate audit is not arbitrary-control execution or an actual runtime flag observation; no game/Rust action.

## Research plan: caller x87 precision sensitivity (2026-10-29 EM)

EL retains fast/scalar raw80/64 differences despite all finite float32 results agreeing at caller x87 control0x027f. The exact scalar control helper preserves precision bits while temporarily using nearest rounding/masked exceptions; cleanup restores the caller word. Next reuse the sealed EL bundles/inputs in a new fixture at explicit nearest/masked24-,53- and64-bit precision words0x007f/0x027f/0x037f. Keep MXCSR0x1f80 and one supplied zero XMM context fixed; retain return words/native and post-store status, stack/RO/pins/cleanup. Independently compare each precision against retained53-bit records and stable mathematical references, preserving differences. This tests a supplied finite caller context, not the actual runtime CW, arbitrary rounding/error ABI, actor or Rust parity. No game/DLL initialization or implementation action.

## Research plan: acos return precision and caller context (2026-10-29 EL)

EK conditionally copies admitted owned defaults through the exact initializer; actual loader/actor state remains open. The full blend's float32 comparisons also leave intermediate acos precision unspecified. Next reuse only the closed selected acos dispatch/scalar/fast/helper graphs from EI in a new authored fixture, separating dispatch and scalar-mode storage, guarding opaque errors and admitting bounded finite input/index domains. Retain raw80-bit x87 returns plus rounded64/32 words, supplied XMM context, caller stack/FP state and complete relocated code/RO/source pins. Compare server/client, forced paths and caller contexts without assuming all representations agree; report any difference and keep mathematical-reference accuracy separate from native byte equality. No general exception ABI, actual runtime flags/actor/rendering/campaign/Rust claim, game or implementation action.

## Research plan: owned default initializer fixture (2026-10-29 EK)

EJ independently admits two owned model inputs with intact version44/direct and version48/relative-array routes,56 bones each and14 corruption controls. Keep their defaults and flags distinct. Next copy only the admitted source ranges into disjoint private read-only buffers at their original offsets, retain zero-filled fixture scaffolding explicitly, and execute DU's exact401-byte initializer plus28-byte wrapper in a new authored x86 process. Admit every conditional read range, the single wrapper CALL relocation, explicit masks, output guards, stack/FP state, source/code/context pins and cleanup before comparing copied/untouched words independently. Neither a sparse fixture buffer nor a supplied wrapper flag vector establishes real loader conversion, runtime masks, actor association, virtual mapping, hierarchy/rendering/campaign or Rust parity. No game/DLL initialization/Rust change.

## Research plan: owned default-pose input admission (2026-10-29 EJ)

EH/EI close and execute the finite fast blend contract while preserving rejected nested-verifier imports and separate offline audits. Next read only the selected owned root/include models through the pinned private reader; retain raw header/bone/default position/quaternion/flag words and selected child weights/flags with exact VPK/member/hash/range provenance. Bind DU's initializer offsets as a conditional consumer, independently compare bone inventories/default words, and preserve mismatches. Any authored name-based map must stay separate from actual loaded virtual-map/default-pose observation. No model-version conversion, actor/rendering/campaign/Rust parity, broad corpus pass, installed writes or implementation change follows automatically.

## Research plan: exact fast-acos frontier (2026-10-29 EH)

EF/EG now add finite table2/near-one scale evidence and a bounded exhaustive x-component family, with explicit derived-domain limits. Next retrieve the fast-acos numerical bodies only through the exact server/client wrapper CALLs; compare complete reachable graphs, consumed literal/table extents, relocations and error transfers. Keep mutable flags/context independent and opaque errors guarded. Only after static closure may a separate authored fixture enable the previously excluded fast-acos/full-blend path, retaining new inputs, raw mappings, FP state, pins, footer/cleanup and any disagreement. No native actor/rendering/campaign/Rust claim or implementation change follows automatically.

## Research plan: bounded near-one component family (2026-10-29 EG)

EF's extended scale inputs pass while explicitly leaving table2 indices27..32 uncovered. Next independently enumerate the finite float32 norm interval to bound possible table2 indices, then admit representatives for those six bins. Separately test all65,536 positive x-component words in `[.99609375,1)` with fixed remaining components, one weight, both forced paths/aliases, split into new owned fixture batches below the existing loader cap. Retain exact union/order/source/mapping/FP/cleanup evidence and any numerical failure. This is a bounded component family and derived read-domain audit, not all quaternion inputs, live branch-PC traces, actual actor rendering or Rust parity. No game or implementation action.

## Research plan: finite fast-scale domain extension (2026-10-29 EF)

ED closes the selected client scalar blend graphs and EE's separate client process agrees with all retained server numerical/FP/context records under authored inputs. Keep this isolated computational contract separate from actual actor/cache/rendering evidence and retain all rejected preparations.

Next extend DS's finite scale corpus into its unexercised table2/near-one interiors. Reuse the sealed canonical bundle and authored loader image in a new root; force explicit fast/scalar paths and aliases. Derive branch/index/range admission from exact instructions, sample table bins and float32 threshold neighbors, and retain prepared host-double expectations plus actual outputs/pins/FP/mappings/footer/cleanup. Report disagreements without fitting or rewriting captures. No live runtime-state, general transcendental/exception ABI, actor/rendering/campaign or Rust-parity inference; no Rust or native-game action.

Continue DW with exact on-demand retrieval of the stack allocator, descriptor `0x4000` redirect and nondelta quaternion helpers. ## Optional callback and pose initializer follow-up (2026-10-29)

DT binds the optional context+0xc/virtual+4 call and three exact constructor sites that pass zero. Intervening initialization calls remain a boundary before claiming the field stays zero.

DU binds the28-byte wrapper and401-byte call-free/no-relocation initializer; all three first intervals contain only that wrapper call. Under valid disjoint memory/no asynchronous mutation, the zero callback remains zero through those first intervals. DV passes756 authored cases/296,352 copied-or-untouched words and16 controls with explicit RO context/model/flags. Direct x87 signaling-NaN position quieting is retained separately from alternate MOVSS/raw quaternion words. This does not admit installed/live data or later/other callers.

Next bounded research: admit actual accumulator model/mask/record/output identities only when the ownership/cursor guard allows a new finite native observation; preserve DE/DF/DR rejections and never rerun sealed producers. Independent static alternatives include the dynamic allocator/descriptor4000/nondelta frontiers in DM. No final-pose/campaign/Rust parity follows automatically. No Rust change.

## Scale dispatch and finite path validation plan (2026-10-29)

DP binds the complete client record decoder to the admitted server kernel, while DQ binds the scale dispatcher and exposes flag/x87 context that the decompiler signatures omit. Dispatch/math-mode globals remain runtime inputs; local code equivalence does not close indexed tables or error handlers.

DR's first fixture is rejected for literal alignment/extent admission; DS preserves the rejection and corrects both in a new root. DS passes58,320 finite exact outputs across both forced math paths/aliases with explicit authored globals and trapped opaque transfers. Indexed table bytes and scalar cleanup are now bound; table2/near-one interiors remain unexercised. A guarded finite path is not an unrestricted CRT/ABI contract or a live actor pose.

Next bounded research: bind the client's optional pose-debug callback context through exact constructors/callers, using SDK names only as navigation. Distinguish an optional pointer read and virtual slot from a live callback or mutation; preserve the direct bone-blend continuation. No Valve DLL initialization, native-game launch or Rust change.

DM binds conditional model-mask/sequence-weight/virtual-map filtering and DELTA/POST operand order. Its scale helper retains an unresolved runtime CRT dispatch frontier. Keep those claims separate from weighted whole-pose execution.

The client accumulator's optional indirect callback remains a context boundary, not an observed execution.

Bind exact client bodies/PE bytes and compare complete leaf instructions, consumed literals and mapped calls with the admitted server graph. Keep virtual/cache/actor input and final rendering separate; do not infer runtime dispatch flags or initialize Valve DLLs. No Rust or native-game change.

## Composite record decoder plan (2026-10-29)

## Plugin composition and dependency injection revision (2026-10-31)

The owner's review supersedes the earlier composition direction: core crates must
have no dependency, import, feature switch or registration naming a concrete
plugin. The executable is the composition root. It depends on the host library
and the selected plugins and injects their contributions before startup.

Dependency direction: application -> plugins -> core. A plugin may consume the
public host API in its optional Bevy adapter; its portable implementation stays
independent of Bevy. Core owns extension contracts and default behavior, while
plugins own implementations and state. Generic hooks and contributed commands,
settings, resources and presentation policies must support another implementation
without editing the core. Do not substitute a renamed facade containing concrete
plugins or global mutable registration for dependency injection.

Bounded migration: (1) expose the host as a library and create the executable's
composition root; (2) inject portable UI contributions through owned per-instance
registries, with duplicate registration errors and no process-global registry;
(3) inject runtime hooks and keep plugin state in plugin resources, moving all
F1-F4 policy out of core; (4) inject video policy and render adapters; (5) enforce
the dependency boundary and validate core-only, each plugin alone and both.
The existing extraction is work in progress until all five steps pass. Preserve
settings, input ordering, lifecycle behavior and the previous validation scope.

Status: steps 1-5 are implemented at source level; see docs/plugins.md for the
contracts and their known gaps, and STATUS.md for what is not tested.

### Extension contract guardrails (owner review, 2026-10-31)

Application composition owns plugin selection and build features. Core exposes
small typed contracts; implementations own their state. Prefer typed components,
events and namespaced registries to a broad callback object with mutable access to
all host state. Multiple contributors must coexist, with explicit selection and
conflict/override rules rather than accidental registration-order replacement.
Validate declared dependencies and required capabilities before gameplay; dependencies
between plugins use public contracts and form an acyclic graph.

Omitted plugins contribute no controls, commands, systems, packaged assets or frame
work, while saved preferences remain intact. Verify core-only, each-plugin and
combined configurations, and demonstrate adding/replacing an independent contributor
without editing core. Keep localized resource priority and engine-independent
simulation boundaries. Future NPC, weapon, ironsight and attachment examples are
contract-design scenarios, not authorization to implement those features now.

## Ultra camera highlights, door budget and retail frame reuse (2026-10-30)

Stronger camera blur follows contrast with the surroundings, not brightness: compare a trail's brightest and
darkest points with a local log-average luminance held in a four-texel-wide image, on a 6:1 to 12:1 ramp. Only
unusual trails refine and blend at full strength. The weapon keeps full strength; Ultra softens the world only.
The object gather runs at quarter size, reads one MSAA sample per tap and shares a 25-tap budget with a per-pixel
phase; the native pass composites its result. The world reduction reads one sample per pixel. Retail blur swaps the view's main textures instead of copying the frame,
and clears its attachment because it overwrites every sample. Motion prepasses cull as the colour pass does;
shadow casters keep both faces. Evidence and rejected variants are in MODLOG.

Compare adaptive sampling to uniform sampling with the same brightness-aware blend, not

only viewmodel animation reconstruction to retail camera blur, as the a8 design did. Ultra
adds reconstructed camera/world and opaque/masked projectile motion. A default-false
motion_blur_world field preserves old Advanced saves as weapon-only; a shared typed mode
policy drives UI and host gates. Selecting Ultra enables both extensions; the retail master
cvar resets to Enabled. Ultra is a localized HL2RS label: the installed GameUI_Ultra token
actually displays Very High. A cyan status explains Ultra's experimental GPU tuning.
Retain the saved fields, test all four modes/legacy JSON/cvar reset and owned labels, then
verify GPU world/weapon pass activation and Advanced performance. Historical benchmark
series labelled Advanced before this split measured what is now Ultra. No GPU model is
required/blocked for Ultra; frame budgets and measured resolution/AA determine suitability.

## Bound the weapon reduction to current raster coverage (2026-10-27)

Both cooperative kernels preserve their results but fail to establish a Metal cost win;
retain them and their GPU comparator on the cooperative archive branch. Return to the
fragment reduction. For the weapon camera only, project the existing per-camera visible
meshes' current AABBs after transform/visibility updates. Union conservative screen bounds;
missing/nonfinite or near-plane-crossing bounds fail open to the full frame. Fully behind
or offscreen boxes contribute nothing. Expand by two source pixels and round outward to
4x4 reduction texels. Clear the entire existing reduced attachment, then scissor only the
first reduction draw; later dilation/gathers remain full and preserve blur trails. No new
image/pass, sample/tap/exposure/AA reduction or per-frame heap allocation. World reduction
stays full. Test projection conservativeness and odd/edge/empty extents, then weapon coverage,
silhouettes and three native pairs. Profile projected-region CPU cost and processed texels.

Promote the independently useful direct-drawing policy only for the measured Apple M1 Pro
Metal adapter, with an explicit startup override and the profiling override retained. Other
GPUs keep their existing default. Choose/register before cameras spawn; record the selected
mode, test hardware/override policy and verify normal packaged captures before committing.
Move background app construction into a startup helper to keep main.rs within its existing
size baseline. This is a narrow startup policy, not a new renderer or third-party patch.

## Declare priming separately from sustained measurements (2026-10-27)

Several first pairs have baseline-memory drift while subsequent pairs are stable. Keep
those original results. For future sustained qualification, BLUR_PRIME=1 prospectively
records one Enabled and one Advanced priming run before the three measured alternating
pairs. Retain their full reports/counters in priming.json; do not mix them into sustained
medians or delete cold/pressure evidence. Every measured pair retains the same resource
matching and frame-time guards. Priming is explicit and defaults off. This distinguishes
cold allocation/cache behavior from steady workload; it does not establish lower peak memory
or excuse pressure caused by a feature. Record the command/policy before collecting data.

## Cooperative motion reduction with unchanged samples (2026-10-27)

Direct drawing removes most render-phase write overhead and greatly improves 720p, but the
native 4x-AA blur still fails its budget. Preserve that source on its archive branch. Replace
only the first 4x4 maximum-motion reduction with a cooperative compute kernel: an 8x8 source
block produces four reduced texels, with up to four sample lanes sharing MSAA loads. Keep
all samples, thresholding and y/x/sample tie order identical; no tap/exposure/AA change and
no readback. Write the existing RGBA16F reduced image directly, avoiding a new image or extra
pass. Dispatch covers partial edge tiles and all output texels. Keep later reductions and
world/weapon gathers unchanged. Validate shader compilation, actual motion/silhouette/coverage
probes, then three alternating native pairs and work/frame against the archived direct path.
Reject/archive any cost or correctness regression. The owner deprioritised watt telemetry;
continue measured work/frame and memory proxies without pursuing privileged power sampling.

## Reduce render preparation and measure work per frame (2026-10-27)

Uncapped native and 720p data show render-phase buffer preparation around 2.3 ms Enabled
and 4.5-4.7 ms Advanced, despite the pixel-count reduction. Test the supported direct-draw
camera mode as a profiling-only experiment: an opt-in startup flag adds NoIndirectDrawing
when cameras are initially created, never during play. Keep default rendering unchanged
until measured. Retain geometry, materials, resolution/AA, simulation and blur filter math.
Compare three alternating pairs at matched settings, CPU work/frame, GPU busy-time proxies
and memory, then image/pose/occlusion checks. The documented general risk is extra CPU draw
work and lost indirect batching/culling; reject/archive a regression rather than assuming a
win. Do not modify Bevy. Preserve this variant separately before replacing it.

The owner also requires compute/energy efficiency, beyond maximum FPS. Report process CPU
milliseconds per rendered frame and explicitly system-wide GPU-busy milliseconds/frame as
a proxy, with missing watt/joule fields null. Local powermetrics refuses unprivileged use;
actual power is unavailable, not estimated from utilisation. Later energy comparisons need
matched-FPS, scene, resolution, thermal and power-source conditions and a trustworthy power
source. Fixed-rate and uncapped measurements answer different questions; neither alone
establishes energy savings. Keep peak FPS, frame tails, work/frame and memory as separate
criteria, preserving gameplay and blur quality.

## Uncapped profiling before low-settings throughput work (2026-10-27)

The existing background runner's 1/60 s interval includes profiling runs. Separate test

After switching compositions to single-sample post_process_write, the per-view foreground

Repeated native qualification defaults to three Enabled/Advanced pairs per scene, with

## Conservative empty-ray skipping (2026-10-27)

Reuse the existing undilated 32x32 maximum-motion tiles for native world gathers. When the
destination is stationary, samples with no independent object motion contribute neither
colour nor coverage. If an undilated tile is zero, its raw samples are all below the motion
onset. Skip directly to a conservative sample index at/before the ray exits that tile,
retaining the original tap denominator and every potentially contributing sample. Do not
skip revealed-background estimates for moving destinations. Enable the lookup only on rays
at least sixteen pixels long, where skipping can offset its cost. Unlike the rejected
occupancy lookup, this avoids whole loop iterations; no image or pass is added. Validate
GPU foreground ownership, occlusion, projected projectile silhouettes and native performance.

Quiet preflight is preferred, but resource comparability remains the committed matched-input
protocol: a stable moderate baseline is a valid input. Retain the quiet-baseline observation
without requiring an arbitrary completely idle machine. Earlier experimental quiet-only
flags stay in their evidence; use fresh runs for qualification. Frame-time thresholds and
actual competing-work/pressure rejection remain unchanged.

## Lazy native world gather (2026-10-27)

World producers already export object-only UV motion and explicitly disable sky/background
vectors. The native object gather can classify static samples from motion alone. Read
MSAA depth only for moving samples, for the destination's occlusion depth, or when a moving
centre estimates revealed background. Compute camera reprojection only for contributing
foreground samples. Quarter camera filtering retains its current depth handling. No new
mask/cache/pass is needed. For short world trajectories, use the existing 1.5 samples/pixel
density with an odd minimum of three instead of seventeen; trajectories over eleven pixels
and the weapon filter retain their dense counts. Preserve odd centred coverage, silhouette
expansion, foreground occlusion, exposure and reach. Validate native performance and all
world/weapon GPU probes before accepting; avoid the rejected extra occupancy texture.

## Conservative weapon motion precheck (2026-10-27)

Reuse the existing dilated maximum-UV-motion field before reading native/MSAA weapon depth
and vectors. If length(maximum UV motion) * max(width,height) * exposure is below one pixel,
all owned sample/mean velocities are also below the existing filter onset, including on
non-square views. Return the same sharp input immediately. Use the conservative maximum
frame dimension, not the dominant vector's pixel length, because maximum UV length does
not preserve pixel-length ordering across axes. Keep all active filter taps, coverage,
shutter and surface ownership unchanged. This adds no resource or pass. Verify the existing
GPU ownership/coverage and crowbar silhouette tests at no AA/4x AA and native performance.

## Headless GPU completion pacing (2026-10-27)

Resource traces show a rising GPU-managed memory footprint during expensive headless runs.
Unlike a window surface, the offscreen target supplies no acquisition backpressure. Test
whether submission outruns completion before accepting CPU-present FPS as GPU throughput.
For headless --profile only, keep two submission fences in a fixed ring. Use an empty queue
submission as a completion marker after rendering; wait only for the oldest marker before
reusing its slot. Record submitted/observed-completed frames, bounded pending fences and
wait durations. This adds no new dependency, render pass, callback allocation or ordinary/
interactive-game work. Verify completion pacing, resource stability and measured throughput;
if it does not explain the growth, retain the failed evidence and change approach.

The environment sampler's first GPU reading can retain the preceding run's load while CPU
counters have no initial delta. Retain this primer separately, wait one sampling interval,
and compare the subsequent baseline only; otherwise stale GPU readings create false
ambient-load mismatches. Preserve raw data and mark earlier qualification flags accordingly.

## Hardware-aware blur qualification (2026-10-27)

Pair each native-resolution blur run with bounded, timestamp-aligned frame and host-resource
windows. Frame windows collect only under --profile after warm-up, without per-frame
allocation or file access. Keep 128 windows in a ring, whole-run frame percentiles, and
report retained/dropped counts. Sample host telemetry about once per second outside the
game; record sampler overhead and missing fields. On Apple Silicon, read aggregate CPU
load counters, driver-reported renderer/tiler/device utilisation, VM page counts/pressure
and benchmark-process CPU time/RSS. Unified memory is one pool; dedicated VRAM availability
is unavailable, not total RAM minus GPU allocations. Unsupported platforms retain explicit
unavailable metrics; do not invent compute availability from core count or free memory.

Align resource samples with frame windows, report same-workload associations and paired
Advanced/Enabled cost ratios. Pixel throughput and frames per consumed game CPU-second
are defined ratios; system GPU busy-time throughput is a labelled utilisation proxy, not
FLOPS or an isolated GPU duration. Resource use includes the workload itself, so correlation
is not causation. Record competing games/builds and pressure changes, flag mismatched
paired environments, and never relax the existing frame-time thresholds to explain a
regression away. Collect all cases even when one performance guard fails. Validate counter
parsers, alignment, unified-memory semantics, missing metrics and correlations with fake
samples, then run isolated owned packages. Keep render experiments separate from this
instrumentation checkpoint; primary checkout/user settings remain untouched.

## Advanced high-resolution/MSAA regression (2026-10-27)

Owner measures a8a050b at about 50 FPS with no shake penalty, versus f7369cd at about
40 FPS idle and 10 FPS shaking, at 3456x2168 with 4x MSAA. The earlier 1024x768/no-AA
measurements do not qualify this path. Preserve the a8 weapon filter and retail modes.
First isolate full-resolution reconstruction, MSAA reads and world-prepass costs with copied
packages at the owner's settings. Bound expensive world gathers to a reduced-resolution
intermediate, composite to native resolution only where motion contributes, and avoid
repeated MSAA/depth/reprojection work in gathers. Retain projectile silhouette tests and
bright-lamp quality checks; do not accept a smooth-but-aliased or scenery-smearing fallback.
Measure still/violent turns at actual resolution/AA against both commits, report peaks as
well as averages, run local/Windows-target checks and packaged regressions, then commit.
Isolation shows the prepass alone is not dominant. Source/effect prepasses export an
explicit host-owned object-only motion channel; only our Advanced filters consume it.
Static positions return zero before projection. Disable the world background-vector pass,
since this renderer reconstructs sky/camera motion itself. Camera reprojection runs separately in the
bounded image filter; moving silhouettes stay native resolution in motion-bounded tiles.
Record --profile frame p95/p99 upper bounds and exact maximum without per-frame allocation;
ordinary play does not collect the distribution. Preserve the same alpha masks in effect
depth/color paths. This channel is not compatible with a future generic temporal consumer
without an adapter; no such consumer is currently installed on these views.

The extra full-resolution resolved-motion cache failed both dense and sparse variants;
a separate single-sample prepass camera also failed the idle budget. Reject both instead
of retaining their framebuffer/geometry overhead. An occupancy-mask lookup using the
existing conservative 4x4 reduction also failed its initial guard; contributor compilation
overlapped part of that batch, so it does not establish isolated GPU cost. Remove the
experiment and obtain individual GPU pass timings before further structural changes.
Potential next designs: motion-bounded work lists, short multiscale camera gathers and
shared-memory tile caching. Preserve thin projectiles, occlusion, the a8 weapon path,
configured visible MSAA and the native-resolution performance thresholds.

measure paired timings, run local/Windows-target checks and commit the bounded checkpoint.

## Movie playback and startup video sequence (2026-10-27)

Bounded design for full-screen movie playback (`playvideo` / `playvideo_exitcommand`) and the startup video sequence across `source-assets`, `hl2-ui`, and `hl2-bevy`:

1. **Bink container parser (`source-assets::bink`)**:
   - Parse Bink 1 container header (`BIKi`, `BIKb`, `BIKf`, `BIKg`, `BIKh`): file size, frame count, width, height, FPS (`fps_num / fps_den`), calculated duration in seconds, flags, and audio track parameters.
   - Unit tests against synthetic header fixtures and owned installation videos (`media/valve.bik`, `media/ep1_recap.bik`, `media/ep2_teaser.bik`).
2. **Console commands and modal `VideoPanel` (`hl2-ui::console`, `hl2-ui::gameui::video`)**:
   - Register `playvideo <filename> [width height]` and `playvideo_exitcommand <filename> <exit command>` in `COMMANDS` (`cvars.golden`).
   - Path resolution: look under `media/<filename>`, appending `.bik` if unadorned.
   - Implement `VideoPanelState`: full-screen modal blackout, aspect-ratio letterboxing/pillarboxing (`flVideoRatio` vs `flFrameRatio`), skip key handling (`KEY_ESCAPE`, `KEY_SPACE`, `KEY_ENTER`, `KEY_BACKQUOTE` / `~`), elapsed playback clock, and exit command dispatch.
   - Retail fallback invariant: if the video cannot be opened or played, log `Unable to play video: %s` and immediately dispatch the exit command (`engine->ClientCmd(pExitCommand)`) so campaign progression (e.g. `ep2/cfg/chapter1.cfg` -> `map ep2_outland_01`) never soft-locks.
3. **Host wiring and startup sequence (`hl2-bevy`)**:
   - Wire `Effect::PlayVideo` in `sim.console_effects`, muting active background/ambient audio while playback is active and unmuting on stop.
   - Startup videos: on launch without `--novid` / `-novid`, without `--map`, and without `--no-setup`, check `media/StartupVids.txt` (default `media/valve.bik`), play before displaying the main menu, and transition to the menu upon completion or skip.
   - Instrument presentation report with active `video` playback state (`filename`, `playing`, `elapsed`, `duration`, `exit_command`).

Validation: unit tests in `source-assets` and `hl2-ui`; `validate.sh`, `check_windows.sh`, and packaged replay checks (`replays.py`).

## Developer commentary mode (2026-10-27)

Bounded design for Developer Commentary across `source-ui`, `hl2-ui`, `hl2-simulation` and `hl2-bevy`:

1. **Chapter browser footer toggle and card badge (`source-ui::card_browser`, `hl2-ui::gameui::new_game`)**:
   - Read `newgamedialog.res` and `newgamechapterpanel.res` through `Reader::new(vfs)` so `hl2_complete/resource/newgamedialog.res` and `hl2_complete/resource/newgamechapterpanel.res` take precedence when present.
   - Parse `ImagePanel1` (`22, 254, 308x40`), `CommentaryIcon` (`25, 258, 43x42`, `scaleAmount 0.5`), and `Commentary` (`CheckButton` at `60, 262, 264x24`, font `ChapterTitle`, label `#HL2_Enable_Commentary`) from `newgamedialog.res`, plus the per-card `CommentaryIcon` (`10, 100, 43x42`, `scaleAmount 0.5`) from `newgamechapterpanel.res`.
   - Decode `materials/vgui/resource/commentary_hl2c.vtf` (fallback `materials/vgui/hud/icon_commentary_small.vtf`).
   - Add generic optional footer toggle and card badge specs to `source_ui::card_browser::BrowserLayout` without naming HL2 in `source-ui`. When checked, each chapter card renders the scaled badge at `(10, 100)` over the thumbnail's bottom-left corner. Include the checkbox in `CardBrowser`'s keyboard focus and pointer handling.
2. **Explanation confirmation and cvar (`hl2-ui::console`, `hl2-ui::config`)**:
   - Add `commentary: bool` (`#[serde(default)]`) to `GameplaySettings` and register the `commentary` cvar in `COMMANDS` (`cvars.golden`).
   - When starting a chapter from `NewGameDialog` with commentary unchecked, clear `config.gameplay.commentary` and `cheats`, emit `Effect::ApplyConfig`, close `NewGame` and start the map.
   - When starting a chapter with commentary checked, open modal `ConfirmKind::StartCommentary(GameMap)` using `MessageBox` (`380x240`, title `#GameUI_CommentaryDialogTitle`, text `#GAMEUI_Commentary_Console_Explanation`, OK `#vgui_ok`, Cancel `#vgui_cancel`, matching `hl2/resource/commentaryexplanationdialog.res`). Confirming sets `config.gameplay.commentary = true` and `cheats = true`, emits `Effect::ApplyConfig`, closes `NewGame` and starts the map; cancelling returns focus to `NewGame`.
3. **In-game `point_commentary_node` simulation and presentation (`hl2-simulation::commentary`, `hl2-ui::hud`, `hl2-bevy`)**:
   - Parse `maps/<map>_commentary.txt` (`Entities` -> `entity` blocks, replacing `^` with `"` in string values and converting `connections` subblocks to standard entity outputs).
   - When commentary mode is enabled on a gameplay map, append the parsed commentary entities and the `commentary_semaphore` `info_target` before model loading so `point_commentary_node` spawns with `models/extras/info_speech.mdl` (preloading both skin 0 `speech_info` and skin 1 `speech_info_used`). Fire `commentary_auto` outputs (`OnCommentaryNewGame`, `OnCommentaryMultiplayerSpawn`) on map spawn.
   - Support `StartCommentary`, `StopCommentary`, `StartUnstoppableCommentary`, `Enable`, `Disable` and `Toggle` inputs on `point_commentary_node`.
   - Run per-tick commentary logic only while `commentary` is enabled (zero per-frame work when off): raycast the player's view against visible `point_commentary_node` `[-16, -16, -16]..[16, 16, 16]` bounds, spin the speech balloon (`0x` idle, `1x` when aimed at while unlistened, `3x` while active), toggle targeted nodes on `+use`, jump to the next node with a brief black fade on `+attack2`, execute `precommands` / `postcommands`, interpolate camera overrides for `viewposition` / `viewtarget` (with `point_commentary_viewpoint` sprite visibility and `prevent_movement`), emit audio and closed captions, switch finished nodes to skin 1 (`speech_info_used`), and make the player invulnerable to damage while commentary mode is active.
   - Add `HudCommentary` (`crates/hl2-ui/src/hud/commentary.rs`, parsed from `scripts/hudlayout.res` `HudCommentary`: `c-190, 350, 380x40`, `vgui/hud/icon_commentary`, speaker label, `N \ M` counter and orange progress bar), drawn while a node is active and `closecaption` is disabled (matching `CHudCommentary::ShouldDraw`).

Validation: unit tests and owned-install tests across `source-ui`, `hl2-ui`, `hl2-simulation` and `hl2-bevy`; `validate.sh`, `check_windows.sh`, and packaged UI/HUD checks.

## Shadow detail and the flashlight (2026-10-25)

Goal (owner): Options > Video > Advanced > Shadow detail at all three levels, and the flashlight that High
needs. Retail sources: Source SDK `game/client/clientshadowmgr.cpp` (casters, frames, atlas, blob and
render-to-texture shadows), `materialsystem/stdshaders/shadow_ps2x.fxc` (the receiver), `flashlighteffect.cpp`
and `common_flashlight_fxc.h` (the flashlight light and its depth shadow), `game/server/hl2/hl2_player.cpp`
(suit power, `impulse 100`); the installed `decals/simpleshadow`, `decals/rendershadow`, `effects/flashlight*`
materials and the maps' `shadow_control` entities (66 of 78 base maps).

Levels: Low = `r_shadowrendertotexture 0` (every caster a blob of `decals/simpleshadow` straight down), Medium =
render-to-texture silhouettes along the map's shadow direction, High = Medium plus the flashlight's depth-map
shadows (`r_flashlightdepthtexture 1`). Shadows exist at every level (retail has no "off" in the dialog).

Steps, each committed on its own:

1. Shadow geometry, pure and tested in hl2-simulation (`shadows`): `shadow_control` settings and the
   no-entity defaults; one caster's projected frame (origin, axes, size, falloff start, cast distance, the
   light-facing box faces); the receiver grid over the world's triangles (brush faces and displacements;
   static props, baked into the world's batches and told apart by their bone weights, receive only NPC
   shadows at render-to-texture detail, as `AddShadowToReceiver` rules); the engine's clipper as the retail
   engine runs it: receivers facing the light (`n . d <= -0.01`) with the shadow origin in front of them,
   polygons kept when a vertex is short of the cast end, clipped to the texture square, to depth 0 and to
   the light faces, each vertex given the fade byte `min(255, 240 * (z - falloff start) / span + bias)`
   (240 at the cast end, fading on past it: nothing is clipped there); the blob frame (+10 units, origin
   backed up twice, snapped to 0.5).
2. Low: host module `hl2-bevy` `shadows/`: the receiver grid built on the map worker (translucent,
   alpha-tested, additive and `SURF_NOSHADOWS` water materials do not receive); one shadow mesh per caster
   entity (studio-model entities without `disableshadows`, bounds as `C_BaseAnimating::GetRenderBounds`: the
   model's view box or hull with the current sequence's box), rewritten in place when its caster moved by a
   quarter shadow texel; a `ShadowMaterial` (multiply blend, the 5-tap coverage blur, colour gamma-to-linear
   from `shadow_control`, no fog term as the world has none yet) drawn with other translucent geometry; the
   blob picture from the installed `decals/simpleshadow`.
3. Medium: the engine's per-frame flow. Shadows in view (frustum and PVS) are ranked by screen area; the 32
   largest get a fragment of the 1024-texel page (`CTextureAllocator`: blocks of 16 to 256 texels, an LRU
   per size, sized by screen area up to the caster's own 2-texels-per-unit size) and their silhouette is
   rasterized on the CPU into it when the fragment is new, the caster moved, or it has pose parameters;
   the rest show the blob picture on the same projection without clip planes. Silhouettes come from the
   caster's coarsest drawable level (a trailing `$shadowlod` is not read yet), posed with its bone
   matrices, translucent and alpha-tested parts by texture alpha.
4. The flashlight: `impulse 100` and the default F bind, suit power drain and recharge and the HUD's power
   bar device line, the on/off sounds, and the light itself: the projected `effects/flashlight_border`
   texture with the SDK's attenuation (constant 0, linear 100), far fade (750 units, from 60 %) and Lambert
   term added to world and model shading in `source.wgsl` (one pass instead of the engine's extra additive
   pass), the origin pull-back trace of `UpdateLightNew`.
5. High: a depth map rendered from the flashlight's frustum (`r_flashlightdepthres` 1024) sampled by the
   flashlight term with the SDK's Poisson filter and shadow attenuation (`r_flashlightshadowatten` 0.35);
   rendered only while the flashlight is on at High.

Performance rules: no shadow work for a caster that did not move and is not animating (its mesh and cell are
kept); the receiver grid is built once per map off the main thread; silhouettes of animated casters only when
their pose sample changes; the flashlight term costs nothing while it is off (a uniform flag, no texture
binding change), its depth pass exists only at High with the light on. Each step measures frame time with the
level off and on (packaged, `d1_trainstation_02` plaza and a citizen-heavy view) and records it in MODLOG.

Not planned: receivers on brush entities (doors, trains), shadows of brush physboxes.

Handoff (2026-10-25): steps 1-3 are committed (blob shadows at Low, render-to-texture at Medium/High). Step 4 is
committed in part at that checkpoint: `hl2_simulation::flashlight` (power, toggle, placement, flicker) and `hl2-bevy` `flashlight.rs`
(input, sounds, a Bevy `SpotLight` read by `source.wgsl`'s `flashlight()`). Frame time is measured (spawn corridor of `d1_trainstation_02`,
eye -4304 -224 64, yaw -90, pitch -10: previous commit 18.40, off 18.40, on 18.39 ms). Left for step 4 at that checkpoint (completed by the continuation below): the HUD: `HudSuitPower` from `hl2/scripts/hudlayout.res` (16/420,
102x48, bar inset 8/15 width 92 height 4, chunks 6 gap 3, label 8/4, device lines from 8/22 every 10, colours 255 220
0 220 / low 255 0 0 220, exhausted alpha 70), its `SuitAuxPower*` animations in `hudanimations.txt`, tokens
`#Valve_Hud_AUX_POWER` and `#Valve_Hud_FLASHLIGHT`, fed from the host's `HostFlashlight` (suit power, flashlight
on); Episode Two's `HudFlashlight` (WeaponIconsSmall glyphs 169/174 and a 28-wide bar). Step 5 (High): turn on
`shadow_maps_enabled` on the flashlight spot light only at High, sample `fetch_spot_shadow` with the light id found
in `flashlight()`, blend toward 1 by `r_flashlightshadowatten` 0.35 as `DoFlashlight` does; custom materials need a
shadow (prepass) shader that discards alpha-tested texels.
