## Architecture guardrails (2026-10-24)

Goal (owner): the refactoring series stops; checks keep the structure from decaying instead.
`scripts/checks/architecture.py` (standard library Python, about 4 s) runs first in validate.sh, in
CI and in the local pre-commit hook, and names the file and the fix in every failure (rules in
AGENTS.md "Architecture guardrails").

- Sizes: functions up to 100 lines, a file's production code up to 1,000 lines, its tests up to
  2,000. Limits apply to new code; the 113 functions, 25 files and 3 test files over them today are
  listed with their sizes in `architecture-baseline.json` and may only shrink (a ratchet:
  `UPDATE_BASELINE=1` lowers entries, nothing raises them except `ACCEPT_GROWTH=1`, for merges of
  main or with the owner's approval). Functions are keyed by file and name, sizes sorted, so
  reordering or moving within a file changes nothing.
- Coupling: the allowed edges between workspace crates (any dependency table, dev and target
  included), the crates that may use bevy/rfd (hl2-bevy) and rapier3d/symphonia/hound
  (hl2-simulation), no file IO in modkit-core, hl2-simulation and source-ui, no HL2 names in
  source-ui's code.
- Duplication: every 8-line window of significant production lines (comments, imports,
  attributes and lone brackets left out; strings kept) is fingerprinted; a fingerprint seen twice
  that the baseline does not list fails. Fingerprints follow the text, so moving code keeps them.
  The 143 known ones are mostly authored data tables (activity tables, default schedules).
- Counts per crate that may not grow: mutable statics, `too_many_arguments` and `dead_code`
  allowances, glob imports.
- Counting: `#[cfg(test)]` items are skipped by matching their braces (not by cutting the file at
  the first one), and braces inside strings, raw strings, chars and comments are ignored.
- Proven to bite (each case reverted): a new 101-line function, one more line in
  `AiRuntime::think`, source-ui depending on hl2-ui, hl2-ui on bevy, file IO and an `HL2RS_` token
  in source-ui, a pasted 8-line block, a new `dead_code` allowance; an improvement left unrecorded
  fails until `UPDATE_BASELINE=1` records it.
- Not covered: architecture that no metric shows (a concern in the wrong crate within the allowed
  layers, blocking reads in frame systems); review still applies.

## Exact and local checks (2026-10-24)

Goal (owner, after the architecture review of 2026-10-24): every change is cheap to check. A
failing check points at its cause, and a replay difference always means a real change. Two steps;
the current scope is the UI, so entity and NPC work from that review is not part of it.

1. Replays that repeat exactly. The gameplay outcome comparison already leaves out fields that
   differ between two runs of one build (`hits` and `ray` of three pallet fixtures,
   `occluded_blast_targets` everywhere), and two more varied during the toolkit split without
   being left out: `bevy-pallet-gibs-fade`'s kill and gibs at tick 32 instead of 34, and
   `bevy-monitors-kleiner-scene`'s gib removals (7 instead of 8 at its last two labels).
   - Method: run each of these four fixtures ten times one game at a time and ten times two at
     once (how often each field varies, and whether machine load matters); compare every report
     field of two differing runs to find the earliest sample and field that diverge; trace that
     field to its inputs.
   - Hypotheses, in this order: a model the map did not preload, loaded on a worker thread and
     inserted when ready (`runtime_props`), arriving a tick later under load; iteration over
     hash-ordered collections (Rust randomizes their order per process; hl2-simulation's physics
     keeps about twenty); something sampled per rendered frame instead of per fixed tick.
   - Fix: in the host, the smallest replay-only change that leaves ordinary play unchanged (as
     `--mute` is); in the simulation, a deterministic order. A field whose cause is fixed leaves
     the comparison's list of varying fields.
   - Stop rule: two real attempts at the cause, then the evidence goes into STATUS and the two
     unmarked fields are marked as varying.
   - Done when each of the four fixtures gives identical outcomes over ten runs, and every field
     still listed as varying is explained in STATUS.
   - Closed without a change (owner): the cause is in the physics library (STATUS), and its fix
     (rapier3d's `enhanced-determinism`, which also forces software math) is not worth its cost
     for UI work; performance is never traded. The fields keep varying as before.
2. Tests for the toolkit itself. Five source-ui files have no tests of their own (`shell.rs`,
   `console/panel.rs`, `console/editor.rs`, `console/command.rs`, `input.rs`: 2,143 lines, 65
   public functions); hl2-ui's console tests cover them only through HL2's `Console`.
   - New tests inside source-ui over a small fake app: a `shell::Host` with two windows, a modal
     window, the console and a base, recording every hook call so the documented call order is
     asserted; a minimal `command::Context` with one command, one cvar and one list of names.
   - Cases: Escape's order (the app's drag, the console's drag, history search or completion, the
     modal state, the top window, the base) and a held Escape closing on release; the pointer to
     the topmost window else the base, a press staying with its window until release, a modal
     window taking every press; key repeat stopping on a mode change and during key capture; the
     console toggle (blocked, enabled, opening, closed); `close_window`; the console dialog's
     scrollback and numbering, `replace_snapshot`, filters and timestamps, wrapping, selection
     and copying, Close and Submit (Enter too); commands (run, unknown, usage, tokenizing); the
     editor's history and completion through a fake `Completer`; key repeat timing.
   - hl2-ui's console tests stay: they test HL2's use of the toolkit.
   - Done when no source-ui file is without tests, and an Escape-order bug introduced on purpose
     fails a source-ui test (then reverted).
   - Done: the five files test themselves over a fake app (`shell::tests::App`, a `command`
     test context, a completer for the editor, one that completes nothing for the dialog). The
     key-repeat test and the four line-editor tests moved from hl2-ui, which keeps its tests of
     HL2's own completion; moving the app's modal-state check before the console's history
     search fails `escape_undoes_a_drag_then_ends_the_console_search_then_the_modal_state_then_closes_the_top`.

Validation: step 1 with the repeated runs and the full replay suite; step 2 with
`scripts/checks/validate.sh` and the Windows-target check (tests only, no behaviour change).

## UI toolkit split: source-ui and Half-Life 2's UI (2026-10-24)

Goal (owner): code that is easy to find and understand. hl2-ui is split into a game-agnostic,
Source-aware UI toolkit, `source-ui`, and Half-Life 2's own UI, `hl2-ui`, the way the Source SDK
keeps `vgui_controls` (Source SDK `vgui2/vgui_controls/`: `Button.cpp`, `Frame.cpp`,
`PropertySheet.cpp`, `Menu.cpp`, `RichText.cpp`, `MessageBox.cpp`, `consoledialog.cpp`, shared by
every Source game) apart from the game's GameUI module (main menu, Options pages, loading dialog;
not part of the SDK 2013 release) and the client HUD (Source SDK
`game/client/hl2/hud_weaponselection.cpp`, `game/client/hud_closecaption.cpp`). The toolkit reads
Source formats through source-assets (`.res` layouts, KeyValues, schemes, fonts from the game's
file system), depends on no hl2-* crate and knows no HL2 content or settings: no `Config`,
`Effect`, weapons, maps, chapters, cvars, console commands, `HL2RS_` tokens or fallback strings.
hl2-ui keeps its name and depends on source-ui (and, from step 5, on hl2-simulation directly).
Call sites import from where the code lives; there are no compatibility re-exports. Nothing
changes behaviour: output, errors, effects, UI and gameplay outcomes stay identical.

Rule: what Source keeps in `vgui_controls`, or what knows nothing about HL2 content or settings,
belongs to the toolkit.

1. `source-ui` with the clearly generic modules, moved verbatim (only paths and visibility
   change, tests move with their code): `canvas`, `native_font` (Windows GDI), `font`
   (`FontFace`, `TextOutline`, from `hud.rs`), `input` (`Key`, `Input`, `KeyRepeat`, from
   `console.rs`), `widgets`, `placement` (with `DialogPlacement`, from `config.rs`), `windows`,
   `layout`, `scheme_fonts`, `keys`, `message_box` (from `gameui/menu.rs`), `viewport` (its
   generic part), `localize` (the token table, parser, escapes and key-name fallback, with a
   `Fallback` trait through which an app adds its own text) and the ProggyClean font. Kept in
   hl2-ui after reading the code: `input.rs` (bindings and mouse filtering read `Config` and
   `MouseSettings`; Source's client `CInput`), `gameui/display.rs` (the Video page's choices,
   with `HL2RS_` placeholder labels, stored in `VideoSettings`), `gameui/loading.rs` (the
   loading dialog carries the background-map transition and the pre-blurred backgrounds),
   `proportional.rs` and `tuning.rs` (HL2RS's narrow-screen design height and menu growth from
   `assets/ui/tuning.json`), and the flexible-frame ("Fit Display") viewport functions, which
   use HL2RS's `9:16..=32:9` range from the same file (`hl2_ui::flexible_frame`). So
   `Painter::scale_for_canvas`, which applied that design height, is hl2-ui's
   `proportional::dialog_scale`; the quit and return-to-menu boxes are `menu::quit_box` /
   `menu::return_to_main_menu_box` over the toolkit's `MessageBox`; `Windows::new` takes the
   unfocused alpha from the app; hl2-ui's `gameui::localize::Localization` is the toolkit engine
   with HL2RS's `InRepoText` (the placeholders, then `FALLBACK_STRINGS`). The `Window` ids
   (Options, Console, SubDialog, Confirmation) stay as they are until step 4.
2. Declarative cvars in hl2-ui `console/commands/`: a golden test of every cvar (query, valid
   set, invalid value, too many arguments: logged lines, error, `Effect`) committed first, then
   each cvar described once (value function and setter) with one shared function that prints
   `"name" = "value"`, saves, echoes and returns `ApplyConfig`, keeping the special cases exact.
3. A generic console framework in source-ui: line editor, history, completion, output buffer,
   panel drawing and a command registry the app fills in, with handlers over an app-provided
   context. HL2's command table, handlers and completion sources (maps, entity targets and
   inputs) stay in hl2-ui and register into it. Two commits:
   - 3a: `source_ui::console::{command, complete, editor}`. The registry is generic over the app
     (`command::Context`: the effect type and the app's lists of names, `Arg::Names`);
     `Spec<C>`, handlers, value functions and setters take the app's context, so HL2's setters
     take the console. hl2-ui's `console::commands` is HL2's instantiation (`Names`,
     `impl Context for Console`, aliases of `Spec`/`Arg`/`Outcome`, the table, `run_setting`);
     its `console::complete` keeps the host-supplied `Sources` and binds the toolkit's
     completion to `COMMANDS` (the editor completes through `complete::Completer`).
   - 3b: the output buffer and the console dialog as one toolkit component: the lines, levels
     (the viewmodel tuner's replaceable line becomes a neutral replaceable level), filter,
     timestamps, wrapping, scrolling, selection, copying, the entry, the completion popup and
     the dialog's buttons and placement. The app passes in what is its own: the scale policy
     (hl2-ui's `proportional::dialog_scale`), the resolved title, Submit and reverse-search
     texts, the window theme and focus, and the Painter's fonts; the component answers input
     with actions (close, submit a line), which the app turns into effects in the current
     order; the app keeps saving the placement in `Config`.
4. `Console` (63 fields, 121 methods) split along the toolkit/app line: the source-ui shell
   (window stack, focus, Escape, pointer and keyboard routing, modal occlusion, the developer
   console panel) and hl2-ui's GameUI (main and pause menus, backgrounds and chapters, the quit
   box, the Options lifecycle, loading, commands, `Config`), each owning the fields it uses. The
   design (see "Step 4 design" below) comes before the code.
5. hl2-shared folds back into hl2-simulation (weapons, selection, damage and `VIEWMODEL_FOV`
   return to their original modules; `InventoryView`, `Inventory`'s delegating methods and the
   HUD tests' copy of the inventory go); hl2-ui depends on hl2-simulation, source-ui still does
   not.
6. Visibility and imports narrowed: functions used only in their own file become private
   (checked by the compiler), glob imports become explicit.
7. `AiRuntime::think` (1,225 lines) split into its phases, one commit per phase, if its local
   state allows it. Not done: one `&mut AiActor` borrow spans about 1,040 of its lines beside 13
   other `AiRuntime` fields and 7 parameters, and 19 locals live more than 300 lines, so phase
   functions would need the actor taken out of `self.actors` for the whole think and a context
   struct of 20 to 30 values; the sensing prefix alone would leave a 1,070-line function.

Validation per step: `scripts/checks/validate.sh`, the Windows-target check (it also lints
source-ui), the packaged console and UI replays (steps 2, 3 and 6) or the full replay suite
(steps 1, 4, 5 and 7) compared with the recorded baseline (only fields the suite marks unstable
may differ), the repository isolation check, the owned-install tests after steps 1, 4 and 5, and
the size change of each step in MODLOG.

### Step 4 design: the shell and HL2's GameUI

After step 3 the developer console dialog is the toolkit's `ConsolePanel`; `Console` still holds
the window stack and runs the control flow every frame goes through. That control flow is generic
(which window gets the pointer and the keyboard, Escape's hold-and-release, the console's own
Escape and toggling), but every step of it asks GameUI questions (is the loading dialog or the
quit box up, which Options layer is active, does the menu take the pointer). Three shapes were
weighed:

- A shell struct that only bundles the toolkit state (`Windows`, `ConsolePanel`, key repeat,
  Escape hold) moves five fields and leaves Escape, routing and modal occlusion in hl2-ui:
  little value, and not the split asked for.
- Splitting `Console` into separately borrowed `{shell, ui}` parts with the routing calling into
  the UI part breaks the command handlers, which run inside the routing loop (a submitted
  `map` starts loading and the loop then stops on the modal loading dialog) on `&mut Console`:
  they would have to be rethreaded or run after the loop, which changes behaviour.
- Chosen: the shell's control flow as generic toolkit code over a host trait that `Console`
  implements. `Console` stays hl2-bevy's facade and the commands' context; the toolkit code
  reaches the shell's state through short `host.shell()` / `host.shell_mut()` borrows and asks
  the host everything that is GameUI's.

The toolkit (`source_ui::shell`, `source_ui::windows`):

- `windows::WindowId`: what the toolkit needs of an app's window ids, whether one is modal and
  which one is the developer console. `Windows<W: WindowId>` keeps the z-order, focus, pointer
  ownership and fades as today. HL2's `Window` enum (Options, Console, SubDialog, Confirmation)
  moves back to hl2-ui and implements it, so reports still name the windows as before.
- `shell::Shell<W>` owns the window stack, the console dialog (`ConsolePanel`), whether Escape
  is held, the key repeat and the latest pointer. It answers what only needs them: the console
  window's theme and bounds, its cursor hint, opening it on screen.
- `shell::Host`: the hooks the control flow calls, in the order it calls them today: the host's
  mode (the key repeat stops when it changes) and key capture; its modal input (the loading
  dialog and the quit box, with the menu's occlusion); preparing a frame's windows (its dialog
  windows synchronized, the modal layer, which dialog takes input); window bounds and the
  console popup's; whether the base takes the pointer; per-window input (the console window's
  too, through the host, which owns the canvas and completion sources); whether the rest is
  blocked; the base's occlusion and input; and for Escape: whether a modal state blocks it,
  whether it closes a window, the held looks, cancelling the host's own drag, the modal Escape
  (loading, quit box), a dialog's Escape, the base's; for the console toggle: blocked,
  enabled, opening, closed; and window-closed bookkeeping.
- `shell::{input, escape_pressed, escape_released, escape, toggle, close_window}`: today's
  `Console` control flow, generic over the host. The order of every check and side effect is
  kept (for example Escape: the host's drag, the console's drag, the console's history search
  and completion list, the loading dialog and quit box, the top window, the base).

hl2-ui keeps everything GameUI: `Mode`, the base, menus, backgrounds and chapters, the quit box
and its placement, the Options lifecycle and its sub-dialog and confirmation windows, loading,
commands, `Config`, saving placements, themes and fonts; `Console` implements `Host` and its
public API (`input`, `escape_pressed`, `toggle`, `log`...) calls the toolkit functions.

Commits, each with `scripts/checks/validate.sh`, the Windows-target check and the console and UI
replays; the full replay suite against the baseline and the owned-install tests after the last:

- 4a: `WindowId`, generic `Windows<W>`, `Window` back to hl2-ui (the toolkit's window tests get
  a local window enum).
- 4b: `Shell<W>`: the window stack, console dialog, Escape hold, key repeat and pointer move
  from `Console` into `console.shell`.
- 4c: input routing over `Host` (`shell::input`).
- 4d: Escape, the console toggle and closing windows over `Host`.

## Architecture review: structure without behaviour change (2026-10-23)

Code review findings, worked through in order, one reviewable commit each. None changes console
output, errors, effects, UI behaviour or gameplay outcomes.

1. Console commands are described once: each `console::commands::Spec` carries its handler (and a
   cvar its value), the handlers live in `console/commands/` by area, `Console::interpret` only
   dispatches.
2. Entity inputs dispatch through a class-to-handler table instead of class-name comparisons in
   `hl2-simulation::entities::Scene::deliver`, starting with the door family, triggers and
   `logic_*`; the order of the special cases (monitors, `env_global`, `npc_template_maker`, AI
   inputs) is kept.
3. `hl2-ui` stops depending on `hl2-simulation`, so the UI crate no longer pulls in Rapier and
   Symphonia. Besides `player_damage::{ScreenFade, DamageMessage}` (and the damage/fade bits),
   `gameplay::definitions` and `viewmodel_effects::VIEWMODEL_FOV`, the HUD reads
   `gameplay::{Inventory, Weapon}` and the `selection` module. These move verbatim into a new
   `hl2-shared` crate (a new crate rather than modkit-core, which stays at glam/serde and
   mod-neutral; weapon script parsing needs source-assets and anyhow). The HUD and selection read
   the inventory through `hl2_shared::inventory::InventoryView` (`&dyn`), which the simulation's
   `Inventory` implements; `Inventory` keeps its fields (about 200 accesses), and hl2-simulation
   re-exports everything at the old paths.
4. Oversized files are split: test modules first, then `console.rs` by concern (windows/focus,
   menu flow, the Options lifecycle), and `entities.rs` / `ai/runtime.rs` if step 2 leaves them
   large. Done as whole-method moves into child modules (`impl` blocks per concern; private
   methods become `pub(super)`): `console/{focus,menu_flow,loading,options_dialog,panel}.rs`,
   `entities/{choreography,parenting,io,sequences,triggers}.rs` and
   `ai/runtime/{goal_inputs,equipment,squads,speech,requests}.rs`. Long functions stay as they
   are (`AiRuntime::think` 1,225 lines, `tick_choreography` 376, `Console::draw` 368); splitting
   them changes code, not just its place.

Validation per step: `scripts/checks/validate.sh`, the Windows-target check, the packaged console
and UI replays (steps 1 and 4) or the full replay suite with every gameplay outcome unchanged
(steps 2-4).

## Portrait viewmodels and Fit Display handoff (2026-10-22)

Portrait framing remains data-driven in `assets/ui/tuning.json`: `fov_offset` adjusts horizontal
viewmodel FOV in degrees (at the 4:3 reference aspect), while `offset` moves the camera in Source
units (`[forward, left, up]`). The viewmodel transform keeps unit scale, so the model's proportions
remain intact. Aspect interpolation blends these explicit FOV and position values from the
unmodified 5:4 boundary to the portrait profile. Portrait values have been restored to the `97fac81`
baseline: default/crowbar `+8°`, physcannon `0°`, and RPG `+16°` with a 4-unit left shift. These
remain direct tuning values; packaged portrait appearance has not been visually verified.

Fit Display is first in the Aspect Ratio combo. Selecting it adopts the active physical
window/display content area (with out-of-range letter/pillarbox stripes removed) as its configured
detail resolution; the host also raises a stale fixed configured size to the active physical area
when applying Fit Display. This prevents a previous 640x480 4:3 choice from silently continuing as
a low-resolution flexible frame on a high-DPI panel. Validation is unit-level: aspect selection,
portrait/launcher bounds, and the 640x480-to-Fit-Display host transition.
Packaged visual comparison on an actual portrait display remains not tested.

## Viewmodel tuning overlay (2026-10-22)

`viewmodel_debug_mode 1|0` (console) toggles a runtime-only viewmodel tuning mode, only while a gameplay map is loaded; a map load, a return to the main menu or a background level ends it. F5 and F6 are not bound. Its debug overlay reports the current aspect
profile, active weapon classname, and direct profile `fov_offset` / `[forward, left, up]` offsets.
`[`/`]` adjust FOV, `,`/`.` adjust forward X, Up/Down adjust Z, and Left/Right adjust lateral Y;
Shift changes each step from 0.1 to 1.0 and Alt/Option to 0.01. Adjust keys autorepeat while held, using the same delay
and interval as Options > Keyboard list arrows (press once, then repeat after 0.4 s every 0.05 s;
the latest pressed adjust key is the one that repeats). `viewmodel_tweak_reset` (only while the mode is on) zeros `fov_offset` and `offset` for the
selected weapon's current aspect profile and records that as the session edit. The mode only adds the
editor adjustments; normal mouse look, movement, firing, reloading, and weapon selection continue
through the ordinary gameplay input path. F1 and the tweak overlay may be shown together.

The tuning asset gains zero-valued standard profiles so the same workflow works at ordinary aspect
ratios as well as portrait/ultrawide; their defaults preserve existing standard-aspect rendering.
Each adjustment updates the live camera and viewmodel-attached effects, and emits a complete,
pretty-printed `tuning.json` snapshot to the developer console log with the selected profile value
replaced. It never writes the file. Unit validation covers profile selection/blending, snapshot JSON,
toggle state, and adjustment steps/signs. Packaged input capture and visual presentation remain
runtime checks.

Session editing extension: keep direct edits in memory by `(weapon classname, aspect profile)` for
the lifetime of one tweak-mode session. Before switching to another weapon/profile, retain the current
framing; when revisiting a key, restore its pending edit, otherwise load the asset baseline. Number
keys 1-6 run the normal slot selection/confirmation path while the tuner is active. The session
survives Pause/Options so the owner can change resolution/aspect; while paused, input belongs to the
UI and tuning resumes after returning to gameplay. Each adjustment prints a full tuning snapshot
with every session edit merged, so the accumulated result can be copied once. Turning the mode off resets the
entire session. Validation covers switching/restoring independent keys, accumulated serialization,
and session reset; hold-repeat and reset are unit-tested. Packaged interaction remains a
separate check.

Gameplay-input correction: tweak mode remains a tuning display, not a gameplay input mode. Keep the existing
target-sync/edit/snapshot path, then continue through normal input sampling so mouse look, movement,
attacks, reload, and configured weapon selection continue unchanged. Remove the separate slot
shortcut to avoid duplicating the normal action path. Validate with the host input and gameplay tests
plus the full strict check suite; packaged firing/reload animation observation remains needed.

Console output correction: mark live `tuning.json` snapshots as replaceable console output. On each
adjustment, remove the previous tuning snapshot and append the latest one after intervening output.
Remap cached wrapping and selection indices while preserving scrolled-back ordinary content and
submitted command history. Validate that snapshots occupy one entry at the end while other output remains.

## Closed captions (2026-10-17)

Retail model (SDK `hud_closecaption.cpp`, `SoundEmitterSystem.cpp`, `sceneentity.cpp`; installed files): every `resource/closecaption_<language>.dat` (`VCCD` v1: header, directory of `{crc32(lower(token)), block, offset, length}`, UCS-2 text blocks) on the game search path is a dictionary, the first one holding a token wins. Ordinary `EmitSound` of a sound script captions the script name for the chosen wave's length unless every sound origin is beyond 1200 units of the player; raw `.wav`/`!sentence` emits and client soundscapes do not caption; `ambient_generic` plays caption at any distance. Choreographed SPEAK events caption only their master event's token (cc token, else the sound name), for the longer of the event length and the last slave's end, never "from player", after a 1200-unit `scene_maxcaptionradius` check against the player and view entity unless the event suppresses attenuation; their sound itself emits no caption. The panel (`HudLayout.res` `HudCloseCaption`, `ClientScheme.res` `CloseCaption_*` fonts and `Panel.BgColor` with `BgAlpha`) runs the SDK item queue: `<delay>` splitting, `<len>`, `<sfx>` (instant; dropped by `cc_subtitles`), `<norepeat>`, `<clr>`/`<playerclr>` stacks, `<B>`/`<I>`/`<cr>`, word wrap, linger/predisplay times, box growth and item fades. Options > Audio "Closed captions": none = `closecaption 0`, subtitles and sound effects = `closecaption 1, cc_subtitles 0`, subtitles = `closecaption 1, cc_subtitles 1`.

Plan: `source-assets::captions` decodes the dictionaries at map load (no frame reads; retail streams 8 KB blocks asynchronously). `hl2-simulation` emits `CaptionRequest`s for scene SPEAK masters (`Scene::captions`) and marks the scene's sound request as captionless; the Bevy audio queue emits sound-script captions after a successful emit with the decoded wave length. `hl2-ui::closecaption` owns the pure caption model (parsing, timing, wrap, paint) on scene time, so pause freezes it, and draws on the HUD canvas in the background level too (retail draws captions behind the main menu), before GameUI. Fonts resolve among the scheme's registered installed files; the requested Tahoma/Verdana are not installed, so the scheme's registered DejaVu Sans family (regular, bold, oblique, bold oblique) is used. Validation: unit tests for the parser and model, owned-file tests for the dictionaries and the Kleiner scenes, strict Clippy/format/Windows check and a packaged capture on `ep1_background02`.

## Selected target search and stateful error query (2026-10-22)

Use-target eligibility requires a nonnull candidate, at least one use capability in maskf0 and every requested bit. Direct/ancestor, ground and radius paths request0/100/200 respectively. The search includes a long initial ray, angular hull attempts and a strict adjusted reach<80; radial candidates need cone>=0.8, a strictly improved ranking and fraction1 or the same returned entity. ## Goal and architecture

Reconstruct HL2 in Rust using owned content. No host-game transport or Valve DLL dependency. Native analysis/databases stay private; public source contains original implementation and permitted third-party resources.

Owned VPK/loose/map content -> source-assets bounded decoders -> modkit-core contracts/player/pose math -> hl2-simulation gameplay/choreography/Rapier -> Bevy or retained presentation. source-ui is the game-agnostic UI toolkit (CPU canvas, fonts, widgets, dialog frames, window focus, `.res` layouts, localization engine; see the toolkit split above). hl2-ui, Half-Life 2's UI over it and over hl2-simulation (the HUD reads the simulation's inventory, weapons and selection), records CPU draw commands for both hosts. Setup or asynchronous transitions load resources; frame systems consume prepared data.

AGENTS.md records fixed-step order and renderer invariants. Bevy uses custom materials/lightmaps, separate sky/world/viewmodel/HUD cameras, GPU skinning/current bounds and player/monitor PVS/frustum rejection. Identical material/lightmap pairs share handles. Spatial splitting remains off by default. Replacing Rapier is a separate behavior migration.

## UI, render-frame input, and movement boundary (2026-10-09)

The portable UI crates own UI-facing policy and data: `source-ui` the canvas geometry, viewport fitting and pointer translation and the CPU draw-command interface, `hl2-ui` binding names/defaults and text/menu input interpretation. A host adapts its native events and window/camera API at the edge only; it does not duplicate viewport math or UI-state policy. Bevy input is split by consumer: `ui.rs` handles menu/console focus, pointer/text adaptation, cursor capture, and UI effects; `input.rs` converts the captured keyboard/mouse frame into gameplay actions and a character movement intent; `video.rs` applies the toolkit's viewport bounds to Bevy cameras.

`movement.rs` owns only character state advancement: the Source player/physics controller, walking/flying, eye origin, angles and fixed-tick movement outcome. Script replay, report sampling, gameplay sequencing, console effects, and Bevy camera presentation are hosted in their respective runtime/input/UI/camera modules. Preserve the existing 15 ms order: UI transition/focus first, input sampling second, then scene/weapons/entity refresh/NPCs/physics/player; a UI transition consumes held attacks and jump before character movement. Validate with the existing UI/menu and movement tests plus a scripted packaged console run. This is a structural move only; it must not change binding defaults, cursor/letterbox hit testing, paused-input semantics, or fixed-step behavior.

## GameUI: startup flow, main menu, options (2026-10-11)

Bounded plan and architecture: [gameui.md](gameui.md). `hl2-ui::gameui` owns resource-driven menu/options models (installed `GameMenu.res`, `OptionsSub*.res`) over the toolkit's `.res` layouts, localization with key-name fallback and `kb_act.lst` parsing (`source-ui`), and draws on the toolkit's CPU canvas; `hl2-bevy` only adapts input, display modes (`Monitor::video_modes`) and applies `Config` effects. Without a valid `--map` the host starts in the main menu over the chapter-1 background map. Validation: unit tests per model/parser plus headless Bevy system tests and a packaged capture.

The scene scheduler first handles an eligible LOOP in current event-vector order, then builds and dispatches an ordered pending-event list. Rewind replaces scene time with authored backtime without preserving frame overshoot. Pending dispositions are determined before callbacks; callbacks can change the scene clock seen by later callbacks. The comparator uses earlier start, longer end for equal starts, then conditional raw event-name/channel comparisons; it provides no universal event-ordinal tie-break. Bounded events and end-less crossings differ: skipping an entire bounded speech window in one long update need not start it. Do not replace all event types with one crossed-start queue or claim a universal one-LOOP-callback-per-tick rule.

Forward SPEAK eligibility uses configured sound startup latency and can attach a suppressed speech event to a SECTION. Those dependencies survive while paused updates use PauseThink; Resume starts the copied dependency list through callbacks and clears resume state. A normal Think rebuilds dependency lists. ResetSimulation resets finite LOOP counts; authored LOOP rewind does not. The optional latency resource has an absent-resource fallback of0.1s, which is not proof of the live value. Actual audible repetition, equal-key container equivalence, simultaneous controls and native timing remain **not tested**.

Preserve requested, actor-waiting, interrupt-waiting, playing, manually paused, interrupt-paused, early-completed tail, canceled and finished states separately. Retail Start ignores already-playing/waiting scenes; accepted starts emit `OnStart`. Actor bindings retain generational identity and can be re-resolved when invalid/dead. `FIRETRIGGER` selects the scene's `OnTrigger1..16`, with scene caller and event actor activator when available. `STOPPOINT` emits `OnCompletion` once while the authored tail can continue; final cleanup releases ownership separately. Cancel emits `OnCanceled` and suppresses normal completion. Preserve ordered outputs, caller/activator, delays and remaining fire counts through reentrant dispatch. Verified native contracts are in `lifecycle/CONTRACT.md`; its sealed type-number typo is corrected by `event-control/ERRATA.md`: LOOP is 12, STOPPOINT is 14.

LOOP rewinds to authored backtime and maintains a per-controller remaining counter (`-1` is unbounded). It is not a scene restart or nominal-duration completion. A non-automated SECTION freezes the scene clock while resume-condition callbacks and actor movement continue; the callback list combines completion with AND, and an empty list succeeds. Manual pause and interruption have separate resume gates. PERMIT_RESPONSES grants an actor deadline on global game time and admits responses only if they fit before it; it does not itself choose dialogue. See `event-control/CONTRACT.md`. Exact LOOP speech/output replay across rewind remains unresolved: the pinned SDK's extra loop-start speech-suppression state was not established by the reviewed retail setter. No SUBSCENE or INTERRUPT event instances occur in the parsed cache; generated scenes and busyactor interruption remain separate scope.

First-map LOOP replay followup: retain a processing flag per event across rewinds. Native Start sets processing before its callback; End calls its callback before clearing processing. At a backtime inside an already-processing event's inclusive window, that event continues; a stopped event can start on a later forward crossing. Weapon-point controllers 392/393 rewind 4.213334 -> 3.1: enabled FACE/LOOKAT overlap that backtime, while earlier MOVETO/SECTION do not restart from zero. ### First map handoff and acceptance

`first-map/dossier.json` covers every `d1_trainstation_01` controller: 44 scene instances referencing 40 distinct cache scenes/605 events, 18 cinematic sequences, four AI schedules, 19 template/maker entities and 740 output connections. Work through authored new-game intro/arrival, ambient/customs paths, pen/escort, doorway/reveal, monitor conversation/storage exit, then changelevel to `d1_trainstation_02`. This organization follows authored dependencies; actual branch reachability and ordinary chronology still need native traces.

For the pen chain, trigger 358 spawns the cop template, then authored delays start the scenes and unlock/open Barney's first door. `security_pen_01` emits Trigger1 at about 3.586667 scene seconds, routes to its own Pause and enables the next player trigger; that trigger delivers Resume. The doorway chain starts `security_intro_01`, its doorpound sequence and the Kleiner template; entering the room starts `security_intro_02`, which cancels the looping predecessor and completes into `security_01`. The helmet-reveal model events switch its authored props. `security_01 -> security_02 -> security_03` is output-driven. In security03, SECTION 6.880634 precedes Trigger3 7.013968; Trigger3 unlocks the storage door and opens it after the authored 0.1 delay. STOPPOINT 9.593970 completes early while the tail remains. Do not unlock on SECTION/manual Resume or infer ownership release from completion. `security_04` starts through its own downstream conditions.

The first map needs three unique LOOP records (four controller instances), 12 SECTIONs and one PERMIT_RESPONSES event, plus ordinary movement/speech/pose/layer work. An empty active target slot in `security_intro01b` is retained without inventing an actor. First-map Breen welcome contains no cache LOOP, so repeated broadcast behavior must come from the entity chain. Before advancing detailed research to the second map, finish the first-map path/actor-resolution and event-replay gaps and retain a clear implementation/replay checklist. Validate accepted/blocked starts, actor identity, game/scene clocks, movement arrival, event crossings, caller/activator, output order and cleanup in equivalent unpaused native and packaged Rust traces. Historical seeded/teleport fixtures do not establish current-build or ordinary campaign completion. Native replay, ABI, ordinary campaign and Rust parity are **not tested** by this research pass.

Resource-path finding retained for the later map backlog: retail normalizes `.vcd` extension and slashes before cache lookup. Use one shared normalization policy when implementing the loader, with a fixture. `scenes/citadel/al_weak.vcd` and `scenes/npc/generic_reliable_walk.vcd` are absent from the inspected stock cache/five stock VPKs/exact loose paths; keep them explicit, without inventing replacements or assuming those nodes execute. See `scene-path/README.md`.

## Active plan: authored NPC gestures with on-demand decompilation

Research order (owner, 2026-10-07): for each system, first read the official Source SDK 2013 code (full pinned clone b8cfb12, private work/references/source-sdk-2013), then retrieve the installed binaries' functions from the private index for engine internals the SDK lacks and retail differences, then implement original Rust and compare with the original game. Leaked engine source is not used. Owner decision 2026-10-06: resume the Rust rewrite now. Whole-corpus coverage passes stop after pass14; the private index (133,473 addresses with pseudocode, about 94% of baseline executable bytes inside identified functions, zero reviewed names/types) is sufficient for targeted retrieval. For each step below, first identify the responsible retail functions (for example server.dll/client.dll animation-layer, sequence and choreography code) through strings, RTTI, callers and pinned SDK correspondence; retrieve exact bodies by installed path/hash/address; record reviewed findings privately with SDK-versus-retail distinctions. Only then implement in shared crates. Native code stays private; Rust contains original implementation.

6. Next bounded step: facial flexes for scene FlexAnimation tracks. (a) source-assets reads flex descriptors, flex controllers (type, name, range), flex rules (RPN ops) and per-mesh flexes (target0..3 ramp, flexpair, vertex position/normal deltas; float16 vs converted fixed-point by header flag), bounded and validated against owned Barney/Kleiner models. (b) shared code evaluates scene flex tracks into controller values (with the existing compiled-ramp curve), runs flex rules into descriptor weights, and turns them into per-flex weights with the target ramp and left/right pairs. (c) renderers apply vertex deltas on the CPU into the skinned bind vertices, or through Bevy morph targets if the custom material path supports them; the retained host shares the CPU path. Validate with synthetic rule/ramp tests, owned model parsing, and packaged security captures compared with native frames at matching scene times. Lip sync (phonemes) and eye/eyelid flexes are a later step. Status 2026-10-06: (a) done (source-assets::flexes, Surface::flex_source); (b) done (Scene::flex_controllers from scene tracks). (c) design: hl2-bevy rendering batches actor surfaces per (entity, material, lightmap) into per-entity meshes (AnimatedMesh with `bind` skin data), so deformation can be per entity. Carry each batched vertex's (flex_source mesh key, mesh-local vertex id) through `append` into AnimatedMesh; load each actor model's FlexModel at setup (no frame IO); each frame, for actors with flex_controllers, map controller values by name into model controller order, run rules, compute per-flex weights and VertAnim weights, and write bind position (+ normal) deltas into the mesh's position/normal attributes before GPU skinning (only when the flex signature changes). Retained viewer applies the same deltas before `skin()`. Validate: owned Barney security_02 capture where the face visibly changes versus a no-flex capture, pixel-diff confined to the head; native comparison. Status: (c) done in Bevy; FACS eyelids added after native close-ups showed half-closed lids (retail StudioRender 1001bd80, reviewed privately). The retained host follows next with the shared `FaceModel` path.

8. Next bounded step (owner report 2026-10-06: entities lack the map's lighting and shadows): Source model lighting. Retrieve and review the retail engine/studiorender lighting path first (leaf ambient sampling, world light selection, VertexLitGeneric diffuse terms). (a) source-assets: leaf ambient lighting (HDR lumps 55 with index 51, or LDR 56 with index 52: CompressedLightCube samples with leaf-local positions), world lights (lump 54 HDR / 15 LDR dworldlight_t: type, color, attenuation, cone, style), and VVD vertex normals on model surfaces. (b) shared code: per-entity illumination origin (or $illumposition), ambient cube from the leaf samples nearest that origin, and the strongest few local lights with Source attenuation; computed in simulation/presentation prep, not by frame IO. (c) Bevy: skinned normals (GPU and CPU paths), shader ambient cube (n squared weighting) plus diffuse lights, with half-Lambert where the material sets $halflambert; retained host gets the vertex-lit equivalent. (d) Validate with Barney close-ups versus native luminance at matching times, a static-prop and an NPC elsewhere in the map, and the regression batch. Render-to-texture shadows (shadow_control direction/color/distance) are a later, separate step.

8a. Retail review done 2026-10-07 (private lighting-20261007/README.md, engine.dll). Default r_radiosity 4: a non-static-prop entity's ambient cube comes from Mod_LeafAmbientColorAtPos (samples of its leaf, weight 1/(d^2+1), linear RGBExp32). World lights flagged DWL_FLAGS_INAMBIENTCUBE are skipped when that leaf ambient is used. Other lights get ratio = falloff x lightstyle x visibility, where visibility is a world-only trace that tolerates up to 8 units of blockage. A light is kept as one of r_worldlights=4 local lights when it is a surface light or its luminance x ratio >= r_worldlightmin (0.0002); the weakest kept light is evicted. Lights that are not kept are added to the cube with per-axis max(dot, 0) weighting. At draw time, models flagged with STUDIOHDR_FLAGS_AMBIENT_BOOST get the boost: if the cube average is below 0.3 and below 0.1 x direct luminance, the cube is scaled by min(5, 0.1 x direct / max face). Light-to-shader conversion: surface lights become spots with 1/d^2, point lights stay points, spots keep their cone and exponent, the skylight becomes directional, and quake/skyambient lights are dropped. The shader follows SDK common_vs_fxc.h.
Implementation (bounded, reversible):
(1) modkit-core `lighting`: serializable `LightingData` with a compact BSP tree (planes, nodes, leaf contents/flags/bounds), leaf ambient samples, world lights and their style. It provides leaf lookup, a solid-leaf segment trace, `state_at(origin)` (retail selection), `boost`, and `vertex_light(n, p, half_lambert)`, which evaluates on the CPU exactly as the shader does. Unit tests use synthetic data.
(2) source-assets: parse lumps 10/1/5 plus the LDR ambient (52/56) or HDR ambient (51/55) and world lights (15/54), matching the lightmap choice. Read VVD normals into `Vertex.normal` and the studio illumposition/flags. Static props get per-vertex colors at load from their origin's state, which approximates retail per-vertex static-prop lighting (the retail 162-sample/vhv path is not reviewed). Owned-file tests run on security/trainstation maps.
(3) Bevy: real mesh normals (GPU skinning skins them, and CPU skinning transforms them), a per-entity `lighting` uniform on model materials (ambient cube plus 4 lights), recomputed when the illumination origin moves more than 1 unit. The shader evaluates ambient + diffuse (+ half-Lambert for $halflambert) on linearized base texture. World/lightmapped surfaces are unchanged. Retained: static-prop colors only, with dynamic models unchanged (**not** lit yet).
Validate: unit tests, owned-map parse counts, packaged Barney close-ups vs native (oracle ORACLE_CLOSEUP), the regression batch. Known approximations: styled lights are treated as on, the skylight uses leaf sky flags instead of a surface-flag trace, flex normal deltas are ignored, and HDR tonemapping is not modeled.

9. Session 5 (2026-10-07): trainstation_02 jumbotron fidelity, bounded and reversible. Evidence: the slate func_brush *87 has `_minlight 255`, which SDK VRAD (radial.cpp:676/813) bakes into its lightmap as a per-luxel floor of `_minlight*128`; it is not a runtime brightness. Our LDR atlas stores gamma(linear) clamped at 1.0, whereas Source LDR lightmaps keep 2x headroom (imaterialsystem.h OVERBRIGHT 2.0; LightmappedGeneric multiplies by the lightmap scale factor), so saturated faces render as plain base texture. tools/toolsblack is an UnlitGeneric black texture (owned VMT), so it renders.
(a) source-assets::bsp stops skipping tools/toolsblack* faces (other tools/ materials stay skipped); (b) lightmap atlas stores gamma(linear/2) and lightmapped materials scale by 2^(1/2.2) before the gamma decode, unchanged for linear <= 1 apart from one bit of precision; (c) UnlitTwoTexture/monitor look is reviewed separately. Validate: unit tests, an early-tick slate fixture against native view-d1_trainstation_02-20261007T054643Z/slate.png, and the regression batch (world brightness changes are expected only where baked light exceeds 1.0). Known gap: native runs the HDR path (HDR lightmaps + auto exposure tonemap), which remains unimplemented.

10. Session 5 (2026-10-07): Source HDR lighting and auto exposure. Evidence: native HL2 runs the HDR path (sky_day01_01_hdr). With the map's HDR lumps our captures are darker everywhere (hall wall 106 vs native 141, plaza street 100 vs 136, both ~1.9-2.0x linear), and trainstation's logic_auto sets env_tonemap_controller SetAutoExposureMin .5 / Max 2 / SetTonemapRate .35 (SDK defaults mat_autoexposure_min/max 0.5/2). LightmappedGeneric, VertexLitGeneric/UnlitGeneric, UnlitTwoTexture and Sky all end in FinalOutput(..., TONEMAP_SCALE_LINEAR), i.e. one global exposure multiplier before the clamped sRGB write. The owner's adapted native screenshot shows the Combine slate saturated (its luxels are linear 128), which needs unclamped lightmaps. Plan, reversible per step:
(a) source-assets: when HDR lighting exists (lumps 53/58), the atlas holds linear unclamped RGB (RGBA16F) from the HDR lump, and model lighting reads the HDR ambient/world-light lumps; LDR-only maps use the LDR lumps linearly. (b) Bevy: lightmapped output = lin(base) x lightmap; all Source material outputs multiply by the camera's exposure (Bevy view.exposure, set from the tonemap scale on the main, monitor and viewmodel cameras); the LDR sky textures keep their current scale until HDR (RGBS) sky decoding is added, because they already match native. (c) Auto exposure per SDK viewpostprocess.cpp (new algorithm): a 17-bin luminance histogram of the presented frame (bins (i/16)^1.5), target = 0.60 / location of the brightest 2% (sticky bin), raised to reach 3% average luminance, times the current scale, clamped to the controller's min/max, moving-average goal; approach rate from the controller's tonemap rate (retail adaptation code reviewed before implementing). The histogram comes from periodic downsampled frame readbacks. `--tonemap-scale` forces a scale (mat_force_tonemap_scale) for deterministic fixtures.
Validate: unit tests (histogram target math, HDR atlas decode), packaged slate/broadcast/plaza/hall captures against native (adapted), regression batch with forced scale, FPS check of the readback. Not covered: bloom (SetBloomScale .4), env_tonemap_controller blending.
Status 2026-10-07 (wip/hdr-path, merged after validation): (a)-(c) implemented plus RGBS HDR sky faces and VMT conditionals in HDR mode. Corrections from evidence: the owner's real game runs mat_hdr_level 2 (Steam Cloud ROOT_MOD config) while the oracle's private config was LDR, so earlier "native" captures were LDR; the oracle now passes +mat_hdr_level 2. (1) source-assets::vtf `decode_cube`: the six faces of frame 0 at the chosen mip (the 7.4 spheremap face is skipped), RGBA8 for LDR formats and raw half floats for format 24, with synthetic tests and an owned trainstation_02 check.
(2) source-assets::bsp: world vertices carry their face plane normal (displacements use the base face normal, an approximation).
(3) hl2-bevy: MaterialData reads $envmap (HDR mode prefers the .hdr.vtf variant; env_cubemap left unresolved falls back to no envmap), $envmaptint, $envmapcontrast, $envmapsaturation, $fresnelreflection and $basealphaenvmapmask. SourceMaterial binds a cube texture (a black fallback cube otherwise) and an envmap uniform. The lightmapped branch adds spec = min(cube, 16) x mask x tint, then contrast, saturation and the Fresnel term, before exposure. $envmapmask textures, bumped envmaps and model (VertexLitGeneric) envmaps come later.
Validate: unit tests, a packaged d1_trainstation_02 capture of the left windows against the native HDR capture (target ~(159,150,118) from ~(70,60,38)), the regression batch. Status 2026-10-08: (1)-(3) implemented. Brush face normals take the plane normal oriented by the summed winding (the stored plane already faces the rendered side for both `side` values in owned maps). Validated outside the window002c wall (native view-d1_trainstation_02-20261007T231545Z/winout): panes within 3-5 levels of native, with matching reflection layout. The left-window target was wrong: those panes are the trainstation_arch001 prop texture (static-prop lighting), and the upper hall windows are $selfillum window002e. Added after owner review: SDK masks ($normalmapalphaenvmapmask, $envmapmask, opaque-base clearing of $basealphaenvmapmask) and bump normals in a derivative tangent frame (SDK GetBumpNormals s/t orientation); the main hall tile floor is within 1-2 levels of native. $selfillum followed (SDK base-alpha blend); it, not static-prop lighting, explained the dark hall windows.

11b plan (session 6, 2026-10-08). Evidence: SDK vbsp overlay.cpp (record layout, V-flip bit), SDK collisionutils.cpp (PointInQuadToBarycentric / TexCoordInQuadFromBarycentric) and retail engine.dll COverlayMgr (private overlay-20261008/README.md: 10149db0, 10147b70, 1014c970, 1014d230, 1014c700, 1014cbd0, 1014a6c0). Steps:
(1) source-assets: per overlay, V = normalize(N x U), negated when the flip bit is set; quad corners origin + uv.x U + uv.y V with texcoords (U0,V0), (U0,V1), (U1,V1), (U1,V0). For each listed brush face, fan triangles (skip area <= 1), flatten each onto the overlay plane, clip the quad against the triangle's edges, give every clipped vertex bilinear texcoords from its inverse-bilinear position in the quad, project it onto the face plane along N, add the face's lightmap coordinates and push it 0.1 units along the face normal. Displacement faces (engine displacement-space path) and fade distances are left for later.
(2) hl2-bevy: overlay surfaces join the world draw with the face's lightmap page and the overlay material (texinfo texdata name).
Validate: synthetic tests (flat quad on one face, clipped quad across two faces, flipped V), an owned trainstation_02 count with all vertices on their faces, packaged plaza captures of the leaves against native, the regression batch. Status 2026-10-08: brush-face path done and validated against native ivy/stain captures; no trainstation_02 overlay is a leaf (the leaves belong to another system).

12 plan (session 8, 2026-10-08): positional ambient audio, then soundscapes. Owner reports: a loud constant hum everywhere on d1_trainstation_02 (not in native); the Combine shield hum should loop near the barrier and fade with distance instead of cutting off; the background should change between the spawn maintenance room, the station and outside. Cause: every looping ambient_generic starts at load as a non-positional sound at a fixed volume (health/10 x 0.3), and env_soundscape is not implemented. Evidence: SDK sound.cpp/SoundEmitterSystem.cpp/SoundParametersInternal.cpp/soundflags.h and retail engine GetDistGainFromSoundLevel (10216bd0 -> 10216a20), recorded privately in positional-audio-20261008/README.md. Steps, each reversible:
(a) hl2-simulation::sounds: parse script volume/pitch/soundlevel (names, ATTN_*, intervals) into the Library; `dist_gain(soundlevel, distance)` per the retail function with default convars. Tests: SNDLVL_NONE, the soft knee at gain 0.5, the 0.01 floor ramp, an owned script.
(b) SoundRequest gains an optional world origin and optional volume/pitch/soundlevel overrides. ambient_generic follows the SDK: looping and not start-silent at spawn; raw-wave radius -> soundlevel, script soundlevel otherwise; volume health/10 (missing health is silent); pitch keyvalue; SourceEntityName origin. PlaySound/StopSound/ToggleSound use the same parameters. Script cue volume and pitch replace the fixed 0.4 cue policy.
(c) Bevy: each positional player's sink volume = volume x global volume x dist_gain(soundlevel, |listener - origin|), refreshed every frame from the player eye. Pitch maps to playback speed. Paused players stay paused.
(d) Then env_soundscape (SDK client soundscape.cpp, server soundscape.cpp; scripts/soundscapes_*.txt): active soundscape by radius/visibility, playlooping/playrandom/playsoundscape, crossfade.
Validate: unit tests; packaged per-process recordings walking from the gate shield outward (gain falls continuously, no cut), the spawn room vs plaza without the hum, native per-process recordings at the same places; the regression batch. Stereo panning, DSP/room effects, dynamic presets (spin/fade/LFO) and occlusion stay out of this step.
Status 2026-10-08: (a)-(c) implemented for ambient_generic spawn loops; game cue requests keep the fixed 0.4 volume and no origin. Packaged report gains at five plaza distances fall continuously; per-process native recording and stereo panning are not done. (d) env_soundscape is next.
12d plan (session 9, 2026-10-08), on `wip/soundscapes-20261008`. Evidence: SDK server soundscape.cpp/soundscape_system.cpp and client c_soundscape.cpp, owned scripts/soundscapes_*.txt (private soundscapes-20261008/README.md). trainstation_02 has 12 env_soundscape; the spawn is inside Interrogation (#940), the hall in Turnstyle (#910/#912), outside in TerminalSquare (#913/#944). Steps, each reversible:
(1) hl2-simulation::soundscapes `Definitions`: manifest files in order, then `soundscapes_<map>.txt` if unlisted; top-level sections by case-insensitive name. `Selector` (server rules): env_soundscape and env_soundscape_proxy candidates in entity order; every fixed tick after player movement, re-check the current one, then others: a candidate takes over when enabled, within radius (or -1) and a zero-size brush trace (contents 0x403b) from its origin to the ear is clear and not start-solid, and either the current one is out of range/sight or the candidate is closer. A disabled current only drops from the comparison; playback continues. Taking over writes params (entity, soundscape name, positions 0-7 from named entity origins) and fires OnPlay. Enable/Disable/ToggleEnabled use the entity enabled state (StartDisabled).
(2) `Playback` (client rules): restart on (entity, soundscape) change; old loops fade to 0, a generation counter lets same wave+pitch ambient loops (or positional at the same place) keep playing and just retarget; new ambient loops start at 0, positional at 0.05; volume approaches the target at dt/3 per second (soundscape_fadetime 3). playrandom: first play at now + 0.5 x time draw, then every time draw; uniform wave, volume/pitch/soundlevel draws; `position random` = eye + 36 (cos a right + sin a forward). playsoundscape: master volume, position offsets and overrides, depth limit 8; positions without a resolved entity are suppressed. Output is a list of start/volume/stop/one-shot commands. Deterministic xorshift draws.
(3) Bevy: preload every wave reachable from the map's soundscapes; apply commands to looping/one-shot sinks; positional ones use dist_gain from the eye; pitch is playback speed; scene time and its delta drive fades and random timing, so pause freezes them.
Validate: unit tests for selection (range, LOS, closer takeover, disabled current, proxy), script expansion (suppressed positions, overrides, reuse) and fades; owned test that trainstation_02's scripts resolve and the waves exist; packaged walk from the spawn through the hall to the plaza with --audio-trace and per-process recording, compared with native per-process recordings at the same places; the regression batch. DSP, dsp_volume, soundmixer, stereo panning, trigger_soundscape and level-transition carry-over stay out of this step.

13e (planned, session 14): breakable props (owner item 0a). SDK chain (props.cpp/props_shared.cpp; retail order corroborated by physics-research-20261013/prop-launch-breakage, in progress): prop health from prop_data (done) -> Event_Killed -> CBreakableProp::Break -> PhysBreakSound (the surfaceprop's break sound) -> PropBreakableCreateAll: gibs from the model's .phy `break` blocks (model, health, fade, ragdoll flag) placed in the parent's model space at its pose, inheriting its linear/angular velocity, plus propdata `breakable_model` chunk sets (random chunk models up to the count); gib limits (`breakable_disable_gib_limit`, props_break_max_pieces). Steps: (0) prop impact damage: CPhysicsProp::VPhysicsCollision -> CalculateDefaultPhysicsDamage with the prop table and m_impactEnergyScale, x10 on the first collision after a gravity-gun launch not thrown by the player (SF_PHYSPROP_DONT_TAKE_PHYSICS_DAMAGE respected); (1) source-assets parses .phy break blocks and propdata chunk sets; at map load, preload gib model assets/physics for every breakable prop (no asset reads in frame systems); (2) the simulation removes the broken prop, plays the break sound and spawns gibs as free bodies (the frag's runtime body path) with a fade timer, reported for rendering; (3) the host draws them like projectile models; (4) damage-type modifiers (propdata) after. Status (session 14): step 0 done (prop impact damage, launch bonus); step 1 started: .phy break blocks parsed (`phy::break_models`, World::model_breaks) and gib physics preloaded at map load (cardboard gibs with non-identity PHY roots skipped). Status (session 15): steps 2-3 done for non-ragdoll gibs: `gibs.rs` spawns each .phy break block as a runtime prop body (`Physics::add_prop_body`: ordinary prop collisions, margin, stored drag) at the parent pose plus its offset, with the parent velocity at that point, its angular velocity and the 100 u/s burst (block "burst" overrides), entity ids in the projectile range so +USE/the gravity gun can hold them; SUB_FadeOut after fadetime (int(256 x tick) alpha per tick, only outside the player 2D view cone 0.766 and unheld; removed at 0); PerformanceMode 1 = no gibs; the host draws them from the preloaded gib meshes with a per-gib material (light state at the gib, alpha blend while fading). Not done: ragdoll gibs (cardboard boxes break into 6-solid ragdoll gibs: needs constraints/ragdolls, owner item c), parent skin, placementOrigin attachments, propdata chunk sets, gib PVS culling, gibs as real entities (move onto DESIGN 14r runtime entities when it lands; owner 2026-10-13: native gibs are new prop_physics entities created at the break, models precached). Plan (session 16, steps 4-5): (4) source-assets resolves the whole prop_data block over its base chain (CPropData::ParsePropFromKV: dmg.bullets/club/explosive default 1, damage_table, breakable_model/count/skin, health) into `World::model_prop_data`, and the BreakableModels chunk sets into `World::breakable_chunks`; entity damage carries its DMG_* type and CBreakableProp takes GetBreakableDamage (bullet x mod, buckshot x 2 mod, club, blast; time-based 0); player bullets and melee on breakable props go through that same entity damage path (prop_data health, break sound, gibs; before, only props with an explicit health keyvalue broke under fire); props naming damage_table "glass" use the glass impact table (retail registry = SDK, physics-research-20261011 SI-25, nonmonotonic order kept). (5) Gibs: the parent's skin (BreakModelCreateSingle: skin > the gib's skin families -> 0), the break block's collision group (fadetime without debris -> COLLISION_GROUP_DEBRIS), and propdata chunk sets for breakable props without .phy gibs (PropBreakableCreateAll else-branch: breakable_count chunks, GetRandomChunkModel up to the max size floor(OBB area of the two largest axes / 1024), random point on the mid plane of the smallest OBB axis, 100 u/s away from the parent origin, no inherited motion, AlignBoxes on the parent's angles, breakable_skin, fade 5-10 s, debris). Validation: owned propdata parse tests (Wooden.Large pallet: WoodChunks 6, bullets 0.75; glass bottle: damage_table glass), unit tests (modifiers, glass table, chunk placement/alignment), packaged: pistol shots break a crate (none before), furniture chunk gibs on tr03 (the tr02 tic-tac-toe blocks override health 0: unbreakable). Status (session 16): steps 4-5 done. `World::model_prop_data`/`breakable_chunks`, typed `projectiles::Damage::kind`, `gameplay::breakable_damage` (+ func_breakable convar defaults), player bullets/melee on breakables through the entity damage path, `impact_damage::{ImpactTable, GLASS_TABLE, table_named}`, gib skin/debris group/chunk sets in `gibs.rs`, host preloads gib skins and used chunk models. Found and fixed on the way (physics, SDK-backed): displacement collision was inside-out (BSP clockwise winding in Rapier's oriented trimesh: props fell through displacements; terrain colliders now reversed, without internal-edge correction, which made a resting pot wander and tip); props collided with player/NPC clip-only brushes (VBSP separate clip solids, CCollisionEvent::ShouldCollide contents vs MASK_SOLID: now filtered for contacts, and shot/use traces skip them); logic_collision_pair startdisabled; bullet/melee push = VPhysicsTakeDamage ApplyForceOffset with the ammo's BULLET_IMPULSE / melee damage x 300 kg in/s at the hit point (was a fixed 1/4 kg m/s at the center). Validated: owned propdata test, unit tests, packaged pistol vs bottle #712 (skin-4 chunks, native T232957Z same outcome) and teeter seat (8 shots -> 4 WoodChunks; native T235049Z broke within 5 shots after rocking: the push/rocking still differs), pot #491 rests upright like native (T231808Z). Not done: ragdoll gibs, gib PVS, gib limit/props_break_max_pieces, explosive props (explosive_damage), fire interactions, damage filters. Validation: owned parse tests (wood_crate001a, cardboard_box001a "Cardboard.small", wood_pallet001a "Wooden.Large"), unit tests (break order, inherited velocity), packaged crate broken by a launched pallet vs native (crate shattered on the cop, T015556Z).
13f (planned, session 15): studio decals on NPCs (owner item 0b: bug bait stains, blood, paint). Retail evidence (owned scripts/decals_subrect.txt and VMTs): a hit's game material translates through TranslationData (F -> Impact.Flesh = decals/flesh/blood1-5_subrect, B -> Impact.BloodyFlesh, H -> Impact.AlienFlesh, M/V/G -> Impact.Metal); on models the engine draws the subrect's `$modelmaterial` (decals/flesh/bloodN: DecalModulate, $decalscale 0.1; BeerSplash -> decals/beersplashmodel) at the world decal's size (subrect width x $decalscale). SDK: FX_ImpactTrace -> Impact() -> effects->DecalShoot on the hit entity; studio decal geometry itself is built inside studiorender.dll (not in the SDK; retail evidence on demand). Plan: (1) modkit-core `studio_decals::project`: given the model's posed triangles (current skinning) with bind positions and bone weights, keep triangles facing the shot within the decal box (depth = radius) and clip them to the decal square in its plane, carrying barycentric bind positions, blended top-4 bone weights and planar UVs; unit tests. (2) Simulation: NPC hits queue studio decal requests (entity, point, direction, model material, radius, rotation) instead of being dropped; the bug bait splash too. (3) Host: build each request from the NPC's GPU-skinned draws (main-world mesh data + joint GlobalTransforms x inverse binds), spawn a decal mesh sharing the draw's SkinnedMesh joints (it follows animation), hidden with the entity; per-model cap (oldest removed). Validation: unit tests; packaged shots/bait on a posed cop and an animated plaza cop vs native at the same views. Not covered: TraceBleed world blood behind NPCs, ragdolls, per-vertex lighting of translucent model decals beyond the entity light state. Status (session 15): steps 1-3 done (`modkit_core::studio_decals::{project, ray_distance}`, `Impacts::studio_requests`, `hl2-bevy studio_decals.rs`); the host re-shoots the hit along the shot against the posed triangles (trace shapes sit off the skin), lifts the decal 0.25 bind units, caps 50 per model and hides decals with their entity. Bullet hits carry the shot direction (`Impacts::add_shot`); bug bait uses the reversed hit normal. Status (session 16): TraceBleed done (CBaseEntity::TraceBleed: 1/2/4 traces by damage < 10 / < 25 / more, +/-0.1/0.2/0.3 noise, 172 units along the shot behind the NPC, MASK_SOLID_BRUSHONLY minus grates approximated by the projectile ray ignoring the NPC; UTIL_BloodDecalTrace: Blood for BLOOD_COLOR_RED, YellowBlood otherwise, none for DONT_BLEED/MECH); native T002821Z (npc_create cop shot 3 times in front of the hall wall) shows the same placement, larger splats and a dead cop. Paint and death/ragdoll blood open.
13g (session 15): VPhysics constraints (owner item 0c). SDK physconstraint.cpp: constraints resolve attach1/attach2 by name (missing = world), build in the reference frame, SF 1 disables pair collision, SF 8 makes world hinges IVP BecomeHinged objects. Done: hinge, ballsocket, ragdoll (spherical + limits), fixed as Rapier impulse joints (`Physics::add_constraints`). Rejected after attempts (recorded in MODLOG): rope joints for phys_lengthconstraint (swings flung) and impulse/multibody joints for SF 8 hinges (drift / Rapier panic). Next approach options: ropes as a custom distance limit applied in the hook/step (IVP-like soft limit), SF 8 hinges as kinematic rotation about the axis driven by applied torque; env_physexplosion and phys_thruster inputs; func_physbox dynamic bodies. Status (session 16): SF 8 world hinges done as revolute joints after the SDK mass-center snap (masscenteroverride_t::SnapToAxis): the session-15 drift came from the teeter seat overlapping an NPC-clip brush (DETAIL|MONSTERCLIP, 14 units) and its base (logic_collision_pair startdisabled); both no longer collide, and the seat rests level on its hinge (native aim view T235049Z matches). Carousel (SF 9, friction 50, phys_thruster) not checked; ropes still off. Also session 16: SF_CONSTRAINT_DISABLE_COLLISION on a world constraint disables the entity pair with the world entity (PhysDisableEntityCollisions on g_PhysWorldObject), which owns the BSP and (inferred: server traces report static props as the world entity) the static props: such bodies ignore every static collider without an entity (the tic-tac-toe blocks sat 4.7 units inside the frame's rod and could not turn); env_physexplosion (physobj.cpp CPhysExplosion::Explode: name/class/radius targets, magnitude - 0.4 x distance, CalculateExplosiveDamageForce x RandomFloat(0.85, 1.15) at the explosion origin, NODAMAGE/RADIAL/TEST_LOS; PUSH_PLAYER counted, not modeled) and prop OnPlayerUse (SF_PHYSPROP_ENABLE_PICKUP_OUTPUT 256, CPhysicsProp::Use) make the blocks turn on +USE. Open: native turns block 5 about a quarter turn and it settles within 0.3 s (T001349Z); ours spins at the 3600 deg/s cap for over 6 s (hinge friction units, impulse transfer through the IVP hinge, or angular drag differ: research needed).
11f (planned, renderer): Sprite-shader materials default to `$nosrgb` 1 (SDK sprite_dx9.cpp: no sRGB read/write, FinalOutput TONEMAP_SCALE_GAMMA), so native adds sprites in gamma space into the integer-HDR (RGBA8) framebuffer. Our effects shader linearizes and blends in a linear float target, making additive sprites dimmer. Options to evaluate: draw gamma-space sprites in a separate pass that composites on the presented (gamma) image with scene depth, or an equivalent that reads the destination; validate on the frag trail (native red excess ~100 vs ours ~50 at the plaza view), redglow1, muzzle flashes and the combine ball against native HDR, then the full regression batch.
Validate per weapon: unit tests of state machines and launch math; owned tests of scripts/sequences/events; packaged captures and per-process audio against native HDR at the same places; the regression batch before the single merge.
NPC behavior and AI (step 14, after 13b; owner 2026-10-09): SDK 2013 AI (schedules, tasks, conditions, squads, senses, navigation) and the per-class NPCs, checked against the private pass14/pass15 retail evidence, aiming at HL2 behavior. Campaign behavior follows the real game: e.g. the trainstation metrocops hold their posts and escalate (warnings, shove, stunstick) through their authored/AI logic, not a paraphrase. A developer NPC spawn overlay on F4 (drawn by the Bevy host like the F1 overlay, outside the claimed hl2-ui menus): choose an NPC class and its weapon (additionalequipment) and spawn it armed; console equivalents follow Source's npc_create/npc_create_aimed. Native F-key binds to avoid (owned config_default.cfg): F5 jpeg, F6 save quick, F9 load quick (F8 on macOS); the owner's config.cfg also binds F3 askconnect_accept. Ours: F1 overlay, F2 fly, F3 dev loadout, F10.

14 plan (cloud session 2026-10-10, branch `wip/cloud-npc-ai-20261010` from main; SDK 2013 b8cfb12 only, retail not compared). New module tree `hl2-simulation/src/ai/`, engine-free and unit-tested; the existing npc.rs Controller (routes, doors, locomotion) stays the motor and is reached through a navigator trait the host implements. Increments, each reversible:
(1) `ai::conditions` (COND_* bitset with SDK names, ai_condition.h), `ai::state` (NPC_STATE_* and the SDK SelectIdealState transitions), `ai::memory` (CAI_Enemies: last seen/known positions, first/last seen times, eluded), `ai::relationships` (Classify classes and CHalfLife2::InitDefaultAIRelationships dispositions/priorities for the HL2 classes).
(2) `ai::schedule`: tasks (TASK_* subset used by the default and police schedules) with start/run/complete/fail, schedules with interrupt condition masks (ai_default.cpp definitions for the subset), the CAI_BaseNPC::MaintainSchedule loop (interrupts, task failure -> SCHED_FAIL, TranslateSchedule/SelectSchedule hooks per class via a trait).
(3) `ai::senses`: sight (view cone from m_flFieldOfView, distance, LOS through a host trait over the existing world queries), hearing (CSound types/volumes from soundent.cpp with lifetimes), producing conditions (SEE_ENEMY, HEAR_*, ENEMY_OCCLUDED...).
(4) `ai::squad` (ai_squad.cpp slots, members, squad memory sharing).
(5) Per-class: npc_metropolice (warnings, shove/ACT_PUSH_PLAYER, stunstick escalation, ai_goal_police/ai_behavior_police posts), then npc_citizen, npc_combine_s, as far as the session allows; every behavior marked "SDK behavior, retail not compared".
(6) F4 developer spawn data model (`ai::spawn`: class list, additionalequipment choices, spawn at crosshair) and console commands npc_create / npc_create_aimed with Source semantics; the Bevy F4 overlay drawing is compile-only and outside the claimed hl2-ui menus.
Validate: unit tests per module (condition/state transitions, schedule interrupts and failures in 15 ms ticks, sight cone/LOS via a fake world, sound lifetimes, squad slots, relationship table, npc_create parsing). Local: the trainstation metrocops on posts against native, F4 spawns in packaged runs.

14 status (cloud, 2026-10-10, later): (1)-(5) host-wired on `wip/cloud-npc-ai-20261010` behind `ai_enable`/`--ai` (default off): `ai::host` adapters (SceneMotor over npc.rs with the reserved AI_SCENE goal scene, PhysicsSight, PhysicsPlacement), `ai::runtime` think loop yielding to scripts, `ai::metropolice` with `ai::police` enabled by the SDK input SetPoliceGoal, +USE PrecriminalUse, F4 overlay and npc_create routing. Unit-tested only; npc_citizen/npc_combine_s not started.
Runtime NPC creation (2026-10-12: implemented as a load-time pool instead, `ai::spawn::append_pool`: hidden pooled entities per class built by every load-time system and activated on spawn; bounded count, no new tables. The append-only plan below stays the route to unlimited spawns): World.entities, Scene.states, actor registration, model/clip loading and physics colliders all index the entity list fixed at map load, and model loading is a setup step (no asset reads in frame systems). Bounded plan, each step reversible: (a) preload the F4 class list's models/clips with the map (setup-time, owned files) into a template table; (b) add an append-only `Scene::spawn_entity(world_entity)` that pushes a World entity and a matching Scene state and registers the actor with the npc Controller (ids stay stable; killed spawned NPCs stay as killed slots); (c) Physics::add_npc_collider for the new id with NPC_GROUP; (d) AiRuntime::add_actor; (e) host instancing in hl2-bevy (a new animated actor entity) and save/load stays deferred. Validate with a synthetic-world unit test per step, then a packaged F4 spawn compared with native `npc_create_aimed npc_metropolice` at the same view.

14h plan (session 17, owner report: NPCs stare at the player with their eyes only and forever; native turns the head and glances around). SDK ai_baseactor.cpp MaintainLookTargets / PickLookTarget / PickRandomLookTarget / MakeRandomLookTarget and AI_Interest_Target.cpp (no retail evidence for this path yet; attention-research-2026-10-05 read the same SDK code). Steps, each unit-tested and reversible:
(1) `attention`: interest targets gain a ramp and a position target; `Interest::interest(now)` = CAI_InterestTarget_t::Interest (ramp in via 1 - ExponentialDecay(0.2, ramp, t), out via smoothstep over the last ramp fraction, x importance; ramp stored as ramp/duration). A per-actor random queue and next random look time.
(2) Every 0.1 s NPC think, for actors with bits_CAP_ANIMATEDFACE (npc_metropolice, npc_combine_s, npc_citizen, npc_barney, npc_alyx, npc_breen, npc_eli, npc_kleiner, npc_mossman, npc_monk, npc_dog, generic_actor; not cyclers), whether or not `--ai` is on: explicit (scene LOOKAT/AI AddLookTarget) interests truncate random ones to ramp + 0.2 s and push the next random pick 1 s away; otherwise, outside scripted_sequence ownership (NPC_STATE_SCRIPT), PickLookTarget(1.5-2.5 s, influence 0.3-0.5, ramp 0.2-0.4): PickRandomLookTarget over entities within 360 units that are visible and drawn (the player when moving or 1 in 3, held 1-4 s; others random importance 1-100, NPCs x10, moving NPCs x100 more; FVisible + ValidHeadTarget; fewer considered than RandomInt(0,5) -> none), else MakeRandomLookTarget (eye + forward x 128 + right U(-32,32) + up U(-16,16), influence 0.01, ramp 0.8-2.8); next pick at duration - 0.4. Not modeled at first: PickTacticalLookTarget (needs AI enemies), the navigating look-ahead branch, combat look-forward, expressions, PVS gating.
(3) Head: the existing Head::update (UpdateHeadControl) takes random + explicit interests (interest x HeadTargetValidity), so the head turns toward glances. Eyes: active targets scanned newest first (explicit before random) with ValidEyeTarget, replacing the host's nearest-visible fallback; no target keeps the latched/random view point as now.
Validate: unit tests (interest ramp curve, truncation, selection rules with a seeded RNG, fallback point); packaged walk past the d1_trainstation_02 queue citizens and the terminal cop with the eye/head report over time (glance durations, share of time on the player) vs native captures of the same citizens at 0.5 s intervals (head turned toward the player sometimes, not continuously); regression batch (attention verifier 26/26: scene LOOKAT must stay unchanged).
