14o session 41 wall stick (2026-11-02, branch `wip/session41-20261102`). Session 40 recorded a native difference: the oracle's blade bounced off the hall pillar where ours stuck. Evidence: (a) retail server.dll HandleInteractionStick (FUN_10222920, found through StickAtPosition FUN_102274c0 and its "Metal.SawbladeStick" string) has the SDK's shape and constants (speed > 1000.0, dot > 0.3 as a double, not 'M'/'G'/'X', embed 8.0); (b) the SDK hold distance (CGrabController::UpdateObject: 24 + 2 x radius, pulled in only when a wall is within half of that) rules out "held against the wall"; (c) a new native recording (private torso-split-20261029/record_sawwall2.py, native-sawwall-2.mkv, 60 fps; session view-d1_trainstation_02-20261102T173953Z) shows the blade embedded edge-on in the pillar for 0.48 s, then a second gravity gun shot frees it and it rebounds to the player's feet: the oracle held +attack ~2 s, and CWeaponPhysCannon::ItemPostFrame calls PrimaryAttack every frame +attack is down (0.5 s refire), so the punt (Physgun_OnPhysGunPickup -> EnableMotion on SF_PHYSPROP_ENABLE_ON_PHYSCANNON) pulled it out. Native sticks like ours; a launch at the flat poster wall (yaw 15, native-sawwall-3) shows the same refire release. (d) Ours was not deterministic: a `launch_impacts` report (first collision of each launch: speed, dot, material, normal, stuck) showed the same launch alternating between run processes between the pillar face (dot 1.0, stuck) and a contact at z 128.0 with dot 0.099 (not stuck): the pillar is two stacked brushes meeting at z 128 and Rapier reports one event per collider pair in a pair order that changes between processes; world_sticks took whichever came first. Fixes: `physics::impact_order` sorts each step's new contacts by collisionSpeed (hardest first, stable) as a stand-in for IVP's time-ordered impacts (four reruns: all stick head-on); CPhysicsProp::EnableMotion's teleport to the enable-motion position (StickAtPosition's SetEnableMotionPosition: the origin before the 8-unit embed), so a freed blade leaves the wall instead of staying wedged (`Physics::stuck` keeps it; `world_sticks` returns it). Correction on the way: OnPhysGunPickup leaves a freed world-stick prop in COLLISION_GROUP_INTERACTIVE_DEBRIS (what ours already did), not NONE. Packaged artifacts/s41 saw-wall (3 of 3 stuck) and saw-wall-held (stuck at 3.51 s, the refire punt frees it at 3.96 s, it drops out of the wall and lands ~90 units in front of the player; native ~50-60, rebounding faster: restitution not compared). Not done: PROPINTER_PHYSGUN_FIRST_IMPALE (harpoons, only in d2_coast_11/12: antlion areas; needs server ragdolls attached to the prop), the native freed-blade rebound speed.
14o session 41 burning walk/moan A/B and NPC damage reports. SDK: CAI_BaseNPC::OnTakeDamage_Alive calls PainSound for any damage, but sets COND_LIGHT_DAMAGE (any damage > 0) and COND_HEAVY_DAMAGE (> 20) and updates the attacker memory only inside `if (attacker has FL_NPC | FL_CLIENT)`; CEntityFlame's damage (FlameThink: CTakeDamageInfo(this, this, ...)) has the flame as attacker, so a burning NPC hears no damage conditions (SCHED_ZOMBIE_MELEE_ATTACK1 interrupts on COND_LIGHT_DAMAGE, which would otherwise cancel every claw); Event_Killed calls DeathSound for every death. Ours: `Inventory::apply_projectile_damage` (flames, blasts, physics impacts, dissolves excepted) now pushes each NPC hit into `scene.npc_damage` with the amount only for a character attacker (`is_character`: the player or a live NPC; 0 otherwise), so the AI tick's pain_or_death_sound runs for every damage kind and StopLoopingSounds (moan, breathing) happens at any death. Validation: unit test; packaged burn-walk-town2 (d1_town_02) with per-process audio against the native oracle (d1_trainstation_02 is unusable for chase A/Bs: its oracle zombie never takes the player as enemy).
14o session 41 fast headcrab plan (npc_headcrab_fast, CFastHeadcrab; Ravenholm onward), next session. SDK npc_headcrab.cpp: models/headcrab.mdl, sk_headcrab_health, NPC_FastHeadcrab.Idle/Alert/Pain/Die/Bite/Attack (pain at most 1 in 11 burn ticks while burning), MaxYawSpeed 120 (idle, turns, leap, default) and 150 (run/walk); SelectSchedule: SF_NPC_WAIT_TILL_SEEN -> SCHED_IDLE_STAND, COND_CAN_RANGE_ATTACK1 (not on a ceiling) -> SCHED_RANGE_ATTACK1 when a SQUAD_SLOT_ATTACK1..2 slot is free (else clear the condition), then CBaseHeadcrab's; TranslateSchedule: SCHED_IDLE_STAND -> SCHED_PATROL_WALK, SCHED_RANGE_ATTACK1 -> SCHED_FAST_HEADCRAB_RANGE_ATTACK1 (stop, face enemy, TASK_RANGE_ATTACK1, ACT_IDLE, face ideal, wait U(0, 0.5); no interrupts), SCHED_CHASE_ENEMY -> SCHED_PATROL_WALK without a free SQUAD_SLOT_ENGAGE1..4; RunTask TASK_RANGE_ATTACK1 turns toward the enemy during the leap (AI_KEEP_YAW_SPEED); QuerySeeEntity outside combat ignores anything 150+ units above or below; PrescheduleThink's run mode while a navigation goal is active (one step per 0.1 s think): IDLE -> (running) ACCELERATE at playback rate 0.5, +0.1 per think to 1.0 -> FULLSPEED for U(0.1, 1) s -> DECELERATE -0.1 per think to 0.5 -> PAUSE: ACT_IDLE in place for U(0.2, 0.5) s -> IDLE (ACT_RUN again); ground speed = the run's ground speed x the playback rate. TASK_HEADCRAB_HOP_ASIDE only serves SCHED_HEADCRAB_HOP_RANDOMLY (off an illegal ground entity: later). Steps: (1) owned check of headcrab.mdl (run/idle/leap sequences, the leap's telegraph and jump events, eye height); (2) ai::headcrab `fast` kind (sounds, yaw speeds, selection/translation, flight facing, sight limit; squad slots treated as free until strategy slots exist: deviation); (3) the controller's per-actor playback rate (advance with dt x rate, which scales the run's distance and its animation time together) and a held pause that reports the actor's idle sequence; the AI drives the run mode; (4) class lists (spawn model, ground hull, crab collision group, F4 list, AllowedToIgnite through is_headcrab_class). Validation: unit tests of the run-mode cycle and schedules, the owned model test, a packaged d1_town_02 fixture (stop-and-go approach, leap, bites), then a native A/B of the same layout.
Result (fast headcrab steps 1, 2 and 4, session 41): owned headcrab.mdl: idle01/idle02 (ACT_IDLE), run1 (ACT_RUN), attack (ACT_RANGE_ATTACK1: AE_HEADCRAB_JUMPATTACK at cycle 0, no telegraph event), hopleft/hopright, flinch, rearup, drown, canister deploys, ragdoll; eye 16 in the model (CBaseHeadcrab::Spawn's view offset 11 applies). `Headcrab::fast` (for_class), `fast_max_yaw_speed`, the selection/translation/flight-facing above, SCHED_FAST_HEADCRAB_RANGE_ATTACK1; AI_GROUND_HUMANS and is_headcrab_class include it (the other class lists already did). A shared fix: Scene::ai_events is open at its start, so a cycle-0 event of a sequence the AI started in its think (after that tick's gather, at the same time stamp) never fired; `runtime::event_window_start` opens the next window at that start when the last gather had not seen the (sequence, start) pair, and keeps the open window otherwise (no replay for sequences a script started before the gather). Packaged d1_town_02 fast-crab-room (ent_create at pitch -20, ~176 units ahead): leap at once, bites at 0.75 s and then about every 1.3 s. Step 3: the run mode is `headcrab::RunState` (unit test of the 0.5 -> 1.0 -> 0.5 cycle and the U(0.2, 0.5) s pause), not yet wired to the controller's playback rate and pause; the sight limit is `headcrab::fast_sees_height` in the sight gather (player and NPCs).
14o session 42 fast headcrab step 3 plan (the run mode wired). SDK: CFastHeadcrab::PrescheduleThink runs only while the navigator's goal is active; SetActivity(ACT_IDLE) at the pause and SetActivity(ACT_RUN) after it reset m_flPlaybackRate to 1 (CBaseAnimating::ResetSequenceInfo), so the pause's idle plays at full rate and the first think after a pause runs at full speed before the next acceleration from 0.5; without a goal only m_flPauseTime expires (mode and rate carry over). Steps: (a) `npc::Controller::set_playback(entity, rate, pause)`: `advance` consumes dt x rate (the run's distance and cycle together; route simplification keeps real time); a paused goal stays Moving, does not move and reports the pause sequence on its own clock; resuming restarts the move's cycle. (b) The fast crab's think drives `RunState` while its AI goal is Moving or Blocked and picks the pause's ACT_IDLE sequence once (`Scene::select_weighted_sequence`, shared with `ai_reselect_activity`); only fast crabs draw the extra random, so other actors' streams stay unchanged. (c) `RunState` resets the rate at both ends of the pause and gains `no_goal`. Validation: unit tests (controller rate and pause, the run cycle), a packaged approach long enough for a full cycle (report: rate, pause sequence, positions), then the native A/B.
Result (fast headcrab step 3, session 42): wired as planned. Packaged d1_town_02 approaches (the crab created down the start corridor at movement-script pitch -13, -8 and -6: 347, 569 and 761 units away): acceleration from 0.5 to 1.0 over about 0.5 s with the step length growing with the rate, full speed for U(0.1, 1) s, deceleration, a 0.3 s pause in idle02 without movement, one think at full rate, then the next acceleration; the leap follows at 1.05, 2.62 and 3.82 s. `RunState::no_goal` also restores the rate, so an acceleration interrupted by a leap resumes at full speed (SetActivity's reset). Native A/B, retail with the same corridor, aim and 569-unit spawn, sampled with the game's own screenshots under host_timescale 0.25 (about 0.3 s of game time apart): the first leap 2.7-2.9 s after notarget is lifted, ours 2.35 s after the spawn; a constant full-speed run would need about 1.3-1.5 s, so the native approach agrees with the stop-and-go run, but this sampling cannot resolve the single pauses. Difference found: native leaps pass the doorway at x -996 and reach the player; ours hit its frame (the session 41 note), next.

Plan: `npc::Controller::find_backaway` (new child module npc/backaway.rs): CAI_TacticalServices::FindBackAwayPos from the save position: CAI_Navigator::FindVectorGoal straight away from the threat (120 units, at least 120; a blocked ground move continues along the blocking plane, CalculateDeflection), else FindBackAwayNode (the first usable link of the nearest usable node to a node farther from the threat and not toward it), else a vector goal of hull width x 4 (at least x 2); `Motor::find_backaway`; the task sets a run goal there (FAIL_NO_BACKAWAY_NODE / FAIL_NO_ROUTE otherwise). It applies to every class whose schedules back away (humanoid COND_TOO_CLOSE_TO_ATTACK too). Validation: unit tests (open floor, a wall behind: deflection, boxed in: none), the packaged far-spawn crab fixtures, PR #4's gameplay replays and the regression batch for the shared change.

(c) Conditions (GatherAttackConditions' distance and 2D facing dot): MeleeAttack1Conditions needs the ground and m_flNextMeleeAttack, and drops the base's TOO_FAR/NOT_FACING; RangeAttack1Conditions: on the ground, m_flNextAttack passed, |dz| <= 128 and distance <= 300 else COND_TOO_FAR_TO_ATTACK, distance >= 200, dot >= 0.8, moving, inside the player's view cone (CBasePlayer m_flFieldOfView 0.766, 2D from his eyes) and a 32-unit cube from its center halfway to the enemy's center clear (MASK_NPCSOLID). (e) Sound patches: MoanSound kick-start (NPC_FastZombie.Moan1 at volume 1, pitch 100, unless gagged; half the time IdleSound), then SetIdleSoundState (pitch 35, volume 0.75 over 1 s); NPC_FastZombie.Gurgle silent; combat: LeapAttack sound and pitch 70, volume 1 over 0.5 s; every 0.5 s in combat with an enemy the pitch follows the distance (center to center less the hull's 26-unit width: under 480 units pitch 70 + 60 x (1 - d/480), else back to 35); pain envelopes (gurgle up then out, moan down then back); death NPC_FastZombie.NoSound, StopLoopingSounds, Die. 14o session 44 fast zombie step 2 plan (navigation jumps and climbing; branch `wip/session44-20261104`). Survey of the owned graphs for HULL_HUMAN: jump-only links on every map (d1_town_02 688, of which 536 within the fast zombie's limits; d1_town_03 397), climb links only on d1_town_02 (29), d1_town_03 (8), d3_c17_06b (6) and background03 (14); fast zombies are placed on d1_town_02 (30), d1_town_02a (8), d1_town_03 (10). A ladder is climb nodes (node type 4) joined by climb links, with same-height climb links to the mount and dismount nodes and ground links from those to the floor nodes around it. Owned fast.mdl: climbloop ACT_CLIMB_UP (15 frames, 44.9 units up per 0.467 s cycle, about 96 u/s; AE_FASTZOMBIE_CLIMB_LEFT 0.36, _RIGHT 0.86), climbdismount ACT_CLIMB_DISMOUNT (53 frames, 53.4 forward and 106.6 up), jumpnavmove ACT_JUMP (53 frames), no ACT_GLIDE or ACT_LAND sequence, climbmount has no activity. SDK rules: (a) capabilities: CFastZombie adds bits_CAP_MOVE_CLIMB and bits_CAP_MOVE_JUMP; IsLinkUsable needs the link's move types for the hull to meet them, and a jump link must pass IsJumpLegal between the node positions (the fast zombie: 220 up, 384 down, 512 long, apex at most 1.25 x the rise); CFastZombie::MovementCost scales a link: a jump x 0.5 going down, x 1.5 going up; a climb x 0.5 going up, x 4 going down. (b) A waypoint's nav type is the type of the link that leads to it (MakeRouteFromParents); SimplifyPath leaves jump and climb waypoints alone. (c) MoveJump: on the ground, CAI_MoveProbe's jump limit finds the lowest arc that clears the way (CalcJumpLaunchVelocity from the height needed for GetMaxJumpSpeed 350 horizontally and for the step, a bisection on the apex height from a 1024 step down to 16, each try swept in ten hull steps; a ceiling hit lowers it, a wall hit raises it); a blocked jump fails the link; then MOVETYPE_STEP physics carries it at sv_gravity x GetJumpGravity 1 with ACT_JUMP, and on landing the path advances to the next waypoint (ACT_LAND is skipped when the model has none). (d) MoveClimb: gravity off, a straight line to the waypoint at the sequence's instantaneous speed (at least 30), facing the waypoint yaw; ACT_CLIMB_UP or _DOWN only while the direction's z is above 0.1 (the fast zombie translates DOWN to UP, and same-height links keep the running activity); going up with at most two climb waypoints left and less distance than the dismount's height to go, ACT_CLIMB_DISMOUNT starts at the frame with that much height left; the climb ends at the first waypoint not reached by a climb link. A navigation stop in the middle of a climb lets the NPC drop (MoveClimbStop, then gravity); while climbing, new routes may only be climb routes. (e) CFastZombie: ClimbTouch (the player touched while climbing gets a 200 u/s planar velocity punch; COND_FASTZOMBIE_CLIMB_TOUCH unless dismounting -> SCHED_FASTZOMBIE_UNSTICK_JUMP, which jumps backwards off the pipe at 200 u/s via SCHED_FASTZOMBIE_CLIMBING_UNSTICK_JUMP); moan volume 0 during a climb, back to 1 after; every third climb event the gurgle layer gets pitch U(100, 150) and a volume envelope; the jump's apex is noted for the layer; SCHED_FASTZOMBIE_UNSTICK_JUMP translates to the climbing one while in a climb activity. Steps: (1) `npc/links.rs` (npc.rs is over its size limit, so nothing grows there): `Nav { Ground, Jump, Climb }`, `MoveCaps` per actor (`Controller::set_move_caps`; only npc_fastzombie for now, Combine and other jumpers later), link choice, jump legality, costs; `search` and `node_feet` take the caps (climb nodes count only for climbers); `Route::navs`; pure jump arc functions with SDK-derived cases. (2) The controller traversal, also in npc/links.rs: a jump goal hands `MoveUpdate::launch` to the host, holds while airborne (sequence jumpnavmove on the goal's clock) and resumes at `Controller::landed`; a climb moves the feet in the air on the sequences' root speed and reports climbloop/climbdismount; a new request during a traversal is held until it ends (deviation: Source allows climb-only reroutes in mid-climb), a cancel drops the climber through the shared fall. (3) AiRuntime: launches become `actor.fall`, landings call `Controller::landed`, ClimbTouch, the two sounds, the schedule translation. (4) Tests: unit (legality, costs, arc, route nav types, jump hold, climb speed and dismount frame, cancel drop), owned graph check, packaged d1_town_02 fixture routing a fast zombie over a jump link and up a pipe, native A/B of the same layout. Not in this step: NPC-blocked climbs (MoveClimb's give-way), hint-driven HINT_JUMP_OVERRIDE links, jumps for other classes.
14o session 44 native A/B and SetSchedule's ClearGoal. The oracle's console gives native numbers: a cfg alias loop (`fps_max 60`, `wait 15`, host_timescale 0.25) dumps the NPC named through `!picker` and the player every 0.0625 s of game time (a private recording script; sequence numbers are the model's order, a missing `sequence:` line is sequence 0, `ent_dump` has no origin). Native d1_town_02 corridor at spawn pitch 13: Run, Leap, LeapStrike, the player 100 -> 95 within about 0.4 s of leaving the ground and shoved to x -719.8 (from -756), then idle 1.9 s, Run 1 s, idle, no further damage in the 8.4 s sampled. Ours landed 159 units short and clawed 100 -> 58: session 43's "landing" was an artifact. The fall's horizontal velocity was 0 while the zombie still advanced: the chase goal stayed active through the leap and its ground move overwrote the fall every tick. CAI_BaseNPC::SetSchedule calls GetNavigator()->ClearGoal(); ours never did, for any class. `AiNpc::maintain` now stops the move before a new schedule's first task (task 0, not started), so every NPC whose new schedule does not begin with a stop or a path no longer slides along its old route. After the fix: velocity (735, 0, 191), the player 100 -> 95 at 1.2 s, shoved to x -716. Regression 26/26, 17/17, 26/26 and replays 72 of 72 unchanged; the image differences are a sub-pixel head line on Barney and the monitors' own animation (reviewed by eye). After the leap ours then planned a chase while still airborne, failed ("actor cannot stand"), marked the enemy unreachable and forgot it: a false failure, since Source's navigator can plan from the air. SCHED_FASTZOMBIE_LAND_WAIT (ours, a deviation) holds the zombie until it lands; then it chases and claws. Three native runs at spawn pitch 13 agree on Run, Leap, LeapStrike, the 5-damage leap claw and the shove (the player ends at x -720 to -685), and differ after it: run 2 goes idle, Run, idle; run 3 plays LandLeft and stays in it to the end of the sample, so an earlier note that native has no land animation came from one sample and was wrong. OPEN, not resolved: native dealt no further damage in about 8 s in any run, ours claws on (95 -> 80 by 5 s). ours follows the SDK's logic; the native stall looks like an oracle condition (unfocused window, host_timescale, the view commands), but that is an inference: ent_dump and ent_show_response_criteria give no distance, so where the native zombie stands is unknown. Do not tune ours toward it without a position source or the owner's playtest. The far spawn has no native data: `!picker` missed at 761 units (0 zombie dumps; its `idle` lines mean no data).
14o session 45 plan: NPCs embedded in each other (owner playtest 2026-11-04: two fast zombies that landed together froze in the idle pose, gurgling, neither moving, turning nor attacking). Reproduced on d1_town_02 with two ent_create spawns: blocked_moves counts only `Collision { blocker: None, reason: "actor cannot stand" }` (433 in 36 s) and the pair cycles SCHED_CHASE_ENEMY_FAILED, SCHED_FAIL and SCHED_ZOMBIE_WANDER_FAIL for good; controls: one zombie alone and a pair 400 units apart chase, leap, land and claw. Cause: Controller::request and advance refuse any move whose start hull touches another actor, so each actor of an overlapping pair blocks the other forever. SDK CAI_MoveProbe::GroundMoveLimit (bTryNavIgnore): when a step from the NPC's own position starts solid in an NPC, that NPC is ignored for the probe (up to 16 retries per step), so embedded NPCs walk out of each other; VPhysics objects only when moving away, and the player never. Change: npc/embedded.rs finds the actors the start hull is inside and leaves them out of that move's probes; the same trace replaces `fits` at the start, so a clear start costs no extra trace. The check stays after the link traversal, so climbers keep skipping it. Route planning already ignores NPCs (NPC_BRUSH_ONLY_MASK). Found while validating: AI moves pass no transient list (AiRuntime::tick gets `&[]`), so a neighbour counts through its model collider, wider than its hull: two zombies 26.8 units apart blocked each other with no box overlap. So the NPC test asks the controller, not the transient list. Second cause of the same freeze: an NPC with nothing under it never fell (ent_create onto a metrocop leaves it in the air when UTIL_DropToFloor starts solid; an NPC standing on another that walks away is the same case), and stand failed for good. Now an AI mover with no floor within its step-down distance goes through the controller's existing drops into the step fall, as a MOVETYPE_STEP entity off the ground does; scene movers stay where the script put them. A fall also passes an NPC it starts embedded in: two zombies created in the air inside each other each "landed" on the other (fall records: stuck, ground = the other). Not in this step: the VPhysics case, GroundMoveLimit's STEP_ON_INVALID_GROUND (a start hanging over an edge still fails `stand` here, where Source lets the NPC walk off), and a console ent_create inside the map, which has no position check in the SDK either (F4 refuses bad positions). Validation: unit tests (embedded pair walks out, a start inside the player stays blocked), packaged pair fixtures against the solo control, then regression and replays because every NPC's moves go through this code.
14o session 45 bullet impact effects (owner 2026-10-29: sparks from metal, splinters from wood, dust from concrete). SDK PerformCustomEffects at iScale 1 by game material: FX_DebrisFlecks on concrete and tile (cement flecks) and wood (wood flecks), FX_DustImpact on dirt and sand, FX_MetalSpark on metal and vents along the shot's reflection. Impacts::add_shot picks the kind from `gamematerial`, Projectiles::tick moves it onto the effect list, explosion_particles/bullet.rs emits it (host tick untouched). Simplified or left out: GetColorForSurface reads the surface's texture reflectivity and its light, ours a fixed color per material in the baked light at the hit (leaf_ambient, sampled once per hit); the spark's flash is a sprite, not a quad on the surface; computer sparks and the antlion impact are not modeled. Cost: only on a hit, bounded by MAX_PARTICLES. Validation: unit test of the mapping and emitters, packaged shots on each surface.
14o session 45 map zombies and headcrabs. Found through the campaign's own fast zombie pairs: d1_town_03's last_fz_1 and last_fz_2 woke and failed every move with MissingActor. Every one of the 342 map-placed zombies and headcrabs has no `model` key (their Spawn sets it in code) and append_entity_models knew defaults for four classes only, so they got no model instance and never registered with the movement controller. F4 and ent_create use the pool, whose table knows them, so those tests never showed it. Change: ai::spawn::default_model moves to source_assets::npc_models as the one table; map_default_model gives the map path the classes that have AI here, not the statues (striders, antlions and the rest wait for their AI). Validation: instance count and a capture before and after, map fixtures, regression and replays, with any changed replay outcome reviewed.

14o session 46 plan (owner 2026-11-05: no campaign play-throughs yet, finish the planned NPC behaviors and systems first). npc_barnacle first: 80 placed on 15 maps, the first class in campaign order with no AI, and without it the map class has no model. Evidence: Source SDK hl2/npc_barnacle.cpp and client hl2/c_barnacle.cpp; owned models/barnacle.mdl (test `owned_barnacle_has_its_tongue_bones_and_activities`): `Barnacle.base`, then 8 root-level bones `Barnacle.tongue1..8`, one fewer than the SDK client's loop writes, so the rope uses the bones the model has. Activities: ACT_IDLE idle01, ACT_BARNACLE_SLURP slurp, ACT_BARNACLE_BITE_PLAYER attack_player (looping, AE_BARNACLE_BITE at cycle 0.34), ACT_BARNACLE_BITE_HUMAN eat_humanoid, ACT_BARNACLE_BITE_SMALL_THINGS attack_smallthings, ACT_BARNACLE_CHEW_HUMAN and _SMALL_THINGS, ACT_BARNACLE_TASTE_SPIT taste_spit (AE_BARNACLE_SPIT), ACT_DIESIMPLE death and death2. Bounded steps, each reversible and unit tested: (1) `ai/barnacle.rs`, a state machine with no engine: tongue altitude lowering at the pull speed to the floor (a 2048-unit brush trace less the rest height), a 12-unit box around the tongue for touch, grab, the pull (|sin| of a local timer, 80 u/s, slowed so a lift takes at least 3 s), bite height 60 (85 for the player), the stuck timer, losing prey that is blocked or dead, digesting, gibs, death with the tongue moving to its dead length. (2) AiClass::Barnacle with its own 0.1 s think, no mover and no schedules; the map path gets its model. (3) The rope: a verlet chain between root and tip in the simulation, written over the tongue bones in `actor_matrices`, with the tip in `pose_signature` so the pose cache stays right. (4) The player: held input (no move, no use, weapons free), lifted with the tip, 15 damage per bite, released when the barnacle dies. (5) NPCs and props: hated NPCs killed at the mouth, props up to 70 kg lifted and spat out. Not in this step: the ragdoll on the tongue (an NPC victim is lifted whole, then gibbed), vehicles, Alyx's rescue. Validation: unit tests, owned model test, a packaged d1_canals_01a run with a walking player, regression and replays; native comparison of lift time and bite cadence with the oracle. Then crows and pigeons, npc_manhack and cop deployment, npc_cscanner, in that order.
14o session 46 npc_barnacle status. Steps (1) to (5) are in, as ai/barnacle.rs (state machine, rope, tongue bones) and ai/runtime/barnacles.rs (facts and commands). Barnacles sit in their own table beside the schedule actors: no memory, senses or mover, an idle rope sleeps, and out of the player's PVS one thinks every 1 to 1.5 s. Three things the packaged runs found and unit tests could not: (a) with no class hull the collider was the whole bind mesh, tongue included, so the player stood inside the body and `carried` never moved the player; the body is now the SDK's small box (`ai_hull::body_box`). (b) That hull then made the NPC startup drop every barnacle to the floor, because all 80 have spawnflags 4 (SF_NPC_FALL_TO_GROUND): the SDK class skips NPCInit, so `stays_where_placed` exempts it. (c) A think shortens the tongue in 0.1 s steps; taken at once, each step threw the player upward at about 333 u/s and the player fell back, so the rope length now follows at 120 u/s per tick. Simplified or left out: an NPC victim is lifted whole with no ragdoll on the tongue and no swallow, and is removed at the bite; the rib gibs and the blood spray and decal at death are not drawn (CoughGib is a no-op); idle playback rate is not varied; the spit goes straight down, not along StrikeHeadAttach; the player's bite is DMG_CLUB, not DMG_SLASH; crouching while held does not change the hold; vehicles are out. The first think of a map has no PVS answer, so a barnacle can miss prey for up to about 1.4 s after a map loads.
14o session 46 plan, birds (next, not started): npc_crow, npc_pigeon, npc_seagull (124 placed). One class in the SDK (hl2/npc_crow.cpp) with three models and sounds. HULL_TINY, full view, MOVETYPE_STEP, CLASS_EARTH_FAUNA, zombies neutral. On the ground: stand, wander every 10 to 20 s, walk away from an enemy past 400 units, run from 300 to 400 (morale drops), fly when one is closer than that, on damage, or on a danger or combat sound (at most every 10 s). In the air: steer toward a goal at 220 u/s, 170 at takeoff, turning harder as it nears, a short probe trace deflecting it, and land on a hint or the ground. Unlike the barnacle this class fits the schedule actors, since it needs senses, sounds and the ground mover; flight is new and belongs in its own module, because manhacks and scanners need a flying mover next. Bounded steps: (1) a fly mover with the probe and landing; (2) AiClass::Bird, its SelectSchedule and tasks; (3) FlyAway input, deaf key, SF_CROW_FLYING; (4) map model and packaged runs on d1_canals_01a and d1_town_05. Validation: unit tests, packaged runs, native comparison of flee distance.
Result (fast zombie step 2, session 44; fcef36a, 023b17c): as planned, in npc/links.rs (capabilities, link choice and costs, the jump arc search, the climb state machine, the controller's traversal API), npc/graph.rs (the search, plans and door probes moved out of npc.rs) and ai/runtime/navlinks.rs. One rule the plan missed, found by measuring: CAI_Node::GetPosition does not use a climb node's origin. A hull stands back from the node along its yaw by half the hull's length plus NODE_CLIMB_OFFSET 8 (ahead of it for bits_NODE_CLIMB_OFF_FORWARD, two shifts aside and one back for OFF_LEFT and OFF_RIGHT), which is why a ladder has several nodes on one spot: they are different standing positions. d1_town_02's node 70 lies 3 units inside the wall, so every walk to it was blocked. Rejected on the way: a waypoint tolerance rule for link starts (the block was 18 to 29 units from the node, past the 13-unit tolerance, and the cause was the position; removed), and the suspicion that the pipes collide (all are solid 0 and are skipped; the player clip brush around the pipe correctly does not block NPCs). A speed sample ending at a loop's last frame reached cycle 1 + epsilon, wrapped and added a whole cycle (895 u/s in a unit test): the sample's end is clamped to 1. Packaged d1_town_02 (artifacts/s44 make_fixtures.py, run.sh, fz_summ.py). fz-ladder: the player wakes a fast zombie at the foot of the ladder at node 70 and teleports to the ledge 450 units up; route 35, 70, 236, 514, 344; climbloop at 3.82 s, climbdismount at 7.42 s (z -3243), run on top at 9.0 s (z -3136), then claws; 341 units in 3.6 s is about 95 u/s against the model's 96; 2 plans, 0 failures, 1 climb. fz-drop: the same from the upper level by node 88 with the player at node 419; route 208, 88, 420, 419; jumpnavmove at 2.55 s at velocity (-115, 75, 394), apex 138 units up, landed on node 420 at z -3568 about 1.5 s later, then claws; 1 jump, 0 refused. The probe rightly refuses a landing spot the player stands on (the first fixture stood on node 420: 6 refusals). Not verified against retail: the jump's apex (well above the least arc, so the sweep probably hit something), the 1.3 s of SCHED_CHASE_ENEMY_FAILED after that landing before the replan, all climb and jump timing (no native A/B yet). Not modeled: ACT_CLIMB_DOWN does not silence the moan or arm ClimbTouch in the SDK and here it does; ClimbTouch only reaches the condition with a visible enemy; NPC-blocked climbs; HINT_JUMP_OVERRIDE links; the tolerance rule itself for ground waypoints. Not tested packaged: ClimbTouch, the climbing unstick jump, a climb down, a cancel in mid-climb (unit tested only).
14o session 46 birds, findings and steps. Owned files: 148 birds on 25 maps, 124 of them in the campaign (95 crows, 36 seagulls, 17 pigeons). All three models have ACT_IDLE, ACT_WALK, ACT_RUN, ACT_FLY, ACT_HOP, ACT_CROW_TAKEOFF, ACT_CROW_SOAR and ACT_CROW_LAND (owned test `owned_birds_have_their_flight_activities`). AE_CROW_TAKEOFF sits at cycle 0.18 of the crow and pigeon takeoff and 0.37 of the seagull's, AE_CROW_HOP at cycle 0 of hop; no model carries AE_CROW_FLY, so that event needs no handler. Maps drive flight three ways: FlyAway with no parameter (fly to the nearest hint), FlyAway naming a path_corner chain (16 corners over the d1_trainstation_02 plaza, about 56 on d2_coast_08, where one corner's OnPass fires), and hint type 700 on info_node_air_hint or info_node_hint, with hintgroup on 13 birds. One pigeon sits on d1_trainstation_02 and its template flies it at once, so regression and replay captures there can change when it gets a model: review those differences, do not wave them through. SDK rules the plan had not covered: a flight along corners holds only the next two corners and refills when it passes one, firing InPass on each (hl2 CAI_Navigator::AdvancePath), and it lands only at a corner with no next target; a hint stays held across schedules, so a bird that reaches an air hint falls, sees it is off the ground and takes off for the same hint, which is how it stays up. The player's relationship to birds is neutral; the birds' hate of the player only feeds their distance conditions, and their melee damage is 0, so they never attack. Bounded steps: (0) move `impl AiClass` to a child of ai/runtime.rs and a stage of `think` to its module, so the new arms do not grow a baselined file or function; (1) ai/bird.rs: state, schedules, SelectSchedule order, tasks, and flight as pure functions (steering, probe deflection, bank), unit tested; (2) ai/runtime/birds.rs: type 700 hints with their groups, corner chains and InPass, FlyAway, the takeoff event, per-tick flight through the step fall's hull trace with the one second stuck check, and landing through the existing step fall; (3) wiring: class hull, eye height, health 1, the map model, sounds; (4) packaged runs on d1_canals_01a and d1_trainstation_02. Flight costs one hull trace per flying bird per tick and nothing for a bird on the ground; frame time is measured on d1_town_01, the map with the most birds (19). Nothing selects SCHED_CROW_HOP_AWAY or SCHED_CROW_FLY and nothing reads the morale, so hops and morale are left out with no change in behavior. An enemy under 512 units always sends a bird up, so the 300 to 400 run branch cannot be reached from a fresh condition; birds walk off between 512 and 1024. Birds pass through the player, NPCs and each other (HL2COLLISION_GROUP_CROW): their body box is registered with an empty collision filter, and bullets use the hitboxes. Each bird rolls its own dice so that flights leave the other NPCs' random stream alone. Rejected on the way: flying straight at a hint with only the probe to deflect it. Packaged on d1_town_01 five of the six street crows that took off ended wedged in mid-air against buildings with no speed left, and a sixth stayed pinned with its speed still set. Flights now route over the graph (npc/air.rs): straight when the hull trace is clear, else in by a near node with a clear leg, along the links that accept bits_CAP_MOVE_FLY for the hull, and out by a node with a clear leg to the goal; no route fails the task and the bird stays on the ground, as the SDK's failed SetGoal does. The .ain files hold these links already (owned test: d1_town_01 has 62 air nodes, all flown by HULL_TINY, 420 link ends), so nothing is built at map load; each hull's table is made at its first flight. The SDK's local triangulation is not modeled. The stuck check takes under 1 unit moved and under 1 u/s as stopped, since a scrape along a wall leaves a trace of speed. Simplified on purpose: no feathers or gibs (the SDK has none either); birds held by a barnacle use the barnacle's generic NPC lift, not SCHED_CROW_BARNACLED; sleepstate is not read. Validation runs one job at a time while the owner is playing.

Campaign order after the birds: 69 placed from d1_canals_01, then npc_manhack (63, from d1_canals_03), npc_turret_floor (49, from d2_prison_02). The helpers npc_bullseye (186) and npc_enemyfinder (73) are invisible and need their own short step, since scripted NPCs aim at them. So `npc/air.rs` routes carry over; the bird's velocity step does not. Routes and patrols use the class's hull (HULL_TINY_CENTERED) over npc/air.rs; a patrol enters the network at the nearest reachable node and wanders along the links for about 2000 units, at most 20 nodes, never back over a node. Owned combine_scanner.mdl: idle (ACT_IDLE), inspect1/inspect2 (ACT_RUN, weights 2 and 1), flare and retract (ACT_SCANNER_FLARE_START/END), ragdoll; no ACT_WALK and no ACT_SMALL_FLINCH, so the spotlight hover idles, an alert scanner in good health does not flinch, and a flinch ends at once. Deviations in this step: SCHED_TAKE_COVER_FROM_ORIGIN flies off on patrol (no cover search in the air yet); the way to the enemy is checked with a trace that props also stop (the SDK's is world only); the body passes through NPCs and the player; poses (tail, dynamo, flare, face) are not driven; the lean eases at 120 degrees a second, a rate that is not from the SDK; a route never gives up on a point it cannot reach. Map evidence that changes the plan: scanners are placed from d1_trainstation_01 on, not from d1_canals_01, and on background01 and background07, so this class reaches replayed maps at once. Inputs the maps send to scanners, whole campaign: Kill 23, InspectTargetPhoto 14, InputSetFlightSpeed 13, EquipMine 9, DeployMine 9, SetFollowTarget 6, SetDistanceOverride 3, InspectTargetSpotlight 1. InputSetFlightSpeed is no input of the class (its input is SetFlightSpeed, itself unsupported in the SDK), so those 13 do nothing. New order: (3) InspectTargetPhoto, the photograph schedules and flash, OnPhotographPlayer and OnPhotographNPC, then SetFollowTarget and SetDistanceOverride, all needed from chapter 1; (4) death, moved ahead because a killed scanner takes the generic death until then: the dive when high and far from its enemy, else gibs at once, the blast through `prop_explosions`; (5) the spotlight, inspection of citizens and InspectTargetSpotlight, which need rendering of their own: nothing in the project draws a spotlight beam yet; (6) the flash attack; (7) EquipMine and DeployMine (d3_c17_04 on). Packaged d1_canals_01: three scanners patrol about 3300 units in 25 s (136 u/s; the arithmetic's cruise is 144.6). Two others stop thinking on the way to node (1948, -5356, 1100), which lies inside an unfiltered NPC trigger_multiple with !activator,Kill (x 1845..1979, y -5400..-5312, z 1073..1125): read from positions and the map, the trigger's firing itself not observed. A native comparison of patrol speed and of whether native scanners enter that volume is **not done**.
14o session 46 npc_cscanner step 3: inspection, following, paths, dice. In: InspectTargetPhoto, InspectTargetSpotlight, SetFollowTarget, ClearFollowTarget, SetDistanceOverride and InputShouldInspect; the ShouldInspect key; SCHED_CSCANNER_PHOTOGRAPH, _PHOTOGRAPH_HOVER, _MOVE_TO_INSPECT and SCHED_SCANNER_FOLLOW_HOVER. A photograph needs the photo mode, under 64 u/s, 64 to 128 units across, the aim within 4 degrees (one way only, as the SDK's signed test) and a clear line; an inspection lasts 5 s from when the photograph is taken, and within it another comes after 5 to 10 s. OnPhotographPlayer and OnPhotographNPC fire. 20 of the 74 placed scanners name a path_corner in `target`: they fly the chain and fire OnPass; any schedule change drops it, as a navigation goal is dropped. Hover ranges: spotlight 160 (the first version's 128 was the base class's value, which the class overrides), follow 128, photograph 96; SetDistanceOverride replaces all of them; an info_target or info_hint_air followed marks the side to hover on by its facing. **Scanners draw from their own dice.** With the shared stream every scanner think moved the choices of all other NPCs on the map: replays on b23e809 failed 7 of 72, six of them d1_trainstation_02 fixtures whose input and output counts moved by 1 to 4 with trigger entries unchanged. A first explanation, the plaza pigeon's corners, was tested and rejected: in `soundscapes` the pigeon passes corners with the counts unchanged. `AiRuntime::roll_for` then gave the scanner its own dice and all six passed with nothing re-recorded, so the expected files stay as they were. Other classes keep sharing the stream. Not in this step: the flash's light and its blinding (BlindFlashTarget), the citizen's reaction (g_interactionScannerInspectBegin), the citizen and hint searches that start an inspection on its own, a corner's `wait`, SCHED_CSCANNER_SPOTLIGHT_INSPECT_*. Packaged: d1_canals_01, in the open, pathscanner_1_scanner told InspectTargetPhoto !player reaches range at 3.45 s, one TakePhoto, patrol again at 8.55 s after the inspection ends at 8.4 s. d1_trainstation_02, zippy told the same with the player walking at (-4851, -3611, 80), precriminal: patrol from the start, photograph at 3.45 s, one TakePhoto, patrol again at 8.25 s. A first run from a spot chosen blind, at the edge of the map, gave up by 1.35 s with no photograph; that run also had the hostility bug below, so why it failed was not isolated. The class table says scanners hate the player and only the citizen's class changes while gordon_precriminal is on: `AiClass::spares_precriminal` is the scanner's own relation, a test covers both states.
14o session 46 npc_cscanner step 4: death. A lethal hit leaves the body as a `corpse`, as a barnacle's, so no ragdoll comes and nothing can hit it again. With an enemy more than 120 units below it and more than 360 away it dives: DiveBomb sound, 1600 thrust at 0.05 decay along the line to the enemy, speed up to 2500, four times the noise, a spin; the first thing it meets ends it, and the player takes sk_scanner_dmg_dive (25 in the owned skill.cfg). Otherwise it ends at once. Its end: the engine stops, Die plays, a blast through `prop_explosions` at the no-damage values the prop code uses, and the four owned gib models (models/gibs/scanner_gib01, 02, 04, 05). `npc_models::add_class_gibs` puts them in `model_breaks` under the scanner's model, so `append_break_physics` and the host's `gib_models` preload them like any prop's gibs: no file read at death. Deviations: OnDeath fires at the lethal hit, not at the end of the dive; the spin's values are the SDK's torque range read as degrees a second; gibs leave at the middle of CGib's 250 to 500 u/s. Not in this step: NPCs in its way (flights pass through them), the smoke trail, sparks, the light, DiveBombFlyby, the 30% battery, DeployMine at death. Packaged: scanner-gib, d1_trainstation_02: zippy killed by name with no enemy ends at once, Die once, all four gibs spawn, none skipped. scanner-dive, d1_town_02, a scanner spawned high up the street with the player walking below: dive from 1.38 s, about 360 units above the player, DiveBomb once, top speed 645 u/s, ended beside the player at 3.03 s, Die once, four gibs. That run took no player damage, so the strike itself has only a unit test: a dive holds its line and its noise can carry it past. A first fixture proved nothing: `ent_fire npc_cscanner` names no entity, and a scanner cannot see straight down at a player far below it (field of view 0.2).
14o session 47 npc_cscanner step 5: the flash attack. Evidence: Source SDK hl2/npc_scanner.cpp, hl2/npc_basescanner.cpp, ai_basenpc.cpp, ai_basenpc_schedule.cpp. In: ai/scanner/flash.rs (eye, attack condition, blinding, light), SCHED_CSCANNER_ATTACK_FLASH, Plan::Melee. An attack is looked at only with the enemy seen, within 1024 units, and once m_flNextAttack has passed; it needs 350 units across the ground and a flat facing dot of 0.7. With COND_CAN_MELEE_ATTACK1 a coin picks the flash or a chase. The flash: a 1 s warning, the eye toggling red at scale 0.5 on every think with the shutter sound each time; then AttackFlash, the eye white at scale 1.8, OnPhotographPlayer or OnPhotographNPC and a light at the enemy's last known position; one think later (the wait is 0.05 s, a think is 0.1 s) the blinding, and the next attack in 8 to 12 s, half that for a strider scout (one on the campaign's maps, 1stfloor_scanner1 on d3_c17_12b). Blinding: a look within 60 degrees of the scanner and a clear line, alpha 240 times the dot, times 0.9 for HDR, as a 3 s fade with a 0.5 s hold. A photograph of the player blinds the same way, which step 3 had left out. A task runs in the pass that starts it in the SDK and from the next think here, so the warning's start plays both of the first think's beats. The eye goes out on every schedule change, as the SDK's OnScheduleChange does. Deviations: the SDK's light is a dynamic light on world and models; here it is an elight on models only, since nothing lights world surfaces. The HDR share always applies, since the simulation cannot read the setting (the owned game runs HDR). The eye's visibility is all or nothing: one line from the view, and a body seen from behind hides it; the SDK reads an occlusion query. flDistToEnemy is taken from origin to origin, the SDK's closest of three points. Not in this step: striders told of the target (no striders yet), the scare sound of a scout, the NPC's reaction to a photograph. Host: ShotEffect moved to gameplay/shot_effect.rs and shot_effects to effects/shots.rs, since both files were at their limits.
14o session 47 npc_cscanner poses (owner 2026-11-06: the animated faceplate was missing). In the owned combine_scanner.mdl the faceplate, tail, dynamo and flaps are five autoplay delta sequences (upndown, rightnleft, tail_control, dynamo_turn, alert_control), each blended by one pose parameter (flex_vert and flex_horz -20..20, tail_control -25..80, dynamo_wheel -180..180 looping, alert_control -1..1). The compose path already played them at their defaults, so nothing moved. In: ai/scanner/pose.rs and `Scene::scanner_poses`, read by `actor_pose_values` and mixed into `pose_signature`, as `tongues` are. Rules from SDK npc_basescanner.cpp, once per think: the face turns toward what it watches by up to 50 degrees, and back by 10 with nothing to watch or with that behind it; the tail follows the climb; the dynamo turns up to 60 degrees by speed, flat out in a dive; alert_control is 1 alert or in combat, else 0. A photograph's flare goes back to idling when it has played (PrescheduleThink). The client smooths networked pose parameters, which `Pose::between` stands for: each tick blends the last two thinks. Deviation: the face looks from the eye height, the SDK's fallback, not the `eyes` attachment, which would compose the bones on every think. entities.rs passed 1000 lines with the new field, so its yaw helpers moved unchanged to entities/turning.rs. Its angle_diff was not merged with ai::host's: they differ at exactly 180 degrees, which would flip a scripted turn.
14o session 47 npc_cscanner spotlight. Evidence: Source SDK hl2/npc_scanner.cpp (SpotlightCreate, SpotlightTargetPos, SpotlightCurrentPos, SpotlightUpdate), client view_beams.cpp DrawBeamWithHalo, beamdraw.cpp, stdshaders sprite_dx9.cpp and sprite_ps2x.fxc. In: ai/scanner/spotlight.rs (aim, momentum, end, length, width, fade, halo), `PhysicsSight::first_solid` (visible now calls it), `AiRuntime::scanner_beams`, InputDisableSpotlight, the SpotlightDisabled and SpotlightLength keys (no map sets either), effects/sprite_modes.rs. It is lit in the spot and patrol modes, past m_fNextSpotlightTime: off 2 s after a hit, 1 s after a flash, 2 s for a photograph and 3 s after swinging behind the body; other modes put it out at once. A seen player is aimed at by the eyes, an enemy otherwise on the floor at its last known position, then what it inspects, then a sweep ahead of its flight. The direction has momentum at atan(50 / length), keeps 0.4 of its turning and is capped at 1; a trace of twice its reach finds where it lands, NPCs not stopping it; the end chases at 10 times the distance and jumps past 200 u/s. Rendering needs no new path: a strip of 12 pieces sharing edges, each edge facing the view, brightness falling to zero at the fade length. Owned glow_test02 is a painted cone, narrow at the top row and wide and dim below, mapped once along the beam. Facts found by testing: the material has no $additive, so it drew as an opaque black wedge until it joined the list of entity render modes (beams are additive unless FBEAM_SOLID). Sprites default to NOSRGB, so they multiply and add in gamma space; our shader converts the product, which leaves a dim color about a third as bright, so the spotlight's two sprites take shader mode w = -1 (texture to linear, then the color). Other dim sprites keep the old order: changing them all needs a native comparison. DrawBeamWithHalo: within four widths of the beam's line the beam dims to nothing at one width, and the halo, at the light, grows from 32 to 64 and brightens as the view comes round in front. Aimed at the player the beam therefore gives way to the halo. Deviations: the halo's visibility is one line from the view, not an occlusion query; the light's place comes from a think, as a body-local offset, so no bones are composed each frame; FBEAM_SHADEOUT's fade is stepped over 12 pieces; the spotlight_end entity draws nothing (m_flLightScale 0). A lit scanner joins the host's PVS list by its beam's bounds, and a beam out of the PVS or wholly behind the view is not drawn, as the client never gets one out of the PVS; there is no test against the sides of the view. Not in this step: SCHED_CSCANNER_SPOTLIGHT_INSPECT_* and the citizens' searches. Packaged A/B, d1_town_02, the same deterministic run with DisableSpotlight sent by ent_fire: head-on 61898 pixels rise, around the light; from 179 units off the line 97553 pixels rise, across the frame. Two earlier A/Bs proved nothing: `ent_create` passed no key, so both runs had the beam; then a changed first-step yaw moved the spawn point, the trace started in solid and the beam had no length.
14o session 47 npc_cscanner inspection search. Evidence: Source SDK hl2/npc_scanner.cpp BestInspectTarget, IsValidInspectTarget, GatherConditions. In: ai/scanner/inspect.rs (`nearest`, `search_from`), `Scanner::search_due` and `found`, OnlyInspectPlayers and NeverInspectPlayers (set on 7 and 2 maps). It runs in `steer`, not `gather`, since a line of sight needs physics; both run once a think. The list of people is built only on a tick when a scanner is due, and a candidate farther than the best so far is not traced. Deviations: SCHED_CSCANNER_SPOTLIGHT_INSPECT_CIT needs the citizen to answer (`pNPC->GetTarget() == this`), and citizens have no side of this yet, so an inspection by spotlight stays the spotlight hover, as the SDK's own choice gives without an answer. There is no next-inspect time, so a citizen can be chosen again at once; squads do not share targets; FL_NOTARGET is read as the host's noclip flag; the hint search is not in. Mines (EquipMine, DeployMine) wait for npc_rollermine.
14o session 47 plan, npc_manhack. Evidence: Source SDK hl2/npc_manhack.cpp, npc_manhack.h. 63 placed on 11 maps, 20 of them on d1_canals_03, and 2 npc_makers on d1_canals_08. Inputs from maps: Kill 9, SetRelationship 5, Unpack 4, DisableSwarm 1, Wake 1. A flying class like the scanner, so ai/hover.rs and npc/air.rs carry over. Bounded steps: (1) the class: chases at eye height, hovers, bursts, cuts on contact and bounces, bumps off what it cannot cut, gibs on death; (2) SF_MANHACK_PACKED_UP, Unpack and SCHED_MANHACK_DEPLOY, which cops throw; (3) squads and swarming (SCHED_MANHACK_SWARM, DisableSwarm); (4) the gravity gun: held, thrown, smashed; (5) eye sprites, panels, the crash instead of gibs, water. Numbers for step 1: top speed 500, climb 200, thrust 300 ahead of it and 200 turning, 300 up, decay 0.3; a burst lasts 1 s, thrust 400, with 2 s before the next, started within 200 units of the enemy with its velocity within about 41 degrees of the line to it and its height inside the enemy's; damage 20 a second, doubled on the player, at least 1 a hit; a cut ends the burst and sends it back at 200. 14o session 47 npc_manhack step 1. In: ai/manhack.rs (flight, burst, cut arithmetic, engine pitch, schedules), ai/runtime/manhacks.rs, AiClass moved to runtime/class.rs, models/bodygroups.rs with the blade group in RUNTIME_BODYGROUPS, the six gibs of models/manhack.phy through `add_class_gibs`. `Air::step` now lets a body that starts inside something move, since physics would push it out in the SDK and our step froze it for good: a manhack spawned on a sloping street stood still at 267 u/s. Packaged, d1_town_02, a spawned manhack against the walking player: first burst at 2.61 s, first cut at 3.24 s, 11 cuts in 15 s with 11 Slice cues, last_damage 4 (20 x 0.1 x 2), nearest 27 units. A rig holds only the sequences `class_ai_activities` asks for, and the class was not listed, so `animate` rejected ACT_FLY on every map and the model stood in a wrong pose: that pose was a large purple slab against the camera, which first looked like a bad blade material. manhackblade001.vmt is a valid translucent VertexLitGeneric, and late precache, a first theory, was rejected on d1_canals_03. With ACT_FLY and ACT_MANHACK_UNPACK listed, no rejection is logged and the same tick shows the body and blade. A kill: Die once, six of six gibs. 14o session 48 env_cubemap for models, first version in (e77d79e). The plan's fear did not hold: lit models already have their own materials, so no per-draw binding was needed, and `present_lighting` only reaches a model that moved, so one that stands still pays nothing. The shader needed no change: SDK vertexlit_and_unlit_generic_ps2x has envmap_specular's shape, and a model's fresnel default of 1 removes that term. 14o session 48 plan, env_cubemap for models (the owner's scanner body is black in the dark without it). Evidence so far: owned scanner_sheet.vmt; `load_envmap` returns early for anything but LightmappedGeneric and for the name env_cubemap; SDK bspfile.h LUMP_CUBEMAPS (42) and dcubemapsample_t; IMaterialSystem::BindLocalCubemap. Retail engine 1013d600 is Mod_LoadCubemapSamples: 16-byte samples (origin as three ints, size) become 20-byte records in the world model at +0xd8, count at +0xdc, each loading maps/<map>/c<x>_<y>_<z> with an HDR suffix, then without it, then maps/<map>/cubemapdefault. How the engine picks a cubemap for a model is not in the SDK, and no reader of that table was found by its direct form: read it from the retail engine before coding, per the owner's rule. Bounded steps: (1) retail: how a model's cubemap is chosen and when it is chosen again for one that moves; (2) source-assets reads the samples and their packed cubemaps; (3) VertexLitGeneric keeps $envmap with its mask, tint, contrast and saturation; (4) the host binds a cube per model. Material bind groups are shared, so this needs one material per (material, cubemap) or a per-draw binding: measure both, since rule 11 applies and this touches every model. Validation: the d1_canals_02 tunnel A/B, body pixels against native; frame time before and after; regression and replays, reviewing every changed capture.
14o session 49 env_cubemap: how the engine picks a model's sample, read from the retail engine. The way in was the r_showenvcubemap ConVar: its four readers are the model render path, and the shared lighting setup there binds the local cubemap (render context slot 0x14 with IRefCounted's two slots) with what the light cache returns. Retail: a model's lighting origin is its illumposition, through the illumination attachment when the model names one, else through the model's matrix; static props go through the same field of their own cache entry. The cache's entries are keyed by leaf and by cells of 32 by 32 by 128 units. A new entry takes its point from r_lightcachecenter, default 1: the middle of the cell, if a world-only MASK_OPAQUE trace from the origin reaches it, then the middle at the origin's own height, else the origin. The sample is the nearest to that point by squared distance, ties to the earlier sample, and it is stored in the entry, so a model changes sample only by entering another entry. So session 48's "nearest" was right, measured from the wrong point. In: `cache_cell`, `LightingData::cache_point`, a re-pick only when a model changes cell. Not in: the illumination attachment; the case of a middle in another open leaf, where the engine runs one more step this code does not; an entry's point depends on the first model to fill it when the middle is out of reach, here on each model. Mips: `decode_cube_picmip` read one level and the upload set no mip count, so every reflection, world surfaces too, sampled a 32 by 32 level with no minification, where the owned samples carry 6 levels. `Cube::mips` now carries the texture's own chain, face by face. This was tried as the cause of the speckle and the cool tint and is neither, on the numbers: packaged d1_canals_02, the same frames as session 48, high-frequency detail over mean 0.95 and 0.85 before, 0.94 and 0.89 after, native 0.76 and 0.85; red over blue 1.15 and 1.37 before, 1.15 and 1.31 after, native 1.40 and 1.52. The chain stays because it is what the file holds. Tint, still open: the owned samples differ along that tunnel, red over blue 0.81 for the one nearest our scanner and 1.13 for the next group, top level, clamped at 16. So where the scanner hovers can move the tint, and the native run logged no position (ent_dump printed no origin in 2332 samples), so which sample native used there is **not known**. Next test: the same run with getpos of the scanner.
14o session 49 plan, npc_manhack step 3, squads and swarming. Evidence: Source SDK hl2/npc_manhack.cpp TranslateSchedule, StartTask, RunTask, GatherEnemyConditions; retail server strings list the same schedules and tasks (SCHED_MANHACK_REGROUP, SWARM_IDLE, SWARM, SWARM_FAILURE; TASK_MANHACK_FIND_SQUAD_CENTER, FIND_SQUAD_MEMBER, MOVEAT_SAVEPOSITION; COND_MANHACK_START_ATTACK), with their text read against the SDK before coding. Rules: between bursts, or with no attack slot of two free, a chasing manhack swarms; DisableSwarm turns that off. The swarm's point is the mean of the squad, attackers counting ten times, pulled toward the enemy's last known place with a weight of four. It moves at that point for the task's time, give or take a fifth; a world hit on the way fails the task, and being over 256 units off when time runs out fails it too; under 64 units it circles. SWARM_FAILURE waits 2 to 4 s and goes to a member who is attacking or has a goal. Squad slots reuse `Squad::occupy_range`. Bounded steps: the arithmetic and schedule choice with unit tests in ai/manhack.rs; the runtime's squad center and swarm target in ai/runtime/manhacks.rs; InputDisableSwarm through `entities::inputs::HANDLERS`. Validation: unit tests; packaged on d1_canals_03, whose 20 placed manhacks have never been seen in light, reading squad slots and schedules from the report; then a native comparison of how many attack at once.
14o session 49 env_cubemap for late-loaded models (58f4e38). `ModelCache::add_materials_filtered` uploads the envmap mask with the base and iris (one helper, repeating like the base, since it uses the base UVs); `draw_material` binds it; `ModelCache::model_lit` builds the light state and `reflects` decides: `model_envmap`, not plain materials, and a named mask already on the GPU. Spawned NPCs are not covered by this: they use pool entities built with the map. Static props remain: they are baked into world batches per material, so a sample per prop needs either the prop's light origin kept with its index range, splitting only reflecting materials' batches by sample, or a per-draw binding; measure the draw count first (rule 11).

14s performance step 1 plan for session 53 (owner's request 2026-11-10; evidence docs/performance.md "Owner's manhack plaza on Windows"). Two independent, reversible changes, each measured on artifacts/s52/make_walk.py's plaza scene before and after. (1) Hitboxes on demand, as Source tests hitboxes only when a trace reaches an NPC: `pose_npc_hitboxes` stops posing every NPC every tick; NPC hitboxes are marked stale each tick and posed (with a query refresh) only for NPCs whose bounds a hitbox-capable query (bullet, melee, tracer, impact and decal rays) touches, before that query runs. First list every query that reads hitboxes (`hitgroup`, `hitbox_bone`, studio hitbox colliders). Expected about 2 ms a tick off the crowd scene. (2) Our query pipeline out of `PhysicsPipeline::step`: pass None and update it ourselves after the step with the colliders of awake bodies plus the changed, inserted and removed colliders we track (every insert/remove site must report), refit every tick and rebalance only every N ticks or after large changes. Expected about 1 ms a tick. The Rapier inserts and removals are all inside `Physics` (about ten collider inserts in physics.rs and its body-removal path); route them through two helpers that queue the tree's changes. Not in this step: the broad phase (1 ms; a test with the hitboxes in their own collider set and query tree comes after), the AI's per-manhack cost, the render CPU. Validation: unit tests for staleness and for trace results equal before and after on fixed poses; all replays; the plaza profile.

Native from straight above on a fixed 15 ms clock (artifacts/s53/native_top.py): at full speed only the BladeBlur cards show, a faint translucent three-lobed swirl; below blade speed 40 the solid blade (SpinBlades, spawn speed 20). Ours drew the solid blade at every speed: RUNTIME_BODYGROUPS listed only group 1, so the blur bodies were never built (`body_swap_failures`: "not preloaded"), and once built their material (Blur01) was missing because map material names covered only each instance's starting body. Not in: SpinBlades' m_flPlaybackRate (blade speed / 100) on the flyer's cycle; the lighting in (e). (b) Static prop hits, result. Owner: a bench's back and the plaza trees take no sound, effect or decal. Cause: an impact found its surfaceprop only through the drawn triangle under the hit (zero gap on world surfaces), and a static prop's VPhysics hull sits off curved drawn faces. Now the loader keeps each static prop's .phy surfaceprop (`World::model_surfaceprops`, source-assets models/collision.rs; owned: bench_indoor001a wood_crate, tree_deciduous_01a wood_solid), static colliders carry it (`collider_materials`), `Physics::static_surfaceprop` names it for a hit, and the decal takes the drawn face within STATIC_PROP_GAP (4 units, our bound). Packaged: the bench from the east 7 decals and 0 unclippable (3 and 4 before), from the west 5 and 2; the tree plays Wood_Solid.BulletImpact on every hit but places 1 decal of 7 (its trunk lies further inside the hull; next: project along the shot onto the drawn mesh, as studio decals do). Static colliders' surfaceprops now also reach physics contact sounds (intended; the replays decide). (c) `--borderless --window-at` opened on the primary monitor (startup_mode's MonitorSelection::Primary, GetWindowRect 0,0,1920,1080); a placed borderless window now starts windowed there and goes borderless on its own monitor (-1920,0,0,1080). (d) point_spotlight plan. Owner: lamps cast no light; native shows the spawn lamp's volumetric cone at d1_trainstation_02 (artifacts/s53/lamp-compare.png); no code names point_spotlight (11 in that map, e.g. width 64, length 256, rendercolor 254 216 146, HDRColorScale 0.7, angles 90 0 0, spawnflags 3). SDK point_spotlight.cpp: an unparented spotlight is made once at Activate (CreateEfficientSpotlight): a trace along its angles (MASK_SOLID_BRUSHONLY, twice SpotlightLength, none with IgnoreSolid), a CBeam glow_test02 from its origin to the hit with width SpotlightWidth, end width width x reached / length (MAX_BEAM_WIDTH), fade length min(reached, length), FBEAM_SHADEOUT|NOTILE, brightness 64, rendercolor, HDRColorScale, halo light_glow03 at scale 60; LightOn/LightOff; spawnflag 1 starts on, 2 no dynamic light. Steps, each tested: (1) the Beam fields (scanner beams unchanged); (2) point_spotlight beams and inputs; (3) a packaged A/B with native at the spawn lamp. Out: parented (moving) spotlights, spotlight_end's dynamic light. (e) Found, not fixed: props are lit about twice native's brightness and warmer at the plaza (an identical wood pallet spawned in both, artifacts/s53/pallet-compare.png; the bench likewise; world lightmaps match). This map's static props have no baked .vhv lighting, so static and dynamic props both go through `LightState::state_at`: the next lighting step compares its ambient cube and local lights with native at that spot. The dprp game lump's detail props (the plaza's leaf litter) are not drawn.

14r session 52, two owner playtest findings (2026-11-10). (a) Manhacks against walls and props. Owner: native manhacks are seen hitting walls and stray props, bouncing off and leaving sparks, also after the gravity gun pushes them away; ours showed no recoil and no sparks. Packaged evidence before the fix: one NPC_Manhack.Grind in session 49's 15 s swarm run, seven in session 51's attack run. Cause: one velocity served as the AI's wish and the body's motion, and the per-tick flight step slid it along a surface before the think's CheckCollisions saw the surface, so Bump rarely ran and had no effects. Source SDK: CAI_BasePhysicsFlyingBot keeps m_vCurrentVelocity apart from the VPhysics body, and Simulate's motor pulls the body toward it every tick (an acceleration of the difference over the tick, SIM_GLOBAL_ACCELERATION), which CNPC_Manhack::ClampMotorForces holds to 3000 u/s² per axis times the blade speed over 100 (upward at least 1200; with the engine stalled nothing across); CheckCollisions traces a think of the body's VPhysics velocity; Bump deflects m_vCurrentVelocity off the surface by half its speed (never down within 36 units of the floor), slows the blades to 20 and, on a surface across the blades' plane, dispatches ManhackSparks (client fx_sparks.cpp FX_SparkFan: 35 sparks, METAL_SCRAPE spread 0.3, 128 to 512 u/s, gravity 800, collision damping 0.4, 2 s), te->DynamicLight (255 180 100, radius 50, 0.3 s, decay 150), NPC_Manhack.Grind and the "ManhackCut" decal on a surface neither floor nor ceiling (a 24 unit trace back along the normal); inside something, a random 50 u/s and no bump for 0.5 s; HitPhysicsObject pushes a VPhysics prop across the line to it with 30 kg at 100 in/s from the box's edge. Owned files: manhack.phy's surfaceprop is "metal" (elasticity 0.25, scripts/surfaceproperties.txt, concrete 0.2); scripts/decals_subrect.txt "ManhackCut" lists three materials. Retail: vphysics.dll's material parser reads "elasticity" (FUN_10014c10); how IVP combines two elasticities was not traced. Implementation: ai/manhack/body.rs holds the arithmetic (motor, rebound, deflect, blade push, the light and decal tables); `Manhack::body` is the body's velocity, `hover` stays the wish. Bullets and the club split as the SDK does (VPhysicsTakeDamage's force on the body, m_vForceVelocity on the wish); the gravity gun's events and a cut's rebound set both. ai/runtime/manhacks/contact.rs carries the body every tick (motor, sweep, a contact keeps 0.05 of the speed into the surface, the product rule our rigid bodies use; a prop takes the momentum lost) and shows each think's bumps. Inferred, not in the SDK: a body pressed on a surface it met since the last think, with the wish still into it, counts as a CheckCollisions hit, since VPhysics keeps such a body in contact. `AiRuntime::manhack_marks` hands the lights, decals and prop pushes to the host. Not in: IVP's own bounce, Bump's ApplyTorqueCenter, the slice's physical contact with its victim, the stale wish after a throw (the SDK keeps it; here the throw sets both). (b) A scanner punted or launched into a wall broke only at a second contact. Session 51's packaged scanner launch shows it: at 1500 u/s into the ground the first contact cost nothing and it slid on at about 1320 u/s, dying at the next contact 0.2 s later. Cause: an NPC's health is set only on its first damage (`hits::ensure_health`), and the scanner learned its max health from that value, so the first smash cost 0 x max health. `gameplay::npc_health` and `npc_max_health` now give the spawn health before any damage; the scanner, the zombies and the hit code share them. (c) CNPC_Manhack::OnStateChange: the first state change replaces MANHACK_NOISEMOD_HIDE's noise (5000 on every axis) with SetNoiseMod(0, 0, 1.7 to 2.3) unless it is packed or waits for a script; without it the 5000 rate sampled once a think shoved idle manhacks at random (`Manhack::state_changed`). Validation plan: unit tests; packaged: session 51's scanner launch (death at the first contact), session 49's swarm run and session 51's attack run (bumps, grinds and sparks counted), a punt into a wall; replays before merging.

14q session 51, four owner playtest findings (2026-11-10). (a) The gravity gun takes scanners (Source SDK hl2/npc_basescanner.cpp OnPhysGunPickup, OnPhysGunDrop, HasPhysicsAttacker, VPhysicsCollision, TakeDamageFromPhyscannon, MoveToTarget, SCHED_SCANNER_HELD_BY_PHYSCANNON; retail's server names the same schedule and its two conditions; owned combine_scanner.phy: the body is 8.70 kg, about 30 by 29 by 28 units). `Scene::physgun_npcs` now lists `PhysgunNpc` (id, mass, half extents, a punt's damage) and the flight stage hands one tick's `Scene::npc_physgun` to scanners and manhacks alike. ai/scanner/physgun.rs: held it follows the grab and runs the held schedule (grabbed and released as conditions); a punt or a launch stalls its steering 2 s with the dive scream; within 0.75 s of a throw what its flight meets costs max health times speed over 250, at most twice, DMG_GENERIC from the player; a plain drop keeps 285 u/s. Not in: the motor fighting the hold, damage from props the player throws at it, a combine ball, other NPCs' view of a held scanner. (b) The manhack's body (Source SDK ai_basenpc_physicsflyer.cpp Simulate; hl2/npc_manhack.cpp MoveToTarget's banking, MoveExecute_Alive, ClampMotorForces, SpinBlades, Bump; owned manhack.phy: rotdamping 1.5, inertia 5). The SDK never sets its angles: a motion controller accelerates a VPhysics body. ai/manhack/attitude.rs runs that motor on angles the AI keeps: banking 40 degrees times the thrust's share along its forward and right, eased by half a think; pitch and roll take the spin that closes their error in a second; yaw is pushed by its error past 0.1 s of spin, an underdamped spring; all scaled by the blades' speed over 100, which a hit or a bump sets to 20 (a club 10) and SpinBlades raises by 80 a second. A knock (wall bump, bullet, club, punt) adds the spin its impulse gives at a random lever of up to 5 units to a box of the first solid's size; a launch adds LaunchObject's random 600 degrees a second. SpinBlades' bodygroups come with the blade speed: the blade under 40, the blur from 20. Not in: real contact points and shapes, Bump's ApplyTorqueCenter (its units were not resolved), the playback rate by blade speed, the dead manhack's banking noise. (c) CNPC_Manhack::NPC_TranslateActivity: with the blades active ACT_IDLE, ACT_WALK and ACT_RUN are ACT_FLY. The swarm schedules' TASK_STOP_MOVING set ACT_IDLE, the packed pose, after every attack. The motor's class flags are one `ActivityRules` value from `AiClass::activity_rules`. (d) The gravity gun's center and blast sprites ramp as CSprite does (Source SDK hl2/weapon_physcannon.cpp DoEffectClosed, DoEffectReady, DoEffectHolding; shared Sprite.cpp ClientThink, GetRenderScale): weapon_physcannon/glow.rs keeps a scale and a brightness ramp for each, asked every frame; `SpriteRamp::set` takes ClientThink's rule (a new value starts from the last one asked for, the same value restarts nothing), which the crossbow's charger shares. Holding from closed grows the core from 0 to 0.2 over 0.2 s and lights it in 0.1 s; opening shrinks the blast in from scale 1 to 0.1 over 0.2 s. The glow sprites and end caps still flicker at once, as DoEffectIdle sets them without a time. Validation: unit tests for each; packaged on d1_town_02: a scanner taken, held and launched, and session 49's manhack run for the activity, the angles and the blades.
