## Paused: private decompilation coverage (passes01-14 record)

Coverage passes ran 2026-10-05 and are paused by the owner's 2026-10-06 decision. The record below is retained as history and future reference. Preserve the original 22-module project, exports and exact-byte catalogue.

Bound this pass to: (a) a new path/hash-keyed inventory with PE imports, exports, duplicate groups and explicit scope decisions; (b) read-only inspection of all ten failed exports, with separately recorded baseline and evidence-based recovery attempts on disposable program state; (c) discovery accounting for the existing corpus, including executable bytes outside function bodies, entry/export targets and unresolved call/data-pointer candidates. Keep generated scripts, logs and bodies under private work/hl2-decompiled. Never silently mutate the baseline database or count plausible pointer targets as confirmed functions.

Validation: reconcile all installed paths and baseline binary hashes; audit saved report identities/counts and original corpus hashes; record raw instructions, calling conventions, input storage and recovery outcomes for each failure. Audit gaps as uncertain code/data/padding rather than claiming a complete denominator. Check public documentation, source whitelist and publication audit. Runtime code is unchanged, so gameplay rebuilds are not required for this pass.

Completed coverage-pass-20261005-01:126 installed paths/hashes and 632 import edges; all 22 baseline hashes match. Three loader-referenced modules add 1,091 identified addresses/1,090 original bodies. Eleven original failures have separate relaxed-input recovery bodies with verified transaction rollback, giving 88,884 identified addresses with some pseudocode across 25 selected modules. The baseline catalogue is unchanged; a path/hash/address supplement audits 301 artifacts and preserves all rejected attempts. Its gamedb navigator misses 263 bodies and has 36 range mismatches. Full discovery/ABI/semantics remain unverified.

Next bounded pass: review a small set of unlabelled RTTI/vtable pointer groups and executable orphan ranges using a disposable project. Require slot/reference and instruction-flow evidence before creating a candidate function, compare inferred boundaries with raw flow, and keep rejected/shared-tail cases. Extend a new database version rather than rewriting this pass. Reconcile remaining optional renderer/video/audio loader branches. The current 22-module audit's1,459,199 gap bytes and 13,830 pointer-slot observations are uncertain evidence, not a count of missing functions. Review the recovered x87 callees/types before treating those bodies as behavior specifications.

Active continuation after the owner's correction: checkpoints are review/validation boundaries, not permission to stop the authorized work. In coverage-pass-20261005-02, verify raw RTTI complete-object-locator/type/class records and contiguous vtable slots against the hashed private binaries. Clone the baseline analysis project privately before creating functions. Promote only defined instruction entries backed by verified table slots and bounded flow; preserve rejected interior/shared-tail/data cases. Export newly identified functions to a scoped database and audit before/after coverage. Then continue the remaining loader/module and discovery batches, retaining the baseline corpus and all earlier checkpoints. ABI/type/semantic uncertainty remains explicit; do not mark Rust or native parity from export success.

Coverage-pass-20261005-03 continues while the discovery exports are audited: retrieve optional loader references through the canonical databases, preserve the exact caller bodies and binary identities, and copy/hash the 17 remaining installed optional service/UI/input/renderer/audio-video modules into a separate private corpus. Export them conservatively even where runtime relevance is conditional or unresolved. Audit each new module's discovery gaps and export failures, then add a new path/hash/address catalogue. Tool libraries, middleware and other game variants keep separate scope records; including an optional module does not establish its runtime activation or complete campaign coverage.

Discovery follow-through: raw prototype flow replaces analysis-adjusted flow for rejected jumps in pass04, with read-only transaction rollback and independent entry checks. Pass05 handles the 15 relocated targets without defined instructions using bounded disassembly on temporary program state; verify restoration of both function and instruction absence. Pass06 audits pointer groups in the 17 optional modules and discovery accounting in the three earlier extensions, using separate outputs/databases and rollback proofs. Preserve ambiguous entry/shared-tail cases and all original export failures.

Pass07 widens discovery evidence beyond contiguous read-only tables: audit data references into executable addresses without a containing function, including relocated writable callback slots and relocated instruction immediates. Require exact byte/relocation agreement at each reference source; distinguish code operands, writable data and read-only data, preserve caller identity and do not promote from numeric resemblance alone. Combine all 42 saved program snapshots with separate transient-candidate identities, then inspect bounded entry flow on temporary state and audit retrieval/rollback before any semantics are used by Rust.

Pass08 follows the 2,462 referenced targets with no defined instructions. On read-only temporary state, disassemble within a bounded executable range; clear only small undefined data beginning exactly at the target, and reject typed/overlapping data. Reuse raw-flow and independent-boundary checks, preserve large/shared/indirect cases, and verify restoration of instruction/function absence in a separate postscript after the enclosing transaction ends. Prior accepted addresses remain separate; a new exact-byte ledger must retain rejected attempts before any new count is published.

Pass09 consolidates retrieval across the audited private catalogues without rewriting them. Materialize an address-keyed index with installed path, binary hash, source-database hash and export variant; retain exact body bytes, original errors, candidate attempts and recorded references. Reconcile unique addresses and variant counts against every source, reject ambiguous default retrieval, and audit all source hashes and copied rows. Keep reviewed names/types, Rust implementation and native behavior verification separate. Supplemental gamedb navigation must use a completed C-only input copy; retain rejected mixed-file and incomplete-copy indexing attempts. This index improves research access and does not establish a complete function denominator.

Pass10 reviews the 16 original overlapping-input failures as an ABI investigation. Retrieve their original/recovered variants through the consolidated database, compare raw instruction/control-flow patterns across modules, and inspect incoming calls, neighboring entry code, x87 register use and recorded signatures on read-only project state. Preserve duplicate-pattern evidence separately from original names/types. Do not adopt the relaxed-input `void` signatures or a guessed CRT identity as a verified ABI. Record unresolved call/storage and shared-entry cases for a later bounded correction rather than changing Rust from approximate pseudocode.

Pass11 reviews the 2,333 typed/overlapping-data candidates excluded from pass08. Inspect existing data-unit boundaries, raw bytes, exact PE relocations, incoming operand references and any pointer destinations without clearing data or defining instructions/functions. Separate embedded pointer/byte data from unresolved entry hypotheses and identify further address-keyed discovery evidence only where a relocated pointer reaches executable storage. Retain classifications as static evidence; executable section membership alone does not make data a function.

Pass12 reconciles switch dispatch with function-body coverage. The typed-data audit found 44 unowned executable destinations behind indirect jumps, while one control function's existing C already contains a switch using such blocks. On read-only state, inspect decompiler jump-table and p-code address records for dispatchers with a known containing function. Compare case ownership and instruction addresses with saved function bodies and preserve a separate export variant. Do not create functions or extend bodies solely because table destinations are outside the listing's ownership ranges; decompiler recovery can already cover those instructions. Leave dispatchers without a known owner unresolved and record the scope of this control sample.

Pass13 bounds the remaining owner hypotheses: inspect at most two preceding saved function entries within16KiB of each orphan dispatcher. Decompile each distinct candidate once on read-only state and require both the exact dispatcher address and the relocated seed destination in a recovered jump-table record before recording an observed relationship. Preserve unmatched/ambiguous controls and every export failure; a nearby entry alone is not ownership proof. Do not extend listing bodies or create functions. Consolidate audited evidence through a new private index after this pass, leaving all earlier databases unchanged.

## Background loading and dialog lifetime (2026-10-27)

Reproduce the return-confirmation silhouette and requests made while a background
worker is running. Remove orphaned closing dialog payloads when the window stack
is replaced; compose confirmations only at their actual stack position. Keep
chapter controls available during background work. Pending-request flags do not
describe the active worker: campaign owns that distinction. Preserve foreground
loads, queue the newest background request, and discard superseded background
results before installing their world or changing the newer loading presentation.
Foreground loading cancels the background transition presentation. Closing a
browser does not cancel its background worker.

## ActBusy leave for player companions (2026-11-01, bounded plan)

`hl2/background01` and the `d1_trainstation_02` plaza send spawned citizens to `ai_goal_actbusy`'s `ForceThisNPCToLeave`. Plan: host `ai::actbusy` on player companions as well as metrocops, per the SDK's `CNPC_PlayerCompanion::CreateBehaviors`; add `ForceThisNPCToActBusy`, `ForceThisNPCToLeave`, `SCHED_ACTBUSY_LEAVE` and exit hints (`HINT_NPC_EXIT_POINT`, never locked); remove an NPC within 64 units of its exit, so its maker sees it through DeathNotice and spawns the next. Host code lives in `ai/runtime/actbusy_host.rs`. Combine soldiers do not host the behavior yet; that changes the prison maps and needs its own comparison.

Validation: unit tests, a packaged background run reporting citizens that spawn, walk and are removed, and the gameplay replays. Reversible by dropping the commit.

Known cost: removed entities keep their slot (`Scene::spawn_entity` only appends), so an endless maker grows the entity list by one per spawn. Reusing dead copies is a separate step if the measured growth matters.
